{
  "abstract_verbatim": "We prove that every sufficiently small, square-symmetric finite-range perturbation of the planar Ising contour energy has a domain-independent inverse temperature at which its spin interface converges to chordal SLE<sub>3</sub>. The result allows interactions of either sign, arbitrary admissible exterior contours, and uniformly approximated Jordan domains. <a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that every sufficiently small, square-symmetric finite-range perturbation of the planar Ising contour energy has a domain-independent inverse temperature at which its spin interface converges to chordal SLE<sub>3</sub>. The result allows interactions of either sign, arbitrary admissible exterior contours, and uniformly approximated Jordan domains. Convergence holds for the full oriented curve law in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization.

- **claim-interface-universality** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $J>0$, and let $U$ satisfy the finite-range and symmetry assumptions above. There is $\lambda_0=\lambda_0(J,U)>0$ such that for every $\left\|\lambda\right\|<\lambda_0$ there is a positive inverse temperature $\beta_c=\beta_c(J,U,\lambda)$, independent of domains and boundary data, with $$\beta_c(J,U,0)=\beta_0:=\frac{\log(1+\sqrt2)}{2J},$$ for which the following holds.* *Let $D$ be any bounded Jordan domain, let $a,b$ be distinct boundary points, and let $\delta_n\downarrow0$. For each $n$, choose a domain $\Omega_n$, marks and stubs $\xi_n$, and an admissible exterior contour $P_{o,n}$ as above. Suppose there are boundary homeomorphisms $$\phi_n:\partial D\longrightarrow \partial(\delta_n D_{\Omega_n})$$ such that $$\sup_{z\in\partial D}\left\|\phi_n(z)-z\right\|\longrightarrow0,\qquad \phi_n(a)=\delta_n f_{{\rm in},n},\qquad \phi_n(b)=\delta_n f_{{\rm out},n}.$$ If $P_n$ has law $\mu_{\Omega_n,\beta_c,\lambda}^{\xi_n,P_{o,n}}$, then $\delta_n\gamma(P_n)$ converges in distribution, in the metric <a href="#eq:curve-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curve-metric">[eq:curve-metric]</a>, to chordal $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ in $D$ from $a$ to $b$.
  - Explanation: A sufficiently small finite-range square-symmetric contour perturbation admits a bulk temperature at which the prescribed oriented interface converges to chordal SLE. The temperature and perturbation interval are independent of the domain and admissible exterior contours.
- **claim-exact-contour-spin-correspondence** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-contour-spin)): For the boundary data above, every even completion $P\cup P_o$ determines a unique spin configuration that is plus sufficiently far away and has disagreement set $P\cup P_o$. Its restriction $\tau$ to $\mathbb Z^2\setminus\Omega$ is independent of $P$. The map $P\mapsto\sigma\|_\Omega$ is a bijection between $\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi$ and all spin assignments in $\Omega$. Under this bijection, <a href="#eq:contour-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:contour-law">[eq:contour-law]</a> is precisely the Gibbs conditional law for <a href="#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> with exterior spins $\tau$. Denote this law by $\nu_{\Omega,\beta,\lambda}^{\tau}$.* *The exterior nearest neighbors along each of the two boundary arcs between the marks have a constant sign, and the signs of the two arcs are opposite. The interaction in <a href="#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> is finite-range, invariant under global spin flip and the square symmetries, and has finitely many local interaction types up to translation.
  - Explanation: Even contour completions determine spins uniquely after fixing plus at infinity. The resulting bijection preserves the complete conditional Gibbs law, including interactions with exterior contours.
- **claim-buffered-pointwise-comparisons** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-buffered)): Consider a finite critical nearest-neighbor ferromagnetic Ising graph, disjoint vertex sets $I,J,F$, and common pins on $F$. Let $\mu_b$ be the marginal law on $I$ obtained by prescribing an arbitrary configuration $b$ on $J$. Whenever the following sets lie in the indicated square-lattice charts, the bounds hold for all configurations $a$ on $I$ and all $b,b'$.* 1. *For every $c>0$ there is $K_c<\infty$ such that $I\subset B_R(z)$, $J\subset B_{(1+c)R}(z)^c$ imply $$K_c^{-1}\le \frac{\mu_b(a)}{\mu_{b'}(a)}\le K_c.$$* 2. *There are absolute $K<\infty$, $\zeta>0$ such that $I\subset B_w(z)$, $J\subset B_R(z)^c$, and $R\ge8w\ge8$ imply $$\left\|\log\frac{\mu_b(a)}{\mu_{b'}(a)}\right\| \le K(w/R)^\zeta.$$ No free neighborhood of $I$ is required in these two assertions: additional common pins may lie anywhere outside $I\cup J$.* 3. *Either law can be replaced by any mixture of the indicated conditional laws. The fixed-ratio comparison also holds for laws from different domains, including the plane and a torus, if there is a common isometric lattice collar separating the observed spins from every difference in the data. In this cross-domain assertion the collar is unpinned in both laws. Applying a common randomization kernel to the observed spins preserves each density bound.* *All constants are independent of the number, positions, and values of the common pins and of the mesh. In the first two assertions the same ratio bounds hold when the roles of the two terminal sets are reversed.
  - Explanation: Separated terminal sets have uniformly comparable conditional spin densities. The assertions distinguish comparisons with arbitrary common pins from cross-domain comparisons requiring a common unpinned collar.
- **claim-annular-density-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-pure-annular)): Let $2\le r<R/4$, $A=B_R\setminus B_r$, and $W=B_{2R}\setminus B_{r/2}$, with integer rounding of the boundaries. Suppose $W$ is an unpinned square-lattice chart. The law on $A$, conditional on arbitrary spins outside $W$, has pointwise density relative to $\mu_0\|_A$ between $K^{-1}$ and $K$, for an absolute $K$ independent of $R/r$. The assertion applies in a torus chart and to mixtures of exterior conditions. Fixed changes of the two collar ratios only change $K$.
  - Explanation: Separate inner and outer collars control the entire annular density with a constant independent of the annular aspect ratio.
- **claim-signed-source-transmission** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-signed-transmission)): There is a fixed exponent $\eta\in(0,1/8)$ with the following property. Let $\mu$ be a pure critical Gibbs law with common pins, let $q=q(\sigma_S)$ be order-positive and centered for its source marginal, and let $J$ be an admissible cut at scales $w,s$. For every bounded target observable $h=h(\sigma_T)$, when $w$ is sufficiently large, $$\left\|\mathbb E_\mu[qh]\right\| \le C\left\\|h\right\\|_\infty (w/s)^{-7/8+\eta} \mathbb E_\mu[qM_J], \qquad M_J=s^{-7/8}\sum_{x\in J}\sigma_x. \label{eq:pure-signed}$$ The constant depends only on the admissibility constants and the fixed chart geometry. It is independent of the mass of $q_+$, the mesh, the number and values of source pins, and the ratio $w/s$. The same inequality holds for expectation differences of two ordered source configurations, and after averaging an ordered coupling of random source configurations, with the common Gibbs continuation. All changed pins and fields lie on the source side of the cut; the target-side continuation is identical in the compared laws. One may decrease $\eta$, keeping it positive.
  - Explanation: A centered source that pairs nonnegatively with increasing tests transmits to distant bounded observables through an admissible free cut. The estimate also applies to ordered random sources with identical target-side Gibbs continuation.
- **claim-nearby-line-magnetization-continuity** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-line-continuity)): Let $J_s,J'_s$ be horizontal or vertical lattice segments of length $O(s)$, with corresponding portions displaced by at most $Cw$ and unmatched end portions of total length $O(w)$. The assertion also applies to concentric square contours of radii comparable to $s$, differing in radius by at most $Cw$, and to a fixed finite union of these pairs. Assume their union has a free neighborhood of width $cs$, covered by a fixed number of buffered lattice charts. For each fixed $h=w/s\in(0,h_0]$, $$\limsup_{s\to\infty} \left\\|s^{-7/8}\sum_{J_s}\sigma_x -s^{-7/8}\sum_{J'_s}\sigma_x\right\\|_{L^2(\mu)} \le C_0h^{3/8}. \label{eq:pure-lines}$$ The bound is uniform over arbitrary pins beyond the stated free neighborhood and mixtures of their laws. The constant depends only on the fixed geometry and buffer.
  - Explanation: Nearby line or square-contour magnetizations approach one another in the stated norm after the mesh limit, uniformly over pins beyond their free neighborhood.
- **claim-nearby-contour-prediction** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-line-prediction)): In Proposition <a href="#prop:line-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:line-continuity">7</a>, let $\mathcal F$ be any spin information containing $\sigma_{J'_s}$, and let $P_{\mathcal F}$ denote conditional expectation. Then, for each fixed $w/s$, $$\limsup_{s\to\infty} \left\\|M_{J_s}-P_{\mathcal F}M_{J_s}\right\\|_2 \le C_0(w/s)^{3/8}.$$ Both line sums use the same normalization $s^{-7/8}$.
  - Explanation: Conditional expectation using information that contains a nearby contour predicts the original normalized line sum at least as well as the nearby sum does.
- **claim-spin-correlation-and-smearing-limits** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-spin)): There is $a_\sigma>0$, determined by the square-lattice convention, such that, for distinct physical points $z_1,\ldots,z_n$, $$a_\sigma^{-n}\delta^{-n/8} \mathbb E_0\prod_{i=1}^n\sigma_{x_i^\delta} \longrightarrow S(z_1,\ldots,z_n), \qquad \delta x_i^\delta\longrightarrow z_i.$$ For odd $n$, $S=0$; for even $n$, $S$ is the nonnegative square root of $$S(z_1,\ldots,z_n)^2 =2^{-n/2}\!\sum_{\substack{u_i\in\{-1,1\}\\\sum_i u_i=0}} \prod_{i<j}\|z_i-z_j\|^{u_iu_j/2}. \label{eq:pure-spin-formula}$$ Thus $S(z,w)=\|z-w\|^{-1/4}$. The convergence is uniform when the points lie in compact sets with fixed mutual separation. Also $$\begin{aligned} 0\le\mathbb E_0[\sigma_x\sigma_y]&\le C(1+\|x-y\|)^{-1/4}, \label{eq:pure-two-point}\\ 0\le\mathbb E^+_{B_r}[\sigma_0]&\le C(1+r)^{-1/8}. \label{eq:pure-plus-one-point} \end{aligned}$$ For $n\ge2$, repeated sites allowed, $$\left\|\mathbb E_0\prod_{i=1}^n\sigma_{x_i}\right\| \le C^n\prod_{i=1}^n \left(1+\min_{j\ne i}\|x_i-x_j\|\right)^{-1/8}. \label{eq:pure-multipoint-bound}$$ For every fixed finite collection of bounded compactly supported Riemann-integrable spatial test functions $\varphi$, the smeared variables $$\Phi_\delta(\varphi) =a_\sigma^{-1}\delta^{15/8} \sum_{x\in\mathbb Z^2}\varphi(\delta x)\sigma_x$$ have joint moment limits obtained by integrating $S$ against the test functions. Here Riemann integrability is equivalent to having a set of discontinuities of two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero. Spatial smearings are chosen in this class and fixed before taking the mesh limit; polygonal cell indicators are included. The limiting correlation functions are covariant under Möbius transformations, including inversion, with weight $1/8$ at each spin insertion.
  - Explanation: The pure spin correlations have explicit continuum limits and uniform domination sufficient for joint moments of fixed spatial smearings. The formula is a nonnegative square root of a charged sum.
- **claim-pure-reflection-positivity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-pure-reflection)): The full-plane law $\mu_0$ is reflection positive across a horizontal or vertical lattice reflection line. The limiting smeared spin correlation algebra is reflection positive across a straight line.
  - Explanation: Reflection positivity holds for the pure full-plane lattice law and passes to the limiting smeared spin algebra.
- **claim-positive-thermal-covariance** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-energy)): Let $e_1,e_2$ be the coordinate unit vectors and set $b_j(x)=\sigma_x\sigma_{x+e_j}-1/\sqrt2$. In the full-plane critical law, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}_0(b_j(0),b_k(x)) =\frac{1+o(1)}{\pi^2\|x\|^2},\qquad \|x\|\longrightarrow\infty, \quad j,k\in\{1,2\}. \label{eq:pure-energy}$$ For a fixed finite local average $B(x)=\sum_qc_qb_{j_q}(x+d_q)$, with $c_q\ge0$ and $c=\sum_qc_q>0$, $$\mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}_0(B(0),b_k(x))\sim\frac{c}{\pi^2\|x\|^2},\qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Cov}}_0(B(0),B(x))\sim\frac{c^2}{\pi^2\|x\|^2}.$$ In particular square symmetrization of the bond energy cannot cancel its leading covariance.
  - Explanation: The leading long-distance bond-energy covariance is positive for either bond orientation. Positive finite local averaging and square symmetrization preserve that leading thermal signal.
- **claim-pure-dobrushin-interface-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pure-sle)): In ordinary lattice approximations of a fixed polygonal Jordan domain with two distinct marked boundary points, the critical nearest-neighbor Ising interface with opposite constant signs on the two marked arcs converges to chordal $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$. Convergence is in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization, oriented from the first mark to the second. Either local turn at an alternating face is allowed, including the deterministic rule of the problem. Bounded local rounding and half-stubs have vanishing effect on this convergence.
  - Explanation: Pure critical interfaces in the specified polygonal approximations converge in the oriented uniform curve metric. Local turn conventions, bounded rounding, and half-stubs do not alter that limit.
- **claim-complete-loop-resolution-limit** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-pure-cle)): In a plus Jordan domain with lattice boundaries converging uniformly modulo parametrization, every complete noncrossing resolution of all spin-disagreement edges converges to the same oriented nested $\mathop{\mathrm{CLE}}_3$. The assertion is uniform over choices of the complete resolution. Loops are compared in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization and choice of starting point; collections are compared by matching loops and charging the diameters of unmatched loops. Thus the statement includes coverage and multiplicities of all loops above each fixed positive diameter cutoff.
  - Explanation: Every complete noncrossing resolution has the same nested oriented loop limit, including coverage and multiplicities above a fixed diameter cutoff.
- **claim-mesh-first-four-passage-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-four-passages)): Fix a bulk lattice chart containing $B_{2R}(z)$, with all pins and all changes of ambient domain beyond a fixed proportional outer collar. For every $\alpha<21/8$, there are $C_\alpha<\infty$ and $u_\alpha>0$, depending on that fixed collar but not on the outer conditions, such that, for each fixed $0<u<u_\alpha$, $$\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0} \mathbb P\bigl(\mathcal A_4^{\mathrm{cont}}(z;uR,R)\bigr) \le C_\alpha u^\alpha. \label{eq:pure-four-passages}$$ This applies to the fixed diagonal resolution, and uniformly to complete resolutions. The same statement holds for circular annuli and for fixed-factor changes of the radii. In particular one may fix $\alpha>2$. The mesh limit precedes the limit $u\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: Four resolved contour passages have a mesh-first upper bound whose exponent may exceed the spatial location-count threshold. The estimate is uniform over complete resolutions and buffered exterior data.
- **claim-buffered-crossing-positivity-and-one-passage-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-rsw)): Fix a finite collection of polygonal corridors, each a topological rectangle with designated opposite end gates, and polygonal annular bands. Prescribe one color for each required crossing or circuit. Assume each requirement admits a finite rectangle construction with positive-width unpinned buffers, and that the buffered constructions for opposite colors are disjoint. Their simultaneous occurrence has probability bounded below by a positive constant for all sufficiently fine meshes. The constant may depend on this fixed geometry, and is uniform over pins beyond the buffers and ambient plane or torus laws with common lattice charts.* *There are $C<\infty$, $\rho>0$ such that, in a buffered bulk annulus and for $1\le r<R/C$, $$\mathbb P(\text{a monochromatic spin arm or a resolved contour passage from }B_r\text{ to }B_R^c) \le C(r/R)^\rho. \label{eq:pure-one-passage}$$ For the monochromatic event either color may be specified or both allowed. The annular construction may be conditioned on earlier reveals that remain outside its unpinned test collars. Fixed-factor changes of radii and bounded lattice drawing corrections change only the constants.
  - Explanation: Compatible buffered colored corridor and circuit constructions have a positive simultaneous probability. Iterated blocking circuits give decay for a monochromatic arm or resolved contour passage.
- **claim-uniform-scalar-transfer** (bound, [source](#stmt-thm-transfer)): There is a constant $C<\infty$ with the following property. For each $a>0$, all sufficiently large fixed integers $L$ admit an integer $s_0$, linear functionals $\ell_s:\mathcal X_s\to\mathbb R$, and vectors $e_s\in\mathcal X_s$, for every integer $s\geq s_0$, such that $$\begin{aligned} \\|\ell_s\\|&\leq C,& \left\\|e_s\right\\|_{\mathrm q}&\leq C,& \ell_s(e_s)&=1, \label{eq:transfer-uniform-norms}\\ \left\\|A_sf\right\\|_{\mathrm q}&\leq CL\|\ell_s(f)\|+a\left\\|f\right\\|_{\mathrm q}, \label{eq:transfer-stable}\\ \|\ell_{Ls}(A_sf)-L\ell_s(f)\|&\leq a\left\\|f\right\\|_{\mathrm q}. \label{eq:transfer-compatible} \end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ is independent of $a,L,s$. The choices are made in the following order: first $L$, then a larger fixed measurement ratio $H$, then the approximation accuracies used below, and finally $s_0$. In particular, all estimates hold simultaneously for all $s\geq s_0$ and all bounded functions in $\mathcal X_s$.
  - Explanation: Conditional transfer of every bounded even square-invariant function has a uniformly controlled growing scalar coefficient and an arbitrarily small stable remainder. The scalar measurement is compatible across scales.
- **claim-common-likelihood-window** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-window)): Fix nested enlargements $F_0\subset F_*$ of the support of a test function, with all three relevant gaps positive: from the support to $\partial F_0$, from $\partial F_0$ to $\partial F_*$, and from $F_*$ to $D$. For each $t>0$ there is a finite menu of radial windows, each of positive rescaled width, such that one window $[F_-,C]$ satisfies $$\sup_{F_-\subset F\subset C}\sup_h \\|P_Fh-P_Ch\\|_{L^2(\nu)}\leq t. \label{eq:transfer-window}$$ The second supremum is over all the likelihoods in <a href="#eq:transfer-layer-density" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:transfer-layer-density">[eq:transfer-layer-density]</a>. The menu and its widths are independent of the mesh and of the boundary assignments.
  - Explanation: A finite menu contains a radial window that simultaneously stabilizes every permitted exterior likelihood, rather than a window selected separately for each likelihood.
- **claim-uniform-gaussian-likelihood-smoothing** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-smoothing)): Given $b>0$, the window tolerance in Lemma <a href="#lem:transfer-window" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:transfer-window">17</a> can be chosen so that, for a sufficiently large fixed $M$ and then all sufficiently large $s$, $$\sup_h\\|(I-T_M)P_Ch\\|_2<b. \label{eq:transfer-smoothing}$$ All constants are uniform over the likelihood family and the finite menu of possible windows.
  - Explanation: A fixed sufficiently strong Gaussian observation approximates all core likelihoods uniformly once the window tolerance and lattice threshold have been chosen in the prescribed order.
- **claim-bounded-observable-replacement-across-gaps** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-bounded-replacement)): Fix finitely many pairs of positively separated supports, each a square or a square band, with buffered enlargements that remain disjoint. For every $t>0$, each lattice function $v$ of norm at most one on any one support can be replaced, for its full-plane correlations against all functions of norm at most one on the paired supports, with error at most $t$, by a function of finitely many smeared spin sums in its enlargement. The replacement has norm at most one. The number of sums, their shapes, and a modulus of continuity for the replacing functions belong to a fixed finite menu, independent of $v$ and of all sufficiently fine meshes. Along any mesh sequence, further subsequences of these replacements converge to bounded continuous tests of finitely many continuum smeared spins. Replacements on both sides of each gap can be made together, and preserve spin-flip and square symmetries by averaging.
  - Explanation: Across separated supports, arbitrary bounded microscopic observables can be replaced in their correlations by bounded tests of finitely many smeared spins. The finite menu and continuity control are uniform in the observables and sufficiently fine meshes.
- **claim-finite-complex-charge-identity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-transfer-charges)): Let $z_1,\ldots,z_{2m}$ be distinct complex points, with $m\geq1$, and put $$W_z(u)=\prod_{i<j}\|z_i-z_j\|^{u_i u_j/2},\qquad A_q(z)=\sum_{\substack{u_i\in\{-1,1\}\\\sum_i u_i=q}}W_z(u),$$ where an empty charge class has sum zero. Define $$A_0^d(z)=\sum_{\sum_i u_i=0}W_z(u) \left\|\sum_i u_i z_i\right\|^2.$$ Then $$A_0^dA_0=4A_2^2. \label{eq:transfer-charge-identity}$$ If all points lie in the unit disk, then $A_2/A_0\leq1$, and all ratios $A_q/A_0$ are bounded by one.
  - Explanation: Finite charged sums at distinct complex points satisfy an exact quadratic identity. Inside the unit disk the charge ratios are bounded by the neutral sum.
- **claim-even-radial-spectral-gap** (classification, [source](#stmt-prop-radial-gap)): On the subspace of $\mathcal H$ generated by even, quarter-turn invariant spin tests, the spectrum of $\mathcal D$ below $3$ consists of the constant state at $0$ and one nonzero one-dimensional state at $1$. There is a real linear functional $\ell$ with $\|\ell(F)\|\leq\\|F\\|_2$ such that, for real even quarter-turn invariant tests $F,G$ of finite ordinary $L^2$ norm and $0<r\leq1$, $$\left\|\mathbb E[(\Theta F)D_{-\log r}G] -\mathbb EF\mathbb EG-r\ell(F)\ell(G)\right\| \leq r^3\\|F\\|_2\\|G\\|_2. \label{eq:transfer-radial-gap}$$ The assertion extends from smeared polynomials to bounded field tests by ordinary $L^2$ approximation.
  - Explanation: The even quarter-turn invariant radial spectrum has only the constant and thermal states below the stated gap. Their factored contributions leave a uniformly bounded higher-degree remainder.
- **claim-finite-state-reverse-refresh-identity** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-flow-reverse)): Let $\mu_t$ be a differentiable family of strictly positive probability laws on a finite spin space, and let $I_x$ be specified update sets. Let $f_x$ be fixed bounded real functions, with $\\|f_x\\|_\infty\le a$, $a\ge0$, and let $P_{t,x}$ denote conditional expectation under $\mu_t$ given the complement of $I_x$. Suppose $$\label{eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative} \dot\mu_t(\sigma)=\mu_t(\sigma)c \sum_x\bigl(P_{t,x}f_x(\sigma)-f_x(\sigma)\bigr), \qquad c\ge0.$$ If $a>0$, define the refresh law $$\label{eq:flow-refresh-law} Q_{t,x}(d\eta_{I_x}\mid\sigma_{I_x^c}) =\mu_t(d\eta_{I_x}\mid\sigma_{I_x^c}) \left[1+\frac{f_x(\eta_{I_x}\sigma_{I_x^c}) -P_{t,x}f_x(\sigma)}{2a}\right].$$ It is a probability law. Updating set $I_x$ from this law at rate $2ac$, independently for each $x$, has generator $\mathcal L_t$ with $\mathcal L_t^*\mu_t=-\dot\mu_t$. Thus using $\mathcal L_{1-t}$ from time zero to one carries the law at time one exactly to the law at time zero. If $a=0$, the derivative in <a href="#eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative">[eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative]</a> vanishes and no refresh is needed.
  - Explanation: The specified tilted conditional refresh is a probability law. Independent refresh rates reverse the derivative of the finite probability family, with a separate zero-bound case requiring no refresh.
- **claim-analytic-labeled-conditional-flow** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-flow-analytic)): Fix $L\ge9$ and $d>0$. There are $r>0$ and $C<\infty$, independent of the integer $s$, such that for $\\|V\\|_{s,d}<r$, Equation <a href="#eq:flow-ode" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flow-ode">[eq:flow-ode]</a> has a specified analytic solution taking values in $\mathcal A_{s,d/2}$. Uniformly in $0\le t\le1$, $$\label{eq:flow-intermediate} \sum_\ell \|\ell\|e^{(d/2)(\|\ell\|-1)} \\|v_\ell(t)\\|_\infty\le C\\|V\\|_{s,d}.$$ Its endpoint has the form $$\label{eq:flow-before-compression} V(1)=(V-f)+P^0_{Ls}f+\mathcal R_s(V),\qquad \\|\mathcal R_s(V)\\|_{s,d/2}\le C\\|V\\|_{s,d}^2.$$ On a smaller ball the remainder is analytic and satisfies $\\|D\mathcal R_s(V)\\|\le C\\|V\\|_{s,d}$ and $\\|D^2\mathcal R_s(V)\\|\le C$, with these operator norms from $\mathcal A_{s,d}$ to $\mathcal A_{s,d/2}$. The construction preserves real coefficients and the stated symmetries.
  - Explanation: The conditional differential equation has an analytic labeled solution with uniform weighted control. Its endpoint differs from the explicit linear part by a quadratic analytic remainder.
- **claim-covering-list-compression** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-flow-compression)): For every integer $L\ge9$, there is a linear relabeling map $C_s:\mathcal A_{s,d/2}\to\mathcal A_{Ls,d}$ that leaves the local functions and their roots unchanged and satisfies $$\\|C_sW\\|_{Ls,d}\le\\|W\\|_{s,d/2}.$$ If a declared union is contained in $B_{Ls}$, it is relabeled as a single square. The map commutes with the specified symmetries. The rewriting map $\mathcal S_s=L^2C_s$ preserves the translation density: $$\label{eq:flow-rewrite} \mathcal H_{Ls}(\mathcal S_sW)=\mathcal H_s(W).$$ On input lists of length at least two it also satisfies $$\label{eq:flow-carry} \\|\mathcal S_sW\\|_{Ls,d} \le L^2 e^{-d/2}\\|W\\|_{s,d}.$$
  - Explanation: Connected covering lists can be compressed at the larger scale without changing the local functions or roots. The density normalization is preserved, and lists with multiple boxes gain an additional norm reduction.
- **claim-nonautonomous-decaying-sequence-graph** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-flow-stable-graph)): With the choices just described, there are bounded single-square vectors $w_k\in\mathcal X_k$, $\ell_kw_k=1$, and a real analytic function $h$ on a neighborhood of zero in $\ker\ell_0$, with $h(0)=0$, such that each sufficiently small vector $$\label{eq:flow-graph} V_0=h(u_0)w_0+u_0,\qquad u_0\in\ker\ell_0,$$ has a trajectory $V_{k+1}=\Phi_{s_k}(V_k)$ satisfying $$\sup_{k\ge0}2^k\\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}\le C\\|u_0\\|_{s_0,d}.$$ In a sufficiently small ball these are exactly the initial conditions with a trajectory of finite sufficiently small norm $\sup_k2^k\\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}$. The tangent space of the graph at zero is exactly the set of initial conditions whose linear trajectories $Z_{k+1}=T_{s_k}Z_k$ have finite norm $\sup_k2^k\\|Z_k\\|_{s_k,d}$.
  - Explanation: A locally analytic graph parametrizes exactly the sufficiently small decaying nonlinear trajectories. Its tangent space is characterized by decay of the corresponding linear trajectories.
- **claim-domain-independent-critical-trajectory** (construction, [source](#stmt-thm-critical-trajectory)): For every $J>0$ and finite-range real potential $U$ satisfying the symmetries of Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, the preceding scale parameters can be fixed independently of all domains and boundary data. The initial scale $s_0$ may be enlarged by any prescribed finite amount before the following construction. For each sufficiently small $\epsilon>0$ there is $\lambda_*>0$ and a real analytic function $\beta_c:(-\lambda_*,\lambda_*)\to(0,\infty)$, with $\beta_c(0)=\beta_0$, for which the initial potential <a href="#eq:flow-physical-input" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flow-physical-input">[eq:flow-physical-input]</a> has a trajectory $V_{k+1}=\Phi_{s_k}(V_k)$ satisfying $$\label{eq:flow-critical-decay} \\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}\le\epsilon2^{-k},\qquad k\ge0.$$ Its intermediate one-step trajectories obey $$\label{eq:flow-critical-intermediate} \sup_{0\le t\le1} \sum_\ell \|\ell\|e^{(d/2)(\|\ell\|-1)} \\|v_{k,\ell}(t)\\|_\infty\le C\epsilon2^{-k}.$$ The same construction applies to both signs of $\lambda$.
  - Explanation: Temperature transversality selects a real analytic bulk branch whose effective interactions decay at every scale. The scale choices precede all domain and boundary data, and the construction permits either perturbation sign.
- **claim-periodized-flow-residual** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-flow-periodization)): Suppose $Ls\le N/10$, and suppose a real one-step trajectory obeys $$\sup_{0\le t\le1}\sum_\ell \|\ell\| e^{(d/2)(\|\ell\|-1)}\\|v_\ell(t)\\|_\infty\le r_s, \qquad \\|f\\|_\infty\le r_s,$$ with $r_s$ in the fixed small ball. Let $\mu_t=\mu_{s,N}^{V(t)}$, and let $P_{t,x}^{N}$ be its exact torus conditional expectation over $B_{Ls}^\circ(x)$. There is a signed measure $e_t$, of total mass zero, such that $$\label{eq:flow-finite-residual} \dot\mu_t=\mu_t s^{-2}\sum_{x\in\mathbb T_N} (P_{t,x}^{N}f_x-f_x)+e_t, \qquad \\|e_t\\|_{\mathop{\mathrm{TV}}}\le C r_s(N/s)^2e^{-cN/s}.$$ The constants $C,c>0$ depend on the fixed flow parameters, but not on $s,N,t$.
  - Explanation: Periodic evaluation of the plane flow differs from the exact torus probability equation by a centered signed residual. Exponentially weighted long lists control its total variation.
- **claim-independent-mark-edit-coupling** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-edit-coupling)): Let the physical torus law $\mu_{\lambda,N}$ be at the temperature of Theorem <a href="#thm:critical-trajectory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:critical-trajectory">26</a>, with trajectory bound <a href="#eq:flow-critical-decay" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:flow-critical-decay">[eq:flow-critical-decay]</a>. Let $$K_N=\max\{k\ge0:Ls_k\le N/10\}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. There is a coupling of $\sigma^{(0)}\sim\mu_{0,N}$, $\sigma^{(\lambda)}\sim\mu_{\lambda,N}$, and Bernoulli marks $M_{k,x}$, $0\le k\le K_N$, $x\in\mathbb T_N$, with the following properties. The marks are mutually independent and independent of $\sigma^{(0)}$, and $$\label{eq:flow-mark-probabilities} \mathbb P(M_{k,x}=1)\le C\epsilon2^{-k}s_k^{-2}.$$ Except on an event of probability $o(1)$ as $N\to\infty$, $\sigma^{(\lambda)}$ can be obtained from $\sigma^{(0)}$ by changing spins only inside the union of the marked squares $B_{Ls_k}(x)$. The values of the changed spins may depend on all previous refreshes. The constant and the exceptional probability are independent of test domains and exterior configurations. Enlarging these squares by any fixed number of lattice steps preserves the conclusion.
  - Explanation: The perturbed torus law couples to the pure law through independently marked edit squares, apart from a vanishing exceptional event. The marks are independent of the pure sample; refreshed spin values need not be.
- **claim-bulk-tests-stable-under-arbitrary-edits** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-bulk-stability)): After increasing $s_0$ if necessary, there is $\epsilon_*>0$, depending only on the fixed constants in Proposition <a href="#prop:edit-coupling" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:edit-coupling">28</a>, with the following property. Fix any finite family $\mathcal T$ of polygonal corridor crossings, polygonal-band circuits, and four-passage square annulus tests. Let $N=N(\delta)$ be integers with $\delta\downarrow0$ and $\inf_\delta\delta N(\delta)>T_0(\mathcal T)$, where $T_0(\mathcal T)$ is sufficiently large that all tests and their fixed collars lie in one planar chart of the physical torus. If $\epsilon\leq\epsilon_*$ and the marks satisfy <a href="#eq:stability-mark-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stability-mark-law">[eq:stability-mark-law]</a>, then $$\mathbb P\bigl(D_T(\sigma^0,M)=1\text{ for some }T\in\mathcal T\bigr) \longrightarrow0 \quad\text{as }\delta\downarrow0. \label{eq:stability-main}$$ The threshold $\epsilon_*$ is independent of the family, its polygonal complexity, and all its fixed positive widths. The mesh threshold and the constants in the convergence bound may depend on that geometry.* *Consequently the pure and perturbed torus laws at the trajectory of Theorem <a href="#thm:critical-trajectory" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:critical-trajectory">26</a> have asymptotically identical joint laws for every such finite family. In particular, pure positive lower bounds for any fixed finite compatible system of signed buffered corridor and circuit events, and pure upper limits for four-passage events, hold for the perturbed law as well.
  - Explanation: Every fixed finite family of macroscopic tests becomes insensitive to every assignment in the marked-square union. This transfers the joint test laws without imposing restrictions on the refreshed values.
- **claim-pivotal-square-forces-local-passages** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-stability-pivotal)): Let $T$ be a fixed polygonal crossing, circuit, or one of the three annular tests in <a href="#eq:stability-checks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stability-checks">[eq:stability-checks]</a>. Suppose that two spin configurations agree off a square of radius $b$ but give different values to $T$. Enlarge this square by a fixed factor to contain all contour segments affected by the change. There is a positive geometry scale $a_T$ with the following properties. Set $$u=\min\{a_T,\max\{b,\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(z,\mathcal E_T)\}\},\qquad v=\min\{a_T,\max\{b,\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(z,\mathcal V_T)\}\},$$ where $\mathcal E_T$ and $\mathcal V_T$ are the finite collections of sides and vertices of the test polygons, and $z$ is the edit center. For $H_2$, these collections also include the confinement line segments and their intersections with the two square boundaries. For $b<a_T$, the common contours outside the enlarged edit square must supply the following checks, after fixed crops:* 1. *four passages on rings between scales $b$ and $u$;* 2. *two passages confined to an appropriate half-plane on rings between scales $u$ and $v$;* 3. *one passage on rings between scales $v$ and $a_T$.* *Rings within a fixed multiplicative distance of transition scales may be omitted. The half-plane checks may be centered on the nearby side and the last checks near the nearby vertex. For a fixed test only a bounded number of further rings need be omitted. The constants are uniform over translations, rotations, and dilations of each of the three fixed annular shapes.
  - Explanation: A test-changing local edit forces contour checks in successive interior, edge, and vertex regimes. Confinement walls and terminal walls are treated separately, and passages retain parameter multiplicities.
- **claim-integrable-macroscopic-subarc-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stability-counts)): Fix a compact region $K$ and a length $a>0$, in a bulk chart containing a fixed $20a$-neighborhood of $K$. There is a random finite list $\mathcal L_\delta$ of compact contour parameter-subarcs, chosen without reference to a test center or direction, with $\sup_\delta\mathbb E\|\mathcal L_\delta\|<\infty$ for sufficiently fine meshes. The list covers the following visits, with their contour multiplicities. If a contour visits $x\in K$ and reaches distance $10a$ from $x$ in both parameter directions, a member of the list contains that visit and lies between the first such distant visit in each direction. Subarcs are retained only in a fixed enlargement of $K$. The same assertion holds for the limiting complete loop collection, with a list of finite expected cardinality. Fixed enlargements of the chart and fixed crops of the excursion lengths only change the bound.
  - Explanation: A center-independent and direction-independent list covers qualifying macroscopic contour visits with their multiplicities, and its expected size stays bounded in the mesh limit.
- **claim-near-support-contact-area-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stability-tangency)): Fix a bulk region and a positive excursion length. For the pure contours at mesh $\delta$, let $G_K(z,\theta,u)$ be the event that a contour subarc comes within $C u$ of $z$, travels the fixed excursion length on both sides of this visit, and throughout those two portions stays above the line of direction $\theta$ through $z$ lowered by $CKu$. For each fixed $K$, $$\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0}\mathbb P_0(G_K(z,\theta,u))=o_K(u), \label{eq:stability-tangency}$$ as $u\downarrow0$ with $K$ fixed, uniformly over centers and directions in a smaller bulk chart. Fixed crops or fixed changes of the constants have the same conclusion.
  - Explanation: Macroscopic contour excursions that nearly support a line have probability smaller than the contact scale. The argument averages contact areas over directions and uses the integrable subarc list.
- **claim-bulk-half-plane-confined-passage-decay** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-pure-half-plane)): For the events in <a href="#eq:stability-checks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stability-checks">[eq:stability-checks]</a>, in any buffered pure bulk chart, $$\limsup_{\delta\downarrow0}\mathbb P_0(H_2(z,\theta;r,R))=o(r/R) \quad\text{as }r/R\downarrow0. \label{eq:stability-halfplane}$$ The ratio is fixed before taking the mesh limit. The assertion is uniform in translations and rotations of the fixed geometry, and continues to hold with fixed proportional crops.
  - Explanation: Two passages confined geometrically to a half-plane inside a free bulk chart have a mesh-first probability smaller than their radius ratio. This is not a boundary-condition estimate on the confinement line.
- **claim-independent-mark-ring-product-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stability-products)): There is a fixed constant $D$ with the following property. Take annular checks of the types <a href="#eq:stability-checks" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stability-checks">[eq:stability-checks]</a>, whose inner radii exceed $C b$, and whose enlarged neighborhoods and proportional inner and outer collars are disjoint. More precisely, after absorbing rotations and lattice margins, each check has a collar $W_j$ of the form $$S_{2R_j}(z_j,\theta_j)\setminus S_{r_j/2}(z_j,\theta_j),$$ the other check supports lie outside $W_j$, and the sets $\mathcal J_j$ are disjoint. All collars lie in bulk torus charts. For arbitrary independent mark probabilities at or below this cutoff, $$\mathbb P\left(\bigcap_{j=1}^m T_j^+\right) \leq\prod_{j=1}^m D\mathbb P(T_j^+). \label{eq:stability-product}$$ The constant $D$ depends on the fixed collar proportions and is independent of the annular aspect ratios and the mark probabilities.
  - Explanation: Separated annular possibility checks admit a product upper bound through pure conditional density comparison and disjoint independent local mark sets.
- **claim-summable-insertion-center-count** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-stability-area)): Assume the possibility bounds just used. For a fixed polygonal test $T$, the total contribution to its change probability from all marks of level $k$ is at most $$C_T\epsilon 2^{-k} \min\{1,(\delta b_k^{\mathrm{lat}}/a_T)^{c_*}\}, \label{eq:stability-level-bound}$$ for a fixed $c_*>0$. The assertion also holds during any spatially nonuniform partial activation of that level. For the three fixed annular shapes the constant is uniform in their scale, position, and rotation.
  - Explanation: Spatial summation of pivotal insertions gives a summable level bound with a positive power of the physical edit radius. It also holds during nonuniform partial activation.
- **claim-uniform-annular-possibility-bootstrap** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-stability-bootstrap)): There is $\epsilon_*>0$, chosen from the three fixed annular shapes and the fixed constants of the mark law, such that the following holds on every sufficiently fine finite torus. Activate the independent marks in order of increasing radius. At a stage with maximum permitted radius $b$, every check of ratio $A$ with inner radius at least $C_{\rm mon}b$, and with its prescribed bulk collars, satisfies $$\mathbb P(T_i^+)\leq q_i+\tau_i, \qquad i\in\{4,h,1\}. \label{eq:stability-bootstrapped}$$ This remains true throughout continuous, possibly spatially nonuniform activation of marks of the current radius.
  - Explanation: Increasing-radius activation preserves the monitored possibility bounds uniformly over the fixed annular shapes. The threshold is chosen before later polygonal test geometries.
- **claim-exact-private-bit-vertex-representation** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-bit-representation)): There is a finite choice of $M$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\lvert\lambda\rvert$, positive functions $$W_v\bigl(\sigma_v,b_{\mathcal B(v)}\bigr)$$ represent the spin weight in <a href="#eq:comparison-local-factors" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison-local-factors">[eq:comparison-local-factors]</a> exactly, up to a constant, as the spin marginal of $\prod_v W_v$. Here $\mathcal B(v)$ is the set of the $4M$ bits on bonds incident to $v$. The same functions are used whether a physical spin is free or pinned. There are only finitely many local tables, and uniformly in their entries $$W_v(\sigma,b)\longrightarrow W_v^0(\sigma,b):=\exp\left(a\sigma\sum_{i\in\mathcal B(v)}b_i\right) \quad\text{as }\lambda\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:comparison-table-limit}$$ The representation applies to either sign of every sufficiently small local perturbation.
  - Explanation: Private auxiliary bond bits realize each sufficiently small local factor exactly through positive vertex tables. The same tables work for free or pinned physical spins and approach the unperturbed tables uniformly.
- **claim-strict-finite-table-comparison-margins** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-margins)): After decreasing the allowed interval of $\lambda$, every free factor $F_v$ is strictly log-supermodular in each pair of distinct incident bits, and every kernel $k_v$ is strictly increasing in each incident bit. For every incident coordinate $i$, moreover, $$\sup_b d_i\log P_v^-(b) <\inf_b d_i\log F_v(b) \le\sup_b d_i\log F_v(b) <\inf_b d_i\log P_v^+(b). \label{eq:comparison-strict-margins}$$ The gaps are bounded below by a positive constant independent of the volume and the pins.
  - Explanation: Small perturbations preserve strict attraction of free bit factors, monotonicity of free-spin kernels, and uniform separation of minus, free, and plus coordinate increments.
- **claim-order-coupling-at-strict-incidences** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-strict-incidence)): Consider two laws formed from the same vertex factors $W_v$, with possibly different sets of pinned physical spins. Label the laws lower and upper. Let $V$ be a union of nearest-neighbor connected components of their common free vertex set. At each vertex outside $V$ adjacent to $V$, require one of the following possibilities:* <div class="center"> \| *lower law* \| *upper law* \| \|:--------------:\|:-------------:\| \| *free* \| *pinned plus* \| \| *pinned minus* \| *free* \| \| *pinned minus* \| *pinned plus* \| </div> *Then there is a coupling in which all bits on bonds touching $V$, and all physical spins in $V$, are lower than or equal to their upper-law counterparts. The assertion remains valid if arbitrary values of bits on bonds not touching $V$ have been conditioned on separately in the two laws. It consequently remains valid for arbitrary mixtures of these exterior bit conditions.
  - Explanation: The permitted strict pin/free incidence changes order all touching bits and common free spins. Conditioning separately on exterior bits and then mixing preserves the coupling.
- **claim-stopped-reveals-preserve-exact-conditioning** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-stopped-reveal)): Suppose spins are queried one at a time, each next query and the stopping decision being determined by the preceding queries and their answers. Conditional on a complete transcript, the remaining law is exactly the original law with the revealed spins pinned. This remains true after conditioning on any event determined by the transcript.* *In a finite triangulated disk with a prescribed inner disk, one can explore from its outer boundary for a shield of either color $s$. On success, no inner-disk spin has been queried, and every queried neighbor of the remaining component containing that disk has color $s$. A color-$s$ circuit in a narrower annular band separating the two disks guarantees success. All queried sites are on the exterior side of that circuit or on the circuit itself.* *The corresponding exploration in a triangulated topological rectangle, from one side toward the opposite side, determines whether there is a color-$s$ crossing between the other two sides. On success its facing boundary contains such a crossing, and the exploration queries no spins strictly beyond it toward the opposite side. If that crossing extends past the ends of a comparison region, every nearest-neighbor incidence from the region across the explored side meets a pin of color $s$.
  - Explanation: Adaptive transcripts impose exactly the queried spin pins. Finite disk and rectangle explorations produce monochromatic facing boundaries while leaving the protected side unqueried.
- **claim-mtp-extreme-rectangle-density-bound** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-comparison-rectangles)): Let $p(i,j)>0$ be an MTP$_2$ probability mass on two disjoint finite sets of binary coordinates $I,J$. Let $i_-,i_+,j_-,j_+$ denote their constant minus and plus configurations, set $f=\log p$, and put $$\Lambda=f(i_+,j_+)+f(i_-,j_-) -f(i_+,j_-)-f(i_-,j_+)\ge0. \label{eq:comparison-extreme-ratio}$$ Then, for arbitrary configurations $a,a'$ on $I$ and $b,b'$ on $J$, $$\bigl\|f(a,b)+f(a',b')-f(a,b')-f(a',b)\bigr\|\le\Lambda. \label{eq:comparison-all-rectangles}$$ Consequently every two conditional laws of $I$ given $J$, and every two mixtures of those conditional laws, have pointwise density ratio between $e^{-\Lambda}$ and $e^{\Lambda}$.
  - Explanation: The extreme corner interaction bounds every mixed rectangle of a positive attractive binary mass, including unordered terminal configurations. Normalization yields pointwise comparison for conditional laws and arbitrary mixtures.
- **claim-perturbed-interior-density-comparison** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-interior-density)): Fix a square $Q$ and a larger square $\widetilde Q$, with $\overline Q\subset\operatorname{int}\widetilde Q$, in a fixed physical torus chart. Take lattice mesh $\delta\downarrow0$, with torus side $N$ chosen so that this chart has a fixed macroscopic buffer from its translates. Suppose every lattice vertex in $\widetilde Q$ is free in a domain spin law $\nu_{\Omega,\lambda}^{\tau}$. For all sufficiently small mesh there is a constant $C<\infty$, depending on the two fixed squares and the chart but not on $\Omega,\tau$, or the mesh, such that $$C^{-1}\le \frac{\nu_{\Omega,\lambda}^{\tau}(\sigma_{Q\cap\delta\mathbb Z^2}=\eta)} {\mu_{\lambda,N}(\sigma_{Q\cap\delta\mathbb Z^2}=\eta)} \le C \quad\text{for every spin configuration }\eta. \label{eq:comparison-interior-density}$$ The same bounds hold for every event determined by these spins. The exterior pins may be arbitrary, including those induced by any admissible exterior contour configuration.
  - Explanation: An interior square with a fixed free buffer has uniformly comparable domain and perturbed-torus spin densities. The comparison permits arbitrary actual exterior pins.
- **claim-fixed-geometry-colored-boundary-squeeze** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-boundary-squeeze)): Fix the domain, signs, a displacement $h$, the preceding polygonal placements, and a finite list $S_1,\ldots,S_m$ of allowed plus strips. Let $V_A\in\{0,1\}^m$ record their plus crossings in the pure Dobrushin law in an ordinary mesh approximation of $A_h$. Let $V_n^+$ record plus crossings in the target, filling the portions of the strips outside $D_n$ virtually with plus. There is a coupling with $$\mathbb P(V_A\leq V_n^+)=1-o(1) \qquad(n\longrightarrow\infty), \label{eq:curves-vector-order}$$ where the inequality holds coordinatewise. The error is uniform over all admissible target exterior configurations. The sign-reversed construction gives the corresponding transfer of minus crossings from pure domains favoring plus. The smallness condition on $\lambda$ is the one fixed at the beginning of the section; only the mesh threshold and the error bound depend on these fixed geometries.
  - Explanation: A finite vector of pure comparison-domain crossings transfers in order to virtually filled target crossings with a vanishing error. The sign-reversed comparison transfers the opposite color.
- **claim-local-signed-strip-interface-redrawing** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-curves-local-barrier)): Fix finitely many allowed plus strips and, in the sign-reversed construction, finitely many allowed minus strips. For all sufficiently large $n$, the prescribed target curve has a drawing $\widetilde\gamma_n$ with the same traversal order and $$d_{\rm curv}(\widetilde\gamma_n,\gamma_n)=O(\delta_n)$$ that is disjoint from every monochromatic crossing of its respective virtually filled strip. The two half-stubs and neighborhoods of the marks can retain their original drawings. The filling and redrawing change no spin in the conditional law and remove no interaction term.
  - Explanation: A simultaneous local redrawing preserves target traversal order and stays within a mesh-sized curve distance while becoming exactly disjoint from the virtually filled colored barriers.
- **claim-equal-law-ordered-chord-identification** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-ordered-chords)): Let $L,U$ be random simple chords from $a$ to $b$ in a Jordan domain, meeting its boundary only at their endpoints, and let $K$ be a random compact connected subset of its closure containing $a,b$. Suppose <a href="#eq:curves-forbidden-sides" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curves-forbidden-sides">[eq:curves-forbidden-sides]</a> holds almost surely. Then $P(L)\subset P(U)$ almost surely. If $L$ and $U$ have the same trace law, then $L=U=K$ as compact sets almost surely.
  - Explanation: Connectedness and forbidden-side containment order the two chord side domains. Equal trace laws force the side domains and chords to coincide, and connectedness identifies the intervening compact set.
- **claim-progress-coordinate-curve-upgrade** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-lem-curve-upgrade)): Let $\Gamma:[0,1]\to\overline D$ be continuous and injective, with $\Gamma(0)=a$, $\Gamma(1)=b$, and $\Gamma((0,1))\subset D$. Let $g_n:[0,1]\to\mathbb C$ be continuous simple curves whose endpoints tend to $a,b$ and whose traces tend in Hausdorff distance to $\Gamma([0,1])$. There are continuous functions $u_n:[0,1]\to[0,1]$ with endpoint values tending to $0,1$ such that $$e_n:=\sup_t\|g_n(t)-\Gamma(u_n(t))\|\longrightarrow0. \label{eq:curves-coordinate-error}$$ Put $B_n=\sup_{s<t}(u_n(s)-u_n(t))$. If $B_n\to0$, then $d_{\rm curv}(g_n,\Gamma)\to0$. Conversely, for every fixed $0<\eta<1$, a backward drop $B_n\geq\eta$, with $n$ sufficiently large, forces six disjoint passages from radius $r$ to one fixed radius $R_\eta>0$, for every $e_n<r<R_\eta$, about a center on $\Gamma([\eta/2,1-\eta/2])\Subset D$.* *For random curves, suppose the trace and endpoint convergence hold in probability in a coupling with such a random $\Gamma$, and suppose <a href="#eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck">[eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck]</a> holds for every deterministic compact interior $F$ and sufficiently small fixed $R$. Then $d_{\rm curv}(g_n,\Gamma)\to0$ in probability in that coupling.
  - Explanation: Continuous progress coordinates upgrade Hausdorff trace convergence when backward drops vanish. Persistent drops force repeated interior annular passages, allowing the bottleneck estimate to establish full oriented curve convergence in probability.

Its restriction $\\tau$ to $\\mathbb Z^2\\setminus\\Omega$ is independent of $P$. The map $P\\mapsto\\sigma|_\\Omega$ is a bijection between $\\mathcal P_\\Omega^\\xi$ and all spin assignments in $\\Omega$. Under this bijection, <a href=\"#eq:contour-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:contour-law\">[eq:contour-law]</a> is precisely the Gibbs conditional law for <a href=\"#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> with exterior spins $\\tau$. Denote this law by $\\nu_{\\Omega,\\beta,\\lambda}^{\\tau}$.*\n\n*The exterior nearest neighbors along each of the two boundary arcs between the marks have a constant sign, and the signs of the two arcs are opposite. The interaction in <a href=\"#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> is finite-range, invariant under global spin flip and the square symmetries, and has finitely many local interaction types up to translation.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

The map $P\\mapsto\\sigma|_\\Omega$ is a bijection between $\\mathcal P_\\Omega^\\xi$ and all spin assignments in $\\Omega$. Denote this law by $\\nu_{\\Omega,\\beta,\\lambda}^{\\tau}$.*\n\n*The exterior nearest neighbors along each of the two boundary arcs between the marks have a constant sign, and the signs of the two arcs are opposite.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-buffered",
        "exact_quote": "Consider a finite critical nearest-neighbor ferromagnetic Ising graph, disjoint vertex sets $I,J,F$, and common pins on $F$. Let $\\mu_b$ be the marginal law on $I$ obtained by prescribing an arbitrary configuration $b$ on $J$. Whenever the following sets lie in the indicated square-lattice charts, the bounds hold for all configurations $a$ on $I$ and all $b,b'$.*\n\n1.  *For every $c>0$ there is $K_c<\\infty$ such that $I\\subset B_R(z)$, $J\\subset B_{(1+c)R}(z)^c$ imply $$K_c^{-1}\\le \\frac{\\mu_b(a)}{\\mu_{b'}(a)}\\le K_c.$$*\n\n2.  *There are absolute $K<\\infty$, $\\zeta>0$ such that $I\\subset B_w(z)$, $J\\subset B_R(z)^c$, and $R\\ge8w\\ge8$ imply $$\\left|\\log\\frac{\\mu_b(a)}{\\mu_{b'}(a)}\\right|\n     \\le K(w/R)^\\zeta.$$ No free neighborhood of $I$ is required in these two assertions: additional common pins may lie anywhere outside $I\\cup J$.*\n\n3.  *Either law can be replaced by any mixture of the indicated conditional laws. The fixed-ratio comparison also holds for laws from different domains, including the plane and a torus, if there is a common isometric lattice collar separating the observed spins from every difference in the data. In this cross-domain assertion the collar is unpinned in both laws. Applying a common randomization kernel to the observed spins preserves each density bound.*\n\n*All constants are independent of the number, positions, and values of the common pins and of the mesh. In the first two assertions the same ratio bounds hold when the roles of the two terminal sets are reversed.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-buffered",

        "anchor": "stmt-cor-pure-annular",
        "exact_quote": "Let $2\\le r<R/4$, $A=B_R\\setminus B_r$, and $W=B_{2R}\\setminus B_{r/2}$, with integer rounding of the boundaries. Suppose $W$ is an unpinned square-lattice chart. The law on $A$, conditional on arbitrary spins outside $W$, has pointwise density relative to $\\mu_0|_A$ between $K^{-1}$ and $K$, for an absolute $K$ independent of $R/r$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-transmission",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $\\eta\\in(0,1/8)$ with the following property. Let $\\mu$ be a pure critical Gibbs law with common pins, let $q=q(\\sigma_S)$ be order-positive and centered for its source marginal, and let $J$ be an admissible cut at scales $w,s$. For every bounded target observable $h=h(\\sigma_T)$, when $w$ is sufficiently large, $$\\left|\\mathbb E_\\mu[qh]\\right|\n \\le C\\left\\|h\\right\\|_\\infty (w/s)^{-7/8+\\eta}\n               \\mathbb E_\\mu[qM_J],\n \\qquad M_J=s^{-7/8}\\sum_{x\\in J}\\sigma_x.\n \\label{eq:pure-signed}$$ The constant depends only on the admissibility constants and the fixed chart geometry. It is independent of the mass of $q_+$, the mesh, the number and values of source pins, and the ratio $w/s$. The same inequality holds for expectation differences of two ordered source configurations, and after averaging an ordered coupling of random source configurations, with the common Gibbs continuation. All changed pins and fields lie on the source side of the cut; the target-side continuation is identical in the compared laws. One may decrease $\\eta$, keeping it positive.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-transmission",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $\\eta\\in(0,1/8)$ with the following property. Let $\\mu$ be a pure critical Gibbs law with common pins, let $q=q(\\sigma_S)$ be order-positive and centered for its source marginal, and let $J$ be an admissible cut at scales $w,s$. For every bounded target observable $h=h(\\sigma_T)$, when $w$ is sufficiently large, $$\\left|\\mathbb E_\\mu[qh]\\right|\n \\le C\\left\\|h\\right\\|_\\infty (w/s)^{-7/8+\\eta}\n               \\mathbb E_\\mu[qM_J],\n \\qquad M_J=s^{-7/8}\\sum_{x\\in J}\\sigma_x.\n \\label{eq:pure-signed}$$ The constant depends only on the admissibility constants and the fixed chart geometry. It is independent of the mass of $q_+$, the mesh, the number and values of source pins, and the ratio $w/s$. The same inequality holds for expectation differences of two ordered source configurations, and after averaging an ordered coupling of random source configurations, with the common Gibbs continuation. All changed pins and fields lie on the source side of the cut; the target-side continuation is identical in the compared laws. One may decrease $\\eta$, keeping it positive.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "There is a fixed exponent $\\eta\\in(0,1/8)$ with the following property.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-line-continuity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $J_s,J'_s$ be horizontal or vertical lattice segments of length $O(s)$, with corresponding portions displaced by at most $Cw$ and unmatched end portions of total length $O(w)$. The assertion also applies to concentric square contours of radii comparable to $s$, differing in radius by at most $Cw$, and to a fixed finite union of these pairs. Assume their union has a free neighborhood of width $cs$, covered by a fixed number of buffered lattice charts. For each fixed $h=w/s\\in(0,h_0]$, $$\\limsup_{s\\to\\infty}\n \\left\\|s^{-7/8}\\sum_{J_s}\\sigma_x\n       -s^{-7/8}\\sum_{J'_s}\\sigma_x\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n \\le C_0h^{3/8}.\n \\label{eq:pure-lines}$$ The bound is uniform over arbitrary pins beyond the stated free neighborhood and mixtures of their laws. The constant depends only on the fixed geometry and buffer.",

      },

        "exact_quote": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:line-continuity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:line-continuity\">7</a>, let $\\mathcal F$ be any spin information containing $\\sigma_{J'_s}$, and let $P_{\\mathcal F}$ denote conditional expectation.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-spin",
        "exact_quote": "There is $a_\\sigma>0$, determined by the square-lattice convention, such that, for distinct physical points $z_1,\\ldots,z_n$, $$a_\\sigma^{-n}\\delta^{-n/8}\n \\mathbb E_0\\prod_{i=1}^n\\sigma_{x_i^\\delta}\n \\longrightarrow S(z_1,\\ldots,z_n),\n \\qquad \\delta x_i^\\delta\\longrightarrow z_i.$$ For odd $n$, $S=0$; for even $n$, $S$ is the nonnegative square root of $$S(z_1,\\ldots,z_n)^2\n =2^{-n/2}\\!\\sum_{\\substack{u_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}\\\\\\sum_i u_i=0}}\n       \\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_iu_j/2}.\n \\label{eq:pure-spin-formula}$$ Thus $S(z,w)=|z-w|^{-1/4}$. The convergence is uniform when the points lie in compact sets with fixed mutual separation. Also $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le\\mathbb E_0[\\sigma_x\\sigma_y]&\\le C(1+|x-y|)^{-1/4},\n \\label{eq:pure-two-point}\\\\\n 0\\le\\mathbb E^+_{B_r}[\\sigma_0]&\\le C(1+r)^{-1/8}.\n \\label{eq:pure-plus-one-point}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $n\\ge2$, repeated sites allowed, $$\\left|\\mathbb E_0\\prod_{i=1}^n\\sigma_{x_i}\\right|\n \\le C^n\\prod_{i=1}^n\n \\left(1+\\min_{j\\ne i}|x_i-x_j|\\right)^{-1/8}.\n \\label{eq:pure-multipoint-bound}$$ For every fixed finite collection of bounded compactly supported Riemann-integrable spatial test functions $\\varphi$, the smeared variables $$\\Phi_\\delta(\\varphi)\n =a_\\sigma^{-1}\\delta^{15/8}\n                  \\sum_{x\\in\\mathbb Z^2}\\varphi(\\delta x)\\sigma_x$$ have joint moment limits obtained by integrating $S$ against the test functions. Here Riemann integrability is equivalent to having a set of discontinuities of two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero. Spatial smearings are chosen in this class and fixed before taking the mesh limit; polygonal cell indicators are included. The limiting correlation functions are covariant under Möbius transformations, including inversion, with weight $1/8$ at each spin insertion.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-energy",
        "exact_quote": "Let $e_1,e_2$ be the coordinate unit vectors and set $b_j(x)=\\sigma_x\\sigma_{x+e_j}-1/\\sqrt2$. In the full-plane critical law, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_0(b_j(0),b_k(x))\n   =\\frac{1+o(1)}{\\pi^2|x|^2},\\qquad |x|\\longrightarrow\\infty,\n \\quad j,k\\in\\{1,2\\}.\n \\label{eq:pure-energy}$$ For a fixed finite local average $B(x)=\\sum_qc_qb_{j_q}(x+d_q)$, with $c_q\\ge0$ and $c=\\sum_qc_q>0$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_0(B(0),b_k(x))\\sim\\frac{c}{\\pi^2|x|^2},\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_0(B(0),B(x))\\sim\\frac{c^2}{\\pi^2|x|^2}.$$ In particular square symmetrization of the bond energy cannot cancel its leading covariance.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $e_1,e_2$ be the coordinate unit vectors and set $b_j(x)=\\sigma_x\\sigma_{x+e_j}-1/\\sqrt2$.           "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-pure-sle",
        "exact_quote": "In ordinary lattice approximations of a fixed polygonal Jordan domain with two distinct marked boundary points, the critical nearest-neighbor Ising interface with opposite constant signs on the two marked arcs converges to chordal $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SLE}}_3$. Convergence is in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization, oriented from the first mark to the second. Either local turn at an alternating face is allowed, including the deterministic rule of the problem. Bounded local rounding and half-stubs have vanishing effect on this convergence.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-pure-cle",
        "exact_quote": "In a plus Jordan domain with lattice boundaries converging uniformly modulo parametrization, every complete noncrossing resolution of all spin-disagreement edges converges to the same oriented nested $\\mathop{\\mathrm{CLE}}_3$. The assertion is uniform over choices of the complete resolution. Loops are compared in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization and choice of starting point; collections are compared by matching loops and charging the diameters of unmatched loops. Thus the statement includes coverage and multiplicities of all loops above each fixed positive diameter cutoff.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-four-passages",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a bulk lattice chart containing $B_{2R}(z)$, with all pins and all changes of ambient domain beyond a fixed proportional outer collar. For every $\\alpha<21/8$, there are $C_\\alpha<\\infty$ and $u_\\alpha>0$, depending on that fixed collar but not on the outer conditions, such that, for each fixed $0<u<u_\\alpha$, $$\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(\\mathcal A_4^{\\mathrm{cont}}(z;uR,R)\\bigr)\n \\le C_\\alpha u^\\alpha.\n \\label{eq:pure-four-passages}$$ This applies to the fixed diagonal resolution, and uniformly to complete resolutions. The same statement holds for circular annuli and for fixed-factor changes of the radii. In particular one may fix $\\alpha>2$. The mesh limit precedes the limit $u\\downarrow0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-four-passages",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a bulk lattice chart containing $B_{2R}(z)$, with all pins and all changes of ambient domain beyond a fixed proportional outer collar. The same statement holds for circular annuli and for fixed-factor changes of the radii. In particular one may fix $\\alpha>2$. The mesh limit precedes the limit $u\\downarrow0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Fix a bulk lattice chart containing $B_{2R}(z)$, with all pins and all changes of ambient domain beyond a fixed proportional outer collar.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-rsw",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a finite collection of polygonal corridors, each a topological rectangle with designated opposite end gates, and polygonal annular bands. Prescribe one color for each required crossing or circuit. Assume each requirement admits a finite rectangle construction with positive-width unpinned buffers, and that the buffered constructions for opposite colors are disjoint. Their simultaneous occurrence has probability bounded below by a positive constant for all sufficiently fine meshes. The constant may depend on this fixed geometry, and is uniform over pins beyond the buffers and ambient plane or torus laws with common lattice charts.*\n\n*There are $C<\\infty$, $\\rho>0$ such that, in a buffered bulk annulus and for $1\\le r<R/C$, $$\\mathbb P(\\text{a monochromatic spin arm or a resolved contour passage\n                 from }B_r\\text{ to }B_R^c)\n \\le C(r/R)^\\rho.\n \\label{eq:pure-one-passage}$$ For the monochromatic event either color may be specified or both allowed. The annular construction may be conditioned on earlier reveals that remain outside its unpinned test collars. Fixed-factor changes of radii and bounded lattice drawing corrections change only the constants.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-rsw",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite collection of polygonal corridors, each a topological rectangle with designated opposite end gates, and polygonal annular bands. Their simultaneous occurrence has probability bounded below by a positive constant for all sufficiently fine meshes. The constant may depend on this fixed geometry, and is uniform over pins beyond the buffers and ambient plane or torus laws with common lattice charts.*\n\n*There are $C<\\infty$, $\\rho>0$ such that, in a buffered bulk annulus and for $1\\le r<R/C$, $$\\mathbb P(\\text{a monochromatic spin arm or a resolved contour passage\n                 from }B_r\\text{ to }B_R^c)\n \\le C(r/R)^\\rho.\n \\label{eq:pure-one-passage}$$ For the monochromatic event either color may be specified or both allowed. The annular construction may be conditioned on earlier reveals that remain outside its unpinned test collars. Fixed-factor changes of radii and bounded lattice drawing corrections change only the constants.",

          "exact_quote": "There are $C<\\infty$, $\\rho>0$ such that, in a buffered bulk annulus and for $1\\le r<R/C$, $$\\mathbb P(\\text{a monochromatic spin arm or a resolved contour passage\n                 from }B_r\\text{ to }B_R^c)\n \\le C(r/R)^\\rho.\n \\label{eq:pure-one-passage}$$",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-rsw"
    },
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-transfer",
        "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C<\\infty$ with the following property. For each $a>0$, all sufficiently large fixed integers $L$ admit an integer $s_0$, linear functionals $\\ell_s:\\mathcal X_s\\to\\mathbb R$, and vectors $e_s\\in\\mathcal X_s$, for every integer $s\\geq s_0$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\ell_s\\|&\\leq C,& \\left\\|e_s\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q}&\\leq C,& \\ell_s(e_s)&=1,\n \\label{eq:transfer-uniform-norms}\\\\\n \\left\\|A_sf\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q}&\\leq CL|\\ell_s(f)|+a\\left\\|f\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q},\n \\label{eq:transfer-stable}\\\\\n |\\ell_{Ls}(A_sf)-L\\ell_s(f)|&\\leq a\\left\\|f\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q}.\n \\label{eq:transfer-compatible}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ is independent of $a,L,s$. The choices are made in the following order: first $L$, then a larger fixed measurement ratio $H$, then the approximation accuracies used below, and finally $s_0$. In particular, all estimates hold simultaneously for all $s\\geq s_0$ and all bounded functions in $\\mathcal X_s$.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-window",
        "exact_quote": "Fix nested enlargements $F_0\\subset F_*$ of the support of a test function, with all three relevant gaps positive: from the support to $\\partial F_0$, from $\\partial F_0$ to $\\partial F_*$, and from $F_*$ to $D$. For each $t>0$ there is a finite menu of radial windows, each of positive rescaled width, such that one window $[F_-,C]$ satisfies $$\\sup_{F_-\\subset F\\subset C}\\sup_h\n       \\|P_Fh-P_Ch\\|_{L^2(\\nu)}\\leq t.\n \\label{eq:transfer-window}$$ The second supremum is over all the likelihoods in <a href=\"#eq:transfer-layer-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:transfer-layer-density\">[eq:transfer-layer-density]</a>.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-smoothing",
        "exact_quote": "Given $b>0$, the window tolerance in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:transfer-window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:transfer-window\">17</a> can be chosen so that, for a sufficiently large fixed $M$ and then all sufficiently large $s$, $$\\sup_h\\|(I-T_M)P_Ch\\|_2<b.\n \\label{eq:transfer-smoothing}$$ All constants are uniform over the likelihood family and the finite menu of possible windows.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-replacement",
        "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many pairs of positively separated supports, each a square or a square band, with buffered enlargements that remain disjoint. For every $t>0$, each lattice function $v$ of norm at most one on any one support can be replaced, for its full-plane correlations against all functions of norm at most one on the paired supports, with error at most $t$, by a function of finitely many smeared spin sums in its enlargement. The replacement has norm at most one. The number of sums, their shapes, and a modulus of continuity for the replacing functions belong to a fixed finite menu, independent of $v$ and of all sufficiently fine meshes. Along any mesh sequence, further subsequences of these replacements converge to bounded continuous tests of finitely many continuum smeared spins. Replacements on both sides of each gap can be made together, and preserve spin-flip and square symmetries by averaging.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many pairs of positively separated supports, each a square or a square band, with buffered enlargements that remain disjoint. The replacement has norm at most one. The number of sums, their shapes, and a modulus of continuity for the replacing functions belong to a fixed finite menu, independent of $v$ and of all sufficiently fine meshes. Along any mesh sequence, further subsequences of these replacements converge to bounded continuous tests of finitely many continuum smeared spins. Replacements on both sides of each gap can be made together, and preserve spin-flip and square symmetries by averaging.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-charges",
        "exact_quote": "Let $z_1,\\ldots,z_{2m}$ be distinct complex points, with $m\\geq1$, and put $$W_z(u)=\\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_i u_j/2},\\qquad\n A_q(z)=\\sum_{\\substack{u_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}\\\\\\sum_i u_i=q}}W_z(u),$$ where an empty charge class has sum zero. Define $$A_0^d(z)=\\sum_{\\sum_i u_i=0}W_z(u)\n                        \\left|\\sum_i u_i z_i\\right|^2.$$ Then $$A_0^dA_0=4A_2^2.\n \\label{eq:transfer-charge-identity}$$ If all points lie in the unit disk, then $A_2/A_0\\leq1$, and all ratios $A_q/A_0$ are bounded by one.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-charges",
          "exact_quote": "Let $z_1,\\ldots,z_{2m}$ be distinct complex points, with $m\\geq1$, and put $$W_z(u)=\\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_i u_j/2},\\qquad\n A_q(z)=\\sum_{\\substack{u_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}\\\\\\sum_i u_i=q}}W_z(u),$$ where an empty charge class has sum zero. Define $$A_0^d(z)=\\sum_{\\sum_i u_i=0}W_z(u)\n                        \\left|\\sum_i u_i z_i\\right|^2.$$ Then $$A_0^dA_0=4A_2^2.\n \\label{eq:transfer-charge-identity}$$ If all points lie in the unit disk, then $A_2/A_0\\leq1$, and all ratios $A_q/A_0$ are bounded by one.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $z_1,\\ldots,z_{2m}$ be distinct complex points, with $m\\geq1$, and put $$W_z(u)=\\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_i u_j/2},\\qquad\n A_q(z)=\\sum_{\\substack{u_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}\\\\\\sum_i u_i=q}}W_z(u),$$ where an empty charge class has sum zero.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-radial-gap",
        "exact_quote": "On the subspace of $\\mathcal H$ generated by even, quarter-turn invariant spin tests, the spectrum of $\\mathcal D$ below $3$ consists of the constant state at $0$ and one nonzero one-dimensional state at $1$. There is a real linear functional $\\ell$ with $|\\ell(F)|\\leq\\|F\\|_2$ such that, for real even quarter-turn invariant tests $F,G$ of finite ordinary $L^2$ norm and $0<r\\leq1$, $$\\left|\\mathbb E[(\\Theta F)D_{-\\log r}G]\n       -\\mathbb EF\\mathbb EG-r\\ell(F)\\ell(G)\\right|\n \\leq r^3\\|F\\|_2\\|G\\|_2.\n \\label{eq:transfer-radial-gap}$$ The assertion extends from smeared polynomials to bounded field tests by ordinary $L^2$ approximation.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-radial-gap",
          "exact_quote": "On the subspace of $\\mathcal H$ generated by even, quarter-turn invariant spin tests, the spectrum of $\\mathcal D$ below $3$ consists of the constant state at $0$ and one nonzero one-dimensional state at $1$. There is a real linear functional $\\ell$ with $|\\ell(F)|\\leq\\|F\\|_2$ such that, for real even quarter-turn invariant tests $F,G$ of finite ordinary $L^2$ norm and $0<r\\leq1$, $$\\left|\\mathbb E[(\\Theta F)D_{-\\log r}G]\n       -\\mathbb EF\\mathbb EG-r\\ell(F)\\ell(G)\\right|\n \\leq r^3\\|F\\|_2\\|G\\|_2.\n \\label{eq:transfer-radial-gap}$$ The assertion extends from smeared polynomials to bounded field tests by ordinary $L^2$ approximation.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "On the subspace of $\\mathcal H$ generated by even, quarter-turn invariant spin tests, the spectrum of $\\mathcal D$ below $3$ consists of the constant state at $0$ and one nonzero one-dimensional state at $1$.
