    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-reverse",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_t$ be a differentiable family of strictly positive probability laws on a finite spin space, and let $I_x$ be specified update sets. Let $f_x$ be fixed bounded real functions, with $\\|f_x\\|_\\infty\\le a$, $a\\ge0$, and let $P_{t,x}$ denote conditional expectation under $\\mu_t$ given the complement of $I_x$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative}\n \\dot\\mu_t(\\sigma)=\\mu_t(\\sigma)c\n \\sum_x\\bigl(P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)-f_x(\\sigma)\\bigr),\n \\qquad c\\ge0.$$ If $a>0$, define the refresh law $$\\label{eq:flow-refresh-law}\n Q_{t,x}(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n =\\mu_t(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n \\left[1+\\frac{f_x(\\eta_{I_x}\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n                  -P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)}{2a}\\right].$$ It is a probability law. Updating set $I_x$ from this law at rate $2ac$, independently for each $x$, has generator $\\mathcal L_t$ with $\\mathcal L_t^*\\mu_t=-\\dot\\mu_t$. Thus using $\\mathcal L_{1-t}$ from time zero to one carries the law at time one exactly to the law at time zero. If $a=0$, the derivative in <a href=\"#eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\">[eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative]</a> vanishes and no refresh is needed.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-reverse",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_t$ be a differentiable family of strictly positive probability laws on a finite spin space, and let $I_x$ be specified update sets. Let $f_x$ be fixed bounded real functions, with $\\|f_x\\|_\\infty\\le a$, $a\\ge0$, and let $P_{t,x}$ denote conditional expectation under $\\mu_t$ given the complement of $I_x$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative}\n \\dot\\mu_t(\\sigma)=\\mu_t(\\sigma)c\n \\sum_x\\bigl(P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)-f_x(\\sigma)\\bigr),\n \\qquad c\\ge0.$$ If $a>0$, define the refresh law $$\\label{eq:flow-refresh-law}\n Q_{t,x}(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n =\\mu_t(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n \\left[1+\\frac{f_x(\\eta_{I_x}\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n                  -P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)}{2a}\\right].$$ It is a probability law. Updating set $I_x$ from this law at rate $2ac$, independently for each $x$, has generator $\\mathcal L_t$ with $\\mathcal L_t^*\\mu_t=-\\dot\\mu_t$. Thus using $\\mathcal L_{1-t}$ from time zero to one carries the law at time one exactly to the law at time zero. If $a=0$, the derivative in <a href=\"#eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\">[eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative]</a> vanishes and no refresh is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $\\mu_t$ be a differentiable family of strictly positive probability laws on a finite spin space, and let $I_x$ be specified update sets. Let $f_x$ be fixed bounded real functions, with $\\|f_x\\|_\\infty\\le a$, $a\\ge0$, and let $P_{t,x}$ denote conditional expectation under $\\mu_t$ given the complement of $I_x$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative}\n \\dot\\mu_t(\\sigma)=\\mu_t(\\sigma)c\n \\sum_x\\bigl(P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)-f_x(\\sigma)\\bigr),\n \\qquad c\\ge0.$$ If $a>0$, define the refresh law $$\\label{eq:flow-refresh-law}\n Q_{t,x}(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n =\\mu_t(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n \\left[1+\\frac{f_x(\\eta_{I_x}\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n                  -P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)}{2a}\\right].$$ It is a probability law. Updating set $I_x$ from this law at rate $2ac$, independently for each $x$, has generator $\\mathcal L_t$ with $\\mathcal L_t^*\\mu_t=-\\dot\\mu_t$. Thus using $\\mathcal L_{1-t}$ from time zero to one carries the law at time one exactly to the law at time zero. If $a=0$, the derivative in <a href=\"#eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\">[eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative]</a> vanishes and no refresh is needed.",

        "exact_quote": "For every integer $L\\ge9$, there is a linear relabeling map $C_s:\\mathcal A_{s,d/2}\\to\\mathcal A_{Ls,d}$ that leaves the local functions and their roots unchanged and satisfies $$\\|C_sW\\|_{Ls,d}\\le\\|W\\|_{s,d/2}.$$ If a declared union is contained in $B_{Ls}$, it is relabeled as a single square.         "anchor": "stmt-thm-critical-trajectory",
        "exact_quote": "For every $J>0$ and finite-range real potential $U$ satisfying the symmetries of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, the preceding scale parameters can be fixed independently of all domains and boundary data. For each sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ there is $\\lambda_*>0$ and a real analytic function $\\beta_c:(-\\lambda_*,\\lambda_*)\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\beta_c(0)=\\beta_0$, for which the initial potential <a href=\"#eq:flow-physical-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-physical-input\">[eq:flow-physical-input]</a> has a trajectory $V_{k+1}=\\Phi_{s_k}(V_k)$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:flow-critical-decay}\n \\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}\\le\\epsilon2^{-k},\\qquad k\\ge0.$$ Its intermediate one-step trajectories obey $$\\label{eq:flow-critical-intermediate}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le1}\n \\sum_\\ell |\\ell|e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\n \\|v_{k,\\ell}(t)\\|_\\infty\\le C\\epsilon2^{-k}.$$ The same construction applies to both signs of $\\lambda$.",

        "exact_quote": "Suppose $Ls\\le N/10$, and suppose a real one-step trajectory obeys $$\\sup_{0\\le t\\le1}\\sum_\\ell |\\ell|\n e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\\|v_\\ell(t)\\|_\\infty\\le r_s,\n \\qquad \\|f\\|_\\infty\\le r_s,$$ with $r_s$ in the fixed small ball. There is a signed measure $e_t$, of total mass zero, such that $$\\label{eq:flow-finite-residual}\n \\dot\\mu_t=\\mu_t s^{-2}\\sum_{x\\in\\mathbb T_N}\n       (P_{t,x}^{N}f_x-f_x)+e_t,\n \\qquad\n \\|e_t\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\\le C r_s(N/s)^2e^{-cN/s}.$$ The constants $C,c>0$ depend on the fixed flow parameters, but not on $s,N,t$.",

      "exact_statement": "Suppose $Ls\\le N/10$, and suppose a real one-step trajectory obeys $$\\sup_{0\\le t\\le1}\\sum_\\ell |\\ell|\n e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\\|v_\\ell(t)\\|_\\infty\\le r_s,\n \\qquad \\|f\\|_\\infty\\le r_s,$$ with $r_s$ in the fixed small ball. Let $\\mu_t=\\mu_{s,N}^{V(t)}$, and let $P_{t,x}^{N}$ be its exact torus conditional expectation over $B_{Ls}^\\circ(x)$. There is a signed measure $e_t$, of total mass zero, such that $$\\label{eq:flow-finite-residual}\n \\dot\\mu_t=\\mu_t s^{-2}\\sum_{x\\in\\mathbb T_N}\n       (P_{t,x}^{N}f_x-f_x)+e_t,\n \\qquad\n \\|e_t\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\\le C r_s(N/s)^2e^{-cN/s}.$$ The constants $C,c>0$ depend on the fixed flow parameters, but not on $s,N,t$.",
      "explanation": "Periodic evaluation of the plane flow differs from the exact torus probability equation by a centered signed residual. Exponentially weighted long lists control its total variation.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-edit-coupling",
        "exact_quote": "Let the physical torus law $\\mu_{\\lambda,N}$ be at the temperature of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:critical-trajectory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:critical-trajectory\">26</a>, with trajectory bound <a href=\"#eq:flow-critical-decay\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-critical-decay\">[eq:flow-critical-decay]</a>. Let $$K_N=\\max\\{k\\ge0:Ls_k\\le N/10\\}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. There is a coupling of $\\sigma^{(0)}\\sim\\mu_{0,N}$, $\\sigma^{(\\lambda)}\\sim\\mu_{\\lambda,N}$, and Bernoulli marks $M_{k,x}$, $0\\le k\\le K_N$, $x\\in\\mathbb T_N$, with the following properties. The marks are mutually independent and independent of $\\sigma^{(0)}$, and $$\\label{eq:flow-mark-probabilities}\n \\mathbb P(M_{k,x}=1)\\le C\\epsilon2^{-k}s_k^{-2}.$$ Except on an event of probability $o(1)$ as $N\\to\\infty$, $\\sigma^{(\\lambda)}$ can be obtained from $\\sigma^{(0)}$ by changing spins only inside the union of the marked squares $B_{Ls_k}(x)$. The values of the changed spins may depend on all previous refreshes. The constant and the exceptional probability are independent of test domains and exterior configurations. Enlarging these squares by any fixed number of lattice steps preserves the conclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-edit-coupling",

Let $$K_N=\\max\\{k\\ge0:Ls_k\\le N/10\\}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. There is a coupling of $\\sigma^{(0)}\\sim\\mu_{0,N}$, $\\sigma^{(\\lambda)}\\sim\\mu_{\\lambda,N}$, and Bernoulli marks $M_{k,x}$, $0\\le k\\le K_N$, $x\\in\\mathbb T_N$, with the following properties. The marks are mutually independent and independent of $\\sigma^{(0)}$, and $$\\label{eq:flow-mark-probabilities}\n \\mathbb P(M_{k,x}=1)\\le C\\epsilon2^{-k}s_k^{-2}.$$ Except on an event of probability $o(1)$ as $N\\to\\infty$, $\\sigma^{(\\lambda)}$ can be obtained from $\\sigma^{(0)}$ by changing spins only inside the union of the marked squares $B_{Ls_k}(x)$.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-bulk-stability",
        "exact_quote": "After increasing $s_0$ if necessary, there is $\\epsilon_*>0$, depending only on the fixed constants in Proposition <a href=\"#prop:edit-coupling\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:edit-coupling\">28</a>, with the following property. Fix any finite family $\\mathcal T$ of polygonal corridor crossings, polygonal-band circuits, and four-passage square annulus tests. Let $N=N(\\delta)$ be integers with $\\delta\\downarrow0$ and $\\inf_\\delta\\delta N(\\delta)>T_0(\\mathcal T)$, where $T_0(\\mathcal T)$ is sufficiently large that all tests and their fixed collars lie in one planar chart of the physical torus. If $\\epsilon\\leq\\epsilon_*$ and the marks satisfy <a href=\"#eq:stability-mark-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stability-mark-law\">[eq:stability-mark-law]</a>, then $$\\mathbb P\\bigl(D_T(\\sigma^0,M)=1\\text{ for some }T\\in\\mathcal T\\bigr)\n \\longrightarrow0\n \\quad\\text{as }\\delta\\downarrow0.\n \\label{eq:stability-main}$$ The threshold $\\epsilon_*$ is independent of the family, its polygonal complexity, and all its fixed positive widths. The mesh threshold and the constants in the convergence bound may depend on that geometry.*\n\n*Consequently the pure and perturbed torus laws at the trajectory of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:critical-trajectory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:critical-trajectory\">26</a> have asymptotically identical joint laws for every such finite family. In particular, pure positive lower bounds for any fixed finite compatible system of signed buffered corridor and circuit events, and pure upper limits for four-passage events, hold for the perturbed law as well.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability-pivotal",
        "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a fixed polygonal crossing, circuit, or one of the three annular tests in <a href=\"#eq:stability-checks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stability-checks\">[eq:stability-checks]</a>. Suppose that two spin configurations agree off a square of radius $b$ but give different values to $T$. Enlarge this square by a fixed factor to contain all contour segments affected by the change. There is a positive geometry scale $a_T$ with the following properties. Set $$u=\\min\\{a_T,\\max\\{b,\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(z,\\mathcal E_T)\\}\\},\\qquad\n v=\\min\\{a_T,\\max\\{b,\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(z,\\mathcal V_T)\\}\\},$$ where $\\mathcal E_T$ and $\\mathcal V_T$ are the finite collections of sides and vertices of the test polygons, and $z$ is the edit center. For $H_2$, these collections also include the confinement line segments and their intersections with the two square boundaries. For $b<a_T$, the common contours outside the enlarged edit square must supply the following checks, after fixed crops:*\n\n1.  *four passages on rings between scales $b$ and $u$;*\n\n2.  *two passages confined to an appropriate half-plane on rings between scales $u$ and $v$;*\n\n3.  *one passage on rings between scales $v$ and $a_T$.*\n\n*Rings within a fixed multiplicative distance of transition scales may be omitted. The half-plane checks may be centered on the nearby side and the last checks near the nearby vertex. For a fixed test only a bounded number of further rings need be omitted. The constants are uniform over translations, rotations, and dilations of each of the three fixed annular shapes.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

For $H_2$, these collections also include the confinement line segments and their intersections with the two square boundaries. For $b<a_T$, the common contours outside the enlarged edit square must supply the following checks, after fixed crops:*\n\n1.  *four passages on rings between scales $b$ and $u$;*\n\n2.  *two passages confined to an appropriate half-plane on rings between scales $u$ and $v$;*\n\n3.  *one passage on rings between scales $v$ and $a_T$.*\n\n*Rings within a fixed multiplicative distance of transition scales may be omitted.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability-counts",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a compact region $K$ and a length $a>0$, in a bulk chart containing a fixed $20a$-neighborhood of $K$. There is a random finite list $\\mathcal L_\\delta$ of compact contour parameter-subarcs, chosen without reference to a test center or direction, with $\\sup_\\delta\\mathbb E|\\mathcal L_\\delta|<\\infty$ for sufficiently fine meshes. The list covers the following visits, with their contour multiplicities. If a contour visits $x\\in K$ and reaches distance $10a$ from $x$ in both parameter directions, a member of the list contains that visit and lies between the first such distant visit in each direction. Subarcs are retained only in a fixed enlargement of $K$. The same assertion holds for the limiting complete loop collection, with a list of finite expected cardinality. Fixed enlargements of the chart and fixed crops of the excursion lengths only change the bound.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

        "exact_quote": "Fix a bulk region and a positive excursion length. For the pure contours at mesh $\\delta$, let $G_K(z,\\theta,u)$ be the event that a contour subarc comes within $C u$ of $z$, travels the fixed excursion length on both sides of this visit, and throughout those two portions stays above the line of direction $\\theta$ through $z$ lowered by $CKu$. For each fixed $K$, $$\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\mathbb P_0(G_K(z,\\theta,u))=o_K(u),\n \\label{eq:stability-tangency}$$ as $u\\downarrow0$ with $K$ fixed, uniformly over centers and directions in a smaller bulk chart. Fixed crops or fixed changes of the constants have the same conclusion.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"

For each fixed $K$, $$\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\\mathbb P_0(G_K(z,\\theta,u))=o_K(u),\n \\label{eq:stability-tangency}$$ as $u\\downarrow0$ with $K$ fixed, uniformly over centers and directions in a smaller bulk chart.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability-products",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed constant $D$ with the following property. More precisely, after absorbing rotations and lattice margins, each check has a collar $W_j$ of the form $$S_{2R_j}(z_j,\\theta_j)\\setminus\n       S_{r_j/2}(z_j,\\theta_j),$$ the other check supports lie outside $W_j$, and the sets $\\mathcal J_j$ are disjoint. All collars lie in bulk torus charts.         "exact_quote": "There is $\\epsilon_*>0$, chosen from the three fixed annular shapes and the fixed constants of the mark law, such that the following holds on every sufficiently fine finite torus.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-bit-representation",
        "exact_quote": "There is a finite choice of $M$ such that, for all sufficiently small $\\lvert\\lambda\\rvert$, positive functions $$W_v\\bigl(\\sigma_v,b_{\\mathcal B(v)}\\bigr)$$ represent the spin weight in <a href=\"#eq:comparison-local-factors\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:comparison-local-factors\">[eq:comparison-local-factors]</a> exactly, up to a constant, as the spin marginal of $\\prod_v W_v$. Here $\\mathcal B(v)$ is the set of the $4M$ bits on bonds incident to $v$. The same functions are used whether a physical spin is free or pinned. There are only finitely many local tables, and uniformly in their entries $$W_v(\\sigma,b)\\longrightarrow\n W_v^0(\\sigma,b):=\\exp\\left(a\\sigma\\sum_{i\\in\\mathcal B(v)}b_i\\right)\n \\quad\\text{as }\\lambda\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:comparison-table-limit}$$ The representation applies to either sign of every sufficiently small local perturbation.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison-margins",
        "exact_quote": "After decreasing the allowed interval of $\\lambda$, every free factor $F_v$ is strictly log-supermodular in each pair of distinct incident bits, and every kernel $k_v$ is strictly increasing in each incident bit.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-strict-incidence",
        "exact_quote": "Consider two laws formed from the same vertex factors $W_v$, with possibly different sets of pinned physical spins. Label the laws lower and upper. Let $V$ be a union of nearest-neighbor connected components of their common free vertex set. At each vertex outside $V$ adjacent to $V$, require one of the following possibilities:*\n\n<div class=\"center\">\n\n|  *lower law*   |  *upper law*  |\n|:--------------:|:-------------:|\n|     *free*     | *pinned plus* |\n| *pinned minus* |    *free*     |\n| *pinned minus* | *pinned plus* |\n\n</div>\n\n*Then there is a coupling in which all bits on bonds touching $V$, and all physical spins in $V$, are lower than or equal to their upper-law counterparts. The assertion remains valid if arbitrary values of bits on bonds not touching $V$ have been conditioned on separately in the two laws. It consequently remains valid for arbitrary mixtures of these exterior bit conditions.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison-stopped-reveal",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose spins are queried one at a time, each next query and the stopping decision being determined by the preceding queries and their answers. Conditional on a complete transcript, the remaining law is exactly the original law with the revealed spins pinned. This remains true after conditioning on any event determined by the transcript.*\n\n*In a finite triangulated disk with a prescribed inner disk, one can explore from its outer boundary for a shield of either color $s$. On success, no inner-disk spin has been queried, and every queried neighbor of the remaining component containing that disk has color $s$. A color-$s$ circuit in a narrower annular band separating the two disks guarantees success. All queried sites are on the exterior side of that circuit or on the circuit itself.*\n\n*The corresponding exploration in a triangulated topological rectangle, from one side toward the opposite side, determines whether there is a color-$s$ crossing between the other two sides. On success its facing boundary contains such a crossing, and the exploration queries no spins strictly beyond it toward the opposite side. If that crossing extends past the ends of a comparison region, every nearest-neighbor incidence from the region across the explored side meets a pin of color $s$.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-comparison-rectangles",
        "exact_quote": "Let $p(i,j)>0$ be an MTP$_2$ probability mass on two disjoint finite sets of binary coordinates $I,J$. Let $i_-,i_+,j_-,j_+$ denote their constant minus and plus configurations, set $f=\\log p$, and put $$\\Lambda=f(i_+,j_+)+f(i_-,j_-)\n              -f(i_+,j_-)-f(i_-,j_+)\\ge0.\n \\label{eq:comparison-extreme-ratio}$$ Then, for arbitrary configurations $a,a'$ on $I$ and $b,b'$ on $J$, $$\\bigl|f(a,b)+f(a',b')-f(a,b')-f(a',b)\\bigr|\\le\\Lambda.\n \\label{eq:comparison-all-rectangles}$$ Consequently every two conditional laws of $I$ given $J$, and every two mixtures of those conditional laws, have pointwise density ratio between $e^{-\\Lambda}$ and $e^{\\Lambda}$.",

      },

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-interior-density",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a square $Q$ and a larger square $\\widetilde Q$, with $\\overline Q\\subset\\operatorname{int}\\widetilde Q$, in a fixed physical torus chart. Take lattice mesh $\\delta\\downarrow0$, with torus side $N$ chosen so that this chart has a fixed macroscopic buffer from its translates. Suppose every lattice vertex in $\\widetilde Q$ is free in a domain spin law $\\nu_{\\Omega,\\lambda}^{\\tau}$. For all sufficiently small mesh there is a constant $C<\\infty$, depending on the two fixed squares and the chart but not on $\\Omega,\\tau$, or the mesh, such that $$C^{-1}\\le\n \\frac{\\nu_{\\Omega,\\lambda}^{\\tau}(\\sigma_{Q\\cap\\delta\\mathbb Z^2}=\\eta)}\n      {\\mu_{\\lambda,N}(\\sigma_{Q\\cap\\delta\\mathbb Z^2}=\\eta)}\n \\le C\n \\quad\\text{for every spin configuration }\\eta.\n \\label{eq:comparison-interior-density}$$ The same bounds hold for every event determined by these spins. The exterior pins may be arbitrary, including those induced by any admissible exterior contour configuration.",

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-boundary-squeeze",
        "exact_quote": "Fix the domain, signs, a displacement $h$, the preceding polygonal placements, and a finite list $S_1,\\ldots,S_m$ of allowed plus strips. Let $V_A\\in\\{0,1\\}^m$ record their plus crossings in the pure Dobrushin law in an ordinary mesh approximation of $A_h$. Let $V_n^+$ record plus crossings in the target, filling the portions of the strips outside $D_n$ virtually with plus. There is a coupling with $$\\mathbb P(V_A\\leq V_n^+)=1-o(1) \\qquad(n\\longrightarrow\\infty),\n \\label{eq:curves-vector-order}$$ where the inequality holds coordinatewise. The error is uniform over all admissible target exterior configurations. The sign-reversed construction gives the corresponding transfer of minus crossings from pure domains favoring plus. The smallness condition on $\\lambda$ is the one fixed at the beginning of the section; only the mesh threshold and the error bound depend on these fixed geometries.",

        "anchor": "stmt-lem-ordered-chords",
        "exact_quote": "Let $L,U$ be random simple chords from $a$ to $b$ in a Jordan domain, meeting its boundary only at their endpoints, and let $K$ be a random compact connected subset of its closure containing $a,b$. Suppose <a href=\"#eq:curves-forbidden-sides\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:curves-forbidden-sides\">[eq:curves-forbidden-sides]</a> holds almost surely.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-curve-upgrade",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma:[0,1]\\to\\overline D$ be continuous and injective, with $\\Gamma(0)=a$, $\\Gamma(1)=b$, and $\\Gamma((0,1))\\subset D$. Let $g_n:[0,1]\\to\\mathbb C$ be continuous simple curves whose endpoints tend to $a,b$ and whose traces tend in Hausdorff distance to $\\Gamma([0,1])$. There are continuous functions $u_n:[0,1]\\to[0,1]$ with endpoint values tending to $0,1$ such that $$e_n:=\\sup_t|g_n(t)-\\Gamma(u_n(t))|\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:curves-coordinate-error}$$ Put $B_n=\\sup_{s<t}(u_n(s)-u_n(t))$. If $B_n\\to0$, then $d_{\\rm curv}(g_n,\\Gamma)\\to0$. Conversely, for every fixed $0<\\eta<1$, a backward drop $B_n\\geq\\eta$, with $n$ sufficiently large, forces six disjoint passages from radius $r$ to one fixed radius $R_\\eta>0$, for every $e_n<r<R_\\eta$, about a center on $\\Gamma([\\eta/2,1-\\eta/2])\\Subset D$.*\n\n*For random curves, suppose the trace and endpoint convergence hold in probability in a coupling with such a random $\\Gamma$, and suppose <a href=\"#eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck\">[eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck]</a> holds for every deterministic compact interior $F$ and sufficiently small fixed $R$. Then $d_{\\rm curv}(g_n,\\Gamma)\\to0$ in probability in that coupling.",
        "source_id": "PM-218.5"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-curve-upgrade",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma:[0,1]\\to\\overline D$ be continuous and injective, with $\\Gamma(0)=a$, $\\Gamma(1)=b$, and $\\Gamma((0,1))\\subset D$. There are continuous functions $u_n:[0,1]\\to[0,1]$ with endpoint values tending to $0,1$ such that $$e_n:=\\sup_t|g_n(t)-\\Gamma(u_n(t))|\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:curves-coordinate-error}$$ Put $B_n=\\sup_{s<t}(u_n(s)-u_n(t))$. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **finite-range square-symmetric contour potential** ([source](#pm-h-3)): A potential $U$ assigns a real number to each finite subset of $\mathcal E$, with $U(\varnothing)=0$. We assume that $U$ is invariant under lattice translations and the rotations and reflections of the square lattice. Its range is finite: there is $R<\infty$ such that $$U(S)=0 \quad\text{if the Euclidean diameter of the union of the edges in \(S\) exceeds \(R\).}$$
- **permitted interior contours** ([source](#pm-h-3)): The permitted interior contours are $$\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi = \left\{P\subset E_\Omega: d_f(P)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm in}\}}(f)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm out}\}}(f) \text{ is even for every }f\in\mathcal F\right\}.$$
- **admissible exterior contour** ([source](#pm-h-3)): An admissible exterior contour is a finite set $P_o\subset\mathcal E\setminus E_\Omega$ containing the two stubs and satisfying $$d_f(P_o)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm in}\}}(f)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm out}\}}(f) \equiv0\pmod2 \qquad(f\in\mathcal F).$$
- **oriented uniform curve metric** ([source](#eq:curve-metric)): An oriented curve is a continuous map from $[0,1]$ into the plane, considered modulo increasing reparametrization and zero distance for $$\label{eq:curve-metric} d_{\rm curv}(\gamma,\eta) = \inf_{\alpha,\rho} \sup_{t\in[0,1]} \left\|\gamma(\alpha(t))-\eta(\rho(t))\right\|,$$ where $\alpha,\rho$ range over increasing homeomorphisms of $[0,1]$.
- **pin** ([source](#sec:pure)): A *pin* is a prescribed spin.
- **free lattice chart** ([source](#sec:pure)): A free lattice chart is a region isometric to part of the square lattice in which no spins have been pinned.
- **order-positive centered density** ([source](#stmt-def-pure-order-positive)): For a probability measure $\mu_S$ on spins in a finite set $S$, a real function $q$ is an order-positive centered density if $$\mathbb E_{\mu_S}q=0,\qquad \mathbb E_{\mu_S}[qg]\ge0 \quad\hbox{for every increasing }g.$$ Here density means the density of a signed measure.
- **strong and weak spin paths** ([source](#pm-h-12)): A *strong* spin path uses nearest-neighbor edges. A *weak* spin path may also join vertices of the same lattice face.
- **even square-invariant local-function quotient** ([source](#eq:transfer-operator)): Let $\mathcal X_s$ be the real functions of $\sigma_{B_s}$ invariant under spin reversal and the dihedral group of the square, modulo constant functions.
- **radial Hilbert space** ([source](#eq:transfer-radial-l2)): Let $\mathcal H$ be its completion after quotienting out the null space.
- **weighted labeled-potential space** ([source](#pm-h-25)): For $b>0$, let $\mathcal A_{s,b}$ be the space of families $V=(v_\ell)_\ell$ of these centered coefficients with $$\label{eq:flow-list-norm} \\|V\\|_{s,b}:=\sum_\ell e^{b(\|\ell\|-1)}\\|v_\ell\\|_\infty<\infty.$$
- **polygonal corridor** ([source](#pm-h-31)): A *polygonal corridor* is a closed topological rectangle with piecewise linear boundary and two disjoint opposite sides designated as its gates.
- **polygonal band** ([source](#pm-h-31)): A *polygonal band* is the closed region between two disjoint simple polygons, and its circuit test asks for a path of a specified color separating the two boundary components.
- **contour passage and multiplicity** ([source](#pm-h-31)): A passage between two disjoint terminal sets is a contour subarc whose endpoints lie on the two sets and whose interior lies in the permitted region between them. Several passages must have disjoint open parameter intervals on every contour. Their endpoints may coincide at terminal hits, but a subarc of positive length cannot be used twice.
- **auxiliary bond bit** ([source](#pm-h-38)): A variable placed on a bond will be called a *bit*, to distinguish it from a physical spin at a lattice vertex.
- **log-supermodular binary mass** ([source](#pm-h-39)): The function is *log-supermodular* if every such increment is nondecreasing in every other coordinate.
- **plus and minus sides of a simple chord** ([source](#pm-h-47)): For a simple chord $C$ of $D$ from $a$ to $b$, with interior in $D$, let $P(C)$ and $M(C)$ be the open components of $D\setminus C$ adjacent to $I_+$ and $I_-$, respectively.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-unperturbed-critical-inverse-temperature | Unperturbed critical inverse temperature | = | $\frac{\log(1+\sqrt2)}{2J}$ | Zero perturbation coupling. |
| q-pure-critical-bond-coupling | Pure critical nearest-neighbor coupling | = | $\frac12\log(1+\sqrt2)$ | The fixed reference coupling. |
| q-spin-two-point-correlation | Continuum two-point spin correlation | = | $\|z-w\|^{-1/4}$ | Distinct continuum insertion points. |
| q-line-magnetization-continuity-error | Mesh upper limit of the normalized nearby-line difference norm | ≤ | $C_0h^{3/8}$ | Fix the displacement ratio before taking the lattice scale to infinity. |
| q-signed-transmission-exponent-upper-threshold | Positive signed-transmission exponent parameter | < | $1/8$ | The exponent is fixed before the cut ratio and mesh threshold. |
| q-positive-energy-covariance | Long-distance covariance of centered bond energies | = | $\frac{1+o(1)}{\pi^2\|x\|^2}$ | The displacement tends to infinity; both bond orientations are allowed. |
| q-four-passage-exponent-ceiling | Admissible four-passage upper-bound exponent | < | $21/8$ | The annulus ratio is fixed before the mesh limit. |
| q-four-passage-probability | Mesh upper limit of the annular four-passage probability | ≤ | $C_\alpha u^\alpha$ | The mesh limit precedes shrinking the fixed radius ratio. |
| q-radial-correlation-remainder | Radial bilinear remainder after removing the constant and thermal states | ≤ | $r^3\\|F\\|_2\\|G\\|_2$ | Finite ordinary norms and the stated inward radius ratio. |
| q-finite-refresh-rate | Independent refresh rate for each update set | = | $2ac$ | The positive uniform function bound and nonnegative derivative coefficient. |
| q-analytic-flow-quadratic-remainder | Conditional-flow endpoint remainder norm | ≤ | $C\\|V\\|_{s,d}^2$ | A common small input ball, uniformly in the integer lattice scale. |
| q-multibox-carry-contraction | Compressed carry norm on lists with multiple boxes | ≤ | $L^2 e^{-d/2}\\|W\\|_{s,d}$ | The fixed scale factor and exponential list weight. |
| q-critical-interaction-decay | Effective potential norm along the constructed critical trajectory | ≤ | $\epsilon2^{-k}$ | All scale indices at the constructed inverse temperature. |
| q-periodization-total-variation-error | Total variation of the centered periodization residual | ≤ | $C r_s(N/s)^2e^{-cN/s}$ | Uniform over the one-step time interval and admissible lattice and torus scales. |
| q-independent-square-mark-probability | Bernoulli mark activation probability | ≤ | $C\epsilon2^{-k}s_k^{-2}$ | All retained scale indices and torus sites; the marks are independent of the pure configuration. |
| q-confined-half-plane-passage-decay | Mesh upper limit of the confined two-passage probability | = | $o(r/R)$ | Fix the radius ratio, take the mesh limit, and then shrink the ratio. |
| q-complex-charge-sum-identity | Product of the neutral dipole sum and neutral charge sum | = | $4A_2^2$ | The defined neutral and charge classes; the identity does not require the unit-disk restriction. |
| q-interior-density-upper-bound | Domain-to-torus interior spin density ratio | ≤ | $C$ | Fixed physical squares and a fixed free buffer, uniformly over exterior pins and sufficiently fine meshes. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-contour-spin** [claim](#stmt-lem-contour-spin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-contour-spin): Even intersection parity gives path-independent spin assignment; disagreement monomials identify the complete conditional weight. -> Exact spin-contour bijection and conditional Gibbs law.
- **step-pure-buffered** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-buffered) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-buffered): Use pure buffered likelihood comparisons and terminal symmetry of the density-odds estimate. -> Fixed-ratio and small-patch pointwise density bounds.
- **step-pure-annular** [claim](#stmt-cor-pure-annular) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-pure-annular): Change outer data and then inner data across separate collars; mix the reference exterior law. -> Annular density comparison independent of the annular aspect ratio.
- **step-signed-transmission** [claim](#stmt-prop-signed-transmission) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-signed-transmission): Normalize the positive and negative source parts, couple them in stochastic order, and average deterministic transmission. -> Signed transmission bound without dividing by a mesh-dependent source mass.
- **step-line-continuity** [claim](#stmt-prop-line-continuity) / [proof](#stmt-prop-line-continuity): Use the cited nearby-line estimate, continuum covariance integration, and buffered density comparison. -> Mesh-first norm continuity for normalized line sums.
- **step-line-prediction** [claim](#stmt-cor-line-prediction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-line-prediction): Use the nearby measurable line sum as a competitor for orthogonal projection. -> Conditional prediction error bound.
- **step-pure-spin** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-spin) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-spin): Combine published spin limits with nearest-insertion domination, diagonal removal, and convergent spatial Riemann sums. -> Explicit spin correlations, moment domination, and joint smeared moment limits.
- **step-reflection-positive** [claim](#stmt-lem-pure-reflection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-pure-reflection): Factor positive crossing-bond matrices, exhaust symmetric boxes, and pass to smeared moment limits. -> Lattice and continuum line reflection positivity.
- **step-energy-covariance** [claim](#stmt-prop-energy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-energy): Use the pure massless energy formula, approach exact criticality by continuity, and sum positive local coefficients. -> Positive leading thermal covariance under square symmetrization.
- **step-pure-sle** [claim](#stmt-thm-pure-sle) / [proof](#stmt-thm-pure-sle): Use the cited pure polygonal Dobrushin interface theorem with its allowed local-turn conventions. -> Pure oriented uniform interface convergence.
- **step-pure-cle** [claim](#stmt-thm-pure-cle) / [proof](#stmt-thm-pure-cle): Combine published extremal loop convergence with the companion complete-resolution argument for coverage and multiplicity. -> Uniform complete-resolution nested oriented loop convergence.
- **step-pure-four-passages** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-four-passages) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-four-passages): Pass resolved passages to simple limiting loops, crop disjoint side neighborhoods, and invoke the alternating strong-spin arm estimate. -> Mesh-first four-passage bound uniform in complete resolutions.
- **step-pure-rsw** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-rsw) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-rsw): Build buffered FK connections and isolating dual circuits, color isolated clusters, and iterate annular blockers. -> Signed simultaneous crossing positivity and positive one-passage decay.
- **step-likelihood-window** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-window): Bound the conditional kernel in Hilbert-Schmidt norm and select a small projection increment from a finite radial menu. -> One window valid simultaneously for all likelihoods.
- **step-likelihood-smoothing** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-smoothing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-smoothing): Gaussian conditioning preserves attraction; signed transmission and nearby-contour prediction force incompatible orthogonal increments if the residual remains large. -> Uniformly small Gaussian-smoothed likelihood residual.
- **step-bounded-replacement** [claim](#stmt-lem-bounded-replacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-bounded-replacement): Control overlap moments, project observations to fixed cell modes, and use equicontinuity and subsequence compactness. -> Uniform bounded continuum proxies for separated correlations.
- **step-complex-charge-identity** [claim](#stmt-lem-transfer-charges) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-transfer-charges): Convert charged weights to Vandermonde minors, use Cauchy–Binet and complementation, and compute a projection variance. -> Exact charged-sum identity and unit-disk charge-ratio bounds.
- **step-radial-gap** [claim](#stmt-prop-radial-gap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-radial-gap): Construct the reflection-positive dilation semigroup, expand charged correlations, cancel the scalar quadratic coefficient, and use positive spectral measures. -> Constant and thermal spectral projections with a uniform higher-degree remainder.
- **step-uniform-transfer** [claim](#stmt-thm-transfer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-radial-gap-2): Measure a common scalar coefficient with a fixed distant field test, replace arbitrary inner and boundary functions, and use the exact Markov tower identity. -> Uniform transfer norm and coefficient compatibility for every bounded local function.
- **step-reverse-generator** [claim](#stmt-lem-flow-reverse) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flow-reverse): Check normalization and nonnegativity of the tilted refresh table and compute its incoming-minus-outgoing mass. -> An exact reverse refresh generator with spin-independent rates.
- **step-analytic-flow** [claim](#stmt-lem-flow-analytic) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flow-analytic): Expand conditional numerator and reciprocal denominator in connected labeled lists; solve triangular truncations under a decreasing exponential-weight majorant. -> Analytic one-step solution and quadratic endpoint remainder.
- **step-list-compression** [claim](#stmt-lem-flow-compression) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flow-compression): Traverse a doubled spanning tree, retain separated centers, and compare old and compressed exponential list weights. -> Weight-restoring relabeling that preserves translation density.
- **step-decaying-graph** [claim](#stmt-lem-flow-stable-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flow-stable-graph): Build compatible growing vectors, triangularize the linear recurrence, and solve forward stable and backward expanding coordinates by contraction. -> Analytic graph of exactly the small decaying trajectories and its linear tangent characterization.
- **step-critical-temperature** [claim](#stmt-thm-critical-trajectory) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-critical-trajectory): Test iterated temperature derivatives against distant bond energy, prove transversality to the decaying graph, and apply the analytic implicit-function theorem. -> A real analytic domain-independent temperature branch with decaying interactions.
- **step-periodization-error** [claim](#stmt-lem-flow-periodization) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flow-periodization): Identify conditional Hamiltonians for short nonwrapping lists and bound all long-list differences by the exponential tail. -> Centered total-variation residual for periodic flow evaluation.
- **step-independent-edit-coupling** [claim](#stmt-prop-edit-coupling) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-edit-coupling): Couple the terminal law to the pure law, generate independent refresh clocks, reverse every flow step, and sum residuals by total-variation contraction. -> Pure-to-perturbed coupling through independent marked squares with vanishing exceptional probability.
- **step-pivotal-geometry** [claim](#stmt-lem-stability-pivotal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stability-pivotal): Cut common exterior contours and distinguish wall-free, single-wall, and vertex neighborhoods while preserving terminal-visit sequences. -> Required interior, half-plane, and vertex passage checks.
- **step-macroscopic-subarc-counts** [claim](#stmt-lem-stability-counts) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stability-counts): Explore alternating rectangle crossings with a uniform stopping probability, then subdivide macroscopic excursions independently of later test frames. -> A covering list of macroscopic parameter-subarcs with bounded expected size.
- **step-near-support-area** [claim](#stmt-lem-stability-tangency) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stability-tangency): Average centers and directions after passing to continuum loops, and bound contact areas using almost-everywhere unique convex-hull support points. -> Sublinear near-support contact probability.
- **step-half-plane-passages** [claim](#stmt-prop-pure-half-plane) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-pure-half-plane): Split confined passages into a near-support excursion or an additional four-passage event, then iterate the resulting contraction recursion. -> Confined two-passage probability smaller than the radius ratio.
- **step-ring-products** [claim](#stmt-lem-stability-products) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stability-products): Condition on original spins outside each collar and marks outside its local index set, apply pointwise annular comparison, and remove checks successively. -> Uniform product upper bound for possibility checks.
- **step-insertion-area-sum** [claim](#stmt-lem-stability-area) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-stability-area): Sort edit centers by truncated distances to sides and vertices, multiply area counts by mark and pivotal probabilities, and absorb dyadic multiplicity. -> Summable level contributions with a positive physical-radius power.
- **step-annular-bootstrap** [claim](#stmt-prop-stability-bootstrap) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stability-bootstrap): Continuously activate finite independent mark parameters and rule out a first monitored-check failure using the insertion-area sum. -> Universal monitored possibility bounds throughout activation.
- **step-bulk-stability** [claim](#stmt-thm-bulk-stability) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-stability-bootstrap-2): Interpolate discrepancy events through mark activation, use bootstrapped pivotal bounds, and apply dominated convergence over scales. -> Asymptotic agreement of every fixed finite vector of bulk tests.
- **step-private-bit-representation** [claim](#stmt-prop-bit-representation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-bit-representation): Assign private duplicate bit channels to tree spin patterns; centered differences annihilate every nonempty proper vertex selection. -> Exact positive vertex tables with the correct spin marginal.
- **step-finite-table-margins** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-margins) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-margins): Check strict finite-table gaps at the pure weight using convexity and preserve their minimum under uniform perturbation. -> Strict free-bit attraction and separated pin/free odds increments.
- **step-strict-incidence-coupling** [claim](#stmt-prop-strict-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-strict-incidence): Order single-bit heat-bath conditionals at each endpoint, take stationary coupled marginals, and sample common free spins through increasing kernels. -> Ordered bit and physical-spin coupling on common free components.
- **step-stopped-reveal-shields** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-stopped-reveal) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-stopped-reveal): Identify each adaptive transcript with a spin cylinder and explore opposite-color reachable vertices to expose monochromatic facing boundaries. -> Exact conditioning and geometric shields suitable for strict incidence comparisons.
- **step-extreme-rectangle-bound** [claim](#stmt-lem-comparison-rectangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-comparison-rectangles): Express the extreme-minus-arbitrary rectangle difference as a sum of nonnegative ordered differences; normalize conditional ratios. -> Uniform density comparison for arbitrary terminal conditions and mixtures.
- **step-interior-density** [claim](#stmt-prop-interior-density) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-interior-density): Cut every auxiliary-bit channel, use nested opposite-color shields to bound extreme cut odds, and pass the bit-density bound through the common spin kernel. -> Uniform target-to-torus interior spin density bounds across fixed free buffers.
- **step-boundary-squeeze** [claim](#stmt-prop-boundary-squeeze) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-boundary-squeeze): Construct displaced comparison domains and screening corridors, condition finite torus test vectors on their crossings, and glue the equality and strict-order couplings. -> Finite colored crossing vectors ordered from pure comparison domains to the target.
- **step-local-barrier-drawing** [claim](#stmt-lem-curves-local-barrier) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curves-local-barrier): Inspect boundary face configurations, fill only drawing values with the adjacent arc color, and glue zero-segment replacements with unchanged pairings. -> Exactly disjoint barriers with mesh-sized drawing changes and unchanged traversal order.
- **step-ordered-chord-identification** [claim](#stmt-lem-ordered-chords) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-ordered-chords): Use opposite-arc crosscuts to derive side inclusion; equal side-area laws force equality, and connectedness fills the common chord. -> Coincidence of both chords and the intervening compact trace.
- **step-progress-coordinate-upgrade** [claim](#stmt-lem-curve-upgrade) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-upgrade): Construct continuous progress coordinates, replace them by almost-matching running maxima, and show persistent backward drops force repeated interior passages. -> Convergence in the full oriented uniform curve metric.
- **step-main-universality** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-curve-upgrade-2): Fix the bulk perturbation interval, build deterministic finite strip covers and a slow diagonal, identify every trace limit between equal-law chords, and exclude backward travel with fixed-buffer interior passage bounds. -> The prescribed full oriented interface law converges to chordal SLE for every permitted Jordan approximation and exterior contour sequence.

          "anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-critical-trajectory",
          "exact_quote": "The scalar analytic implicit-function theorem applied to $G(V_0(\\beta,\\lambda))=0$ now gives a unique local real analytic branch $\\beta=\\beta_c(\\lambda)$ through $(\\beta_0,0)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "reason": "The analytic implicit-function theorem selects a local temperature branch; an explicit numerical calibration algorithm or convergence rate is not supplied.",
      "step_id": "step-critical-temperature"

  "pm_id": "PM-218.5",
  "schema": "pm.roadmap.v2",
  "steps": [
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-contour-spin",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-contour-spin",
          "exact_quote": "For the boundary data above, every even completion $P\\cup P_o$ determines a unique spin configuration that is plus sufficiently far away and has disagreement set $P\\cup P_o$. Its restriction $\\tau$ to $\\mathbb Z^2\\setminus\\Omega$ is independent of $P$. The map $P\\mapsto\\sigma|_\\Omega$ is a bijection between $\\mathcal P_\\Omega^\\xi$ and all spin assignments in $\\Omega$. Under this bijection, <a href=\"#eq:contour-law\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:contour-law\">[eq:contour-law]</a> is precisely the Gibbs conditional law for <a href=\"#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> with exterior spins $\\tau$. Denote this law by $\\nu_{\\Omega,\\beta,\\lambda}^{\\tau}$.*\n\n*The exterior nearest neighbors along each of the two boundary arcs between the marks have a constant sign, and the signs of the two arcs are opposite. The interaction in <a href=\"#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian\">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> is finite-range, invariant under global spin flip and the square symmetries, and has finitely many local interaction types up to translation.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-contour-spin",
      "inputs": [
        "Admissible interior and exterior contours",
        "Jordan lattice domain",
        "Finite-range contour potential"
      ],
      "method": "Even intersection parity gives path-independent spin assignment; disagreement monomials identify the complete conditional weight.",
      "output": "Exact spin-contour bijection and conditional Gibbs law.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-contour-spin"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-buffered",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-buffered",
          "exact_quote": "Consider a finite critical nearest-neighbor ferromagnetic Ising graph, disjoint vertex sets $I,J,F$, and common pins on $F$. Let $\\mu_b$ be the marginal law on $I$ obtained by prescribing an arbitrary configuration $b$ on $J$. Whenever the following sets lie in the indicated square-lattice charts, the bounds hold for all configurations $a$ on $I$ and all $b,b'$.*\n\n1.  *For every $c>0$ there is $K_c<\\infty$ such that $I\\subset B_R(z)$, $J\\subset B_{(1+c)R}(z)^c$ imply $$K_c^{-1}\\le \\frac{\\mu_b(a)}{\\mu_{b'}(a)}\\le K_c.$$*\n\n2.  *There are absolute $K<\\infty$, $\\zeta>0$ such that $I\\subset B_w(z)$, $J\\subset B_R(z)^c$, and $R\\ge8w\\ge8$ imply $$\\left|\\log\\frac{\\mu_b(a)}{\\mu_{b'}(a)}\\right|\n     \\le K(w/R)^\\zeta.$$ No free neighborhood of $I$ is required in these two assertions: additional common pins may lie anywhere outside $I\\cup J$.*\n\n3.  *Either law can be replaced by any mixture of the indicated conditional laws. The fixed-ratio comparison also holds for laws from different domains, including the plane and a torus, if there is a common isometric lattice collar separating the observed spins from every difference in the data. In this cross-domain assertion the collar is unpinned in both laws. Applying a common randomization kernel to the observed spins preserves each density bound.*\n\n*All constants are independent of the number, positions, and values of the common pins and of the mesh. In the first two assertions the same ratio bounds hold when the roles of the two terminal sets are reversed.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-pure-buffered",
      "inputs": [
        "Pure critical finite graph",
        "Separated terminal sets",
        "Common pins"
      ],
      "method": "Use pure buffered likelihood comparisons and terminal symmetry of the density-odds estimate.",
      "output": "Fixed-ratio and small-patch pointwise density bounds.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-pure-buffered"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-pure-annular",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-pure-buffered"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-pure-annular",
          "exact_quote": "Let $2\\le r<R/4$, $A=B_R\\setminus B_r$, and $W=B_{2R}\\setminus B_{r/2}$, with integer rounding of the boundaries. Suppose $W$ is an unpinned square-lattice chart. The law on $A$, conditional on arbitrary spins outside $W$, has pointwise density relative to $\\mu_0|_A$ between $K^{-1}$ and $K$, for an absolute $K$ independent of $R/r$. The assertion applies in a torus chart and to mixtures of exterior conditions. Fixed changes of the two collar ratios only change $K$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-pure-annular",
      "inputs": [
        "Unpinned inner and outer annular collars",
        "Terminal-symmetric comparison"
      ],
      "method": "Change outer data and then inner data across separate collars; mix the reference exterior law.",
      "output": "Annular density comparison independent of the annular aspect ratio.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-pure-annular"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-transmission",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-signed-transmission",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed exponent $\\eta\\in(0,1/8)$ with the following property. Let $\\mu$ be a pure critical Gibbs law with common pins, let $q=q(\\sigma_S)$ be order-positive and centered for its source marginal, and let $J$ be an admissible cut at scales $w,s$. For every bounded target observable $h=h(\\sigma_T)$, when $w$ is sufficiently large, $$\\left|\\mathbb E_\\mu[qh]\\right|\n \\le C\\left\\|h\\right\\|_\\infty (w/s)^{-7/8+\\eta}\n               \\mathbb E_\\mu[qM_J],\n \\qquad M_J=s^{-7/8}\\sum_{x\\in J}\\sigma_x.\n \\label{eq:pure-signed}$$ The constant depends only on the admissibility constants and the fixed chart geometry. It is independent of the mass of $q_+$, the mesh, the number and values of source pins, and the ratio $w/s$. The same inequality holds for expectation differences of two ordered source configurations, and after averaging an ordered coupling of random source configurations, with the common Gibbs continuation. All changed pins and fields lie on the source side of the cut; the target-side continuation is identical in the compared laws. One may decrease $\\eta$, keeping it positive.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-signed-transmission",
      "inputs": [
        "Order-positive centered source",
        "Admissible free cut",
        "Common target-side Gibbs continuation"
      ],
      "method": "Normalize the positive and negative source parts, couple them in stochastic order, and average deterministic transmission.",
      "output": "Signed transmission bound without dividing by a mesh-dependent source mass.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-signed-transmission"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-line-continuity",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-pure-buffered"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-line-continuity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J_s,J'_s$ be horizontal or vertical lattice segments of length $O(s)$, with corresponding portions displaced by at most $Cw$ and unmatched end portions of total length $O(w)$. The assertion also applies to concentric square contours of radii comparable to $s$, differing in radius by at most $Cw$, and to a fixed finite union of these pairs. Assume their union has a free neighborhood of width $cs$, covered by a fixed number of buffered lattice charts. For each fixed $h=w/s\\in(0,h_0]$, $$\\limsup_{s\\to\\infty}\n \\left\\|s^{-7/8}\\sum_{J_s}\\sigma_x\n       -s^{-7/8}\\sum_{J'_s}\\sigma_x\\right\\|_{L^2(\\mu)}\n \\le C_0h^{3/8}.\n \\label{eq:pure-lines}$$ The bound is uniform over arbitrary pins beyond the stated free neighborhood and mixtures of their laws. The constant depends only on the fixed geometry and buffer.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-line-continuity",
      "inputs": [
        "Nearby buffered line or contour pairs",
        "Pure spin covariance estimates"
      ],
      "method": "Use the cited nearby-line estimate, continuum covariance integration, and buffered density comparison.",
      "output": "Mesh-first norm continuity for normalized line sums.",
      "proof_anchor": "stmt-prop-line-continuity"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-cor-line-prediction",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-line-continuity"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-line-prediction",
          "exact_quote": "In Proposition <a href=\"#prop:line-continuity\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"prop:line-continuity\">7</a>, let $\\mathcal F$ be any spin information containing $\\sigma_{J'_s}$, and let $P_{\\mathcal F}$ denote conditional expectation. Then, for each fixed $w/s$, $$\\limsup_{s\\to\\infty}\n \\left\\|M_{J_s}-P_{\\mathcal F}M_{J_s}\\right\\|_2\n \\le C_0(w/s)^{3/8}.$$ Both line sums use the same normalization $s^{-7/8}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-line-prediction",
      "inputs": [

      ],
      "method": "Use the nearby measurable line sum as a competitor for orthogonal projection.",
      "output": "Conditional prediction error bound.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-cor-line-prediction"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-spin",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-spin",
          "exact_quote": "There is $a_\\sigma>0$, determined by the square-lattice convention, such that, for distinct physical points $z_1,\\ldots,z_n$, $$a_\\sigma^{-n}\\delta^{-n/8}\n \\mathbb E_0\\prod_{i=1}^n\\sigma_{x_i^\\delta}\n \\longrightarrow S(z_1,\\ldots,z_n),\n \\qquad \\delta x_i^\\delta\\longrightarrow z_i.$$ For odd $n$, $S=0$; for even $n$, $S$ is the nonnegative square root of $$S(z_1,\\ldots,z_n)^2\n =2^{-n/2}\\!\\sum_{\\substack{u_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}\\\\\\sum_i u_i=0}}\n       \\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_iu_j/2}.\n \\label{eq:pure-spin-formula}$$ Thus $S(z,w)=|z-w|^{-1/4}$. The convergence is uniform when the points lie in compact sets with fixed mutual separation. Also $$\\begin{aligned}\n 0\\le\\mathbb E_0[\\sigma_x\\sigma_y]&\\le C(1+|x-y|)^{-1/4},\n \\label{eq:pure-two-point}\\\\\n 0\\le\\mathbb E^+_{B_r}[\\sigma_0]&\\le C(1+r)^{-1/8}.\n \\label{eq:pure-plus-one-point}\n\\end{aligned}$$ For $n\\ge2$, repeated sites allowed, $$\\left|\\mathbb E_0\\prod_{i=1}^n\\sigma_{x_i}\\right|\n \\le C^n\\prod_{i=1}^n\n \\left(1+\\min_{j\\ne i}|x_i-x_j|\\right)^{-1/8}.\n \\label{eq:pure-multipoint-bound}$$ For every fixed finite collection of bounded compactly supported Riemann-integrable spatial test functions $\\varphi$, the smeared variables $$\\Phi_\\delta(\\varphi)\n =a_\\sigma^{-1}\\delta^{15/8}\n                  \\sum_{x\\in\\mathbb Z^2}\\varphi(\\delta x)\\sigma_x$$ have joint moment limits obtained by integrating $S$ against the test functions. Here Riemann integrability is equivalent to having a set of discontinuities of two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero. Spatial smearings are chosen in this class and fixed before taking the mesh limit; polygonal cell indicators are included. The limiting correlation functions are covariant under Möbius transformations, including inversion, with weight $1/8$ at each spin insertion.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-pure-spin",
      "inputs": [
        "Pure spin correlation limits",
        "Fixed bounded Riemann-integrable spatial smearings"
      ],
      "method": "Combine published spin limits with nearest-insertion domination, diagonal removal, and convergent spatial Riemann sums.",
      "output": "Explicit spin correlations, moment domination, and joint smeared moment limits.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-pure-spin"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-pure-reflection",

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-pure-reflection"

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-energy",
          "exact_quote": "Let $e_1,e_2$ be the coordinate unit vectors and set $b_j(x)=\\sigma_x\\sigma_{x+e_j}-1/\\sqrt2$. In the full-plane critical law, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_0(b_j(0),b_k(x))\n   =\\frac{1+o(1)}{\\pi^2|x|^2},\\qquad |x|\\longrightarrow\\infty,\n \\quad j,k\\in\\{1,2\\}.\n \\label{eq:pure-energy}$$ For a fixed finite local average $B(x)=\\sum_qc_qb_{j_q}(x+d_q)$, with $c_q\\ge0$ and $c=\\sum_qc_q>0$, $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_0(B(0),b_k(x))\\sim\\frac{c}{\\pi^2|x|^2},\\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Cov}}_0(B(0),B(x))\\sim\\frac{c^2}{\\pi^2|x|^2}.$$ In particular square symmetrization of the bond energy cannot cancel its leading covariance.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-energy-covariance",
      "inputs": [
        "Pure energy correlation formula",
        "Continuity at the critical Gibbs state"
      ],
      "method": "Use the pure massless energy formula, approach exact criticality by continuity, and sum positive local coefficients.",
      "output": "Positive leading thermal covariance under square symmetrization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-energy"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-pure-sle",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-pure-sle",
          "exact_quote": "In ordinary lattice approximations of a fixed polygonal Jordan domain with two distinct marked boundary points, the critical nearest-neighbor Ising interface with opposite constant signs on the two marked arcs converges to chordal $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SLE}}_3$. Convergence is in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization, oriented from the first mark to the second. Either local turn at an alternating face is allowed, including the deterministic rule of the problem. Bounded local rounding and half-stubs have vanishing effect on this convergence.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-pure-sle",
      "inputs": [

    },

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-thm-pure-cle",
          "exact_quote": "In a plus Jordan domain with lattice boundaries converging uniformly modulo parametrization, every complete noncrossing resolution of all spin-disagreement edges converges to the same oriented nested $\\mathop{\\mathrm{CLE}}_3$. The assertion is uniform over choices of the complete resolution. Loops are compared in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization and choice of starting point; collections are compared by matching loops and charging the diameters of unmatched loops. Thus the statement includes coverage and multiplicities of all loops above each fixed positive diameter cutoff.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }

      "id": "step-pure-cle",

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-four-passages",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a bulk lattice chart containing $B_{2R}(z)$, with all pins and all changes of ambient domain beyond a fixed proportional outer collar. For every $\\alpha<21/8$, there are $C_\\alpha<\\infty$ and $u_\\alpha>0$, depending on that fixed collar but not on the outer conditions, such that, for each fixed $0<u<u_\\alpha$, $$\\limsup_{\\delta\\downarrow0}\n \\mathbb P\\bigl(\\mathcal A_4^{\\mathrm{cont}}(z;uR,R)\\bigr)\n \\le C_\\alpha u^\\alpha.\n \\label{eq:pure-four-passages}$$ This applies to the fixed diagonal resolution, and uniformly to complete resolutions. The same statement holds for circular annuli and for fixed-factor changes of the radii. In particular one may fix $\\alpha>2$. The mesh limit precedes the limit $u\\downarrow0$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"

      "inputs": [

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-rsw",
      "depends_on": [

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-pure-rsw",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a finite collection of polygonal corridors, each a topological rectangle with designated opposite end gates, and polygonal annular bands. Prescribe one color for each required crossing or circuit. Assume each requirement admits a finite rectangle construction with positive-width unpinned buffers, and that the buffered constructions for opposite colors are disjoint. Their simultaneous occurrence has probability bounded below by a positive constant for all sufficiently fine meshes. The constant may depend on this fixed geometry, and is uniform over pins beyond the buffers and ambient plane or torus laws with common lattice charts.*\n\n*There are $C<\\infty$, $\\rho>0$ such that, in a buffered bulk annulus and for $1\\le r<R/C$, $$\\mathbb P(\\text{a monochromatic spin arm or a resolved contour passage\n                 from }B_r\\text{ to }B_R^c)\n \\le C(r/R)^\\rho.\n \\label{eq:pure-one-passage}$$ For the monochromatic event either color may be specified or both allowed. The annular construction may be conditioned on earlier reveals that remain outside its unpinned test collars. Fixed-factor changes of radii and bounded lattice drawing corrections change only the constants.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"

      "output": "Signed simultaneous crossing positivity and positive one-passage decay.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-pure-rsw"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-window",
      "depends_on": [

      ],
      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-window",
          "exact_quote": "Fix nested enlargements $F_0\\subset F_*$ of the support of a test function, with all three relevant gaps positive: from the support to $\\partial F_0$, from $\\partial F_0$ to $\\partial F_*$, and from $F_*$ to $D$. For each $t>0$ there is a finite menu of radial windows, each of positive rescaled width, such that one window $[F_-,C]$ satisfies $$\\sup_{F_-\\subset F\\subset C}\\sup_h\n       \\|P_Fh-P_Ch\\|_{L^2(\\nu)}\\leq t.\n \\label{eq:transfer-window}$$ The second supremum is over all the likelihoods in <a href=\"#eq:transfer-layer-density\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:transfer-layer-density\">[eq:transfer-layer-density]</a>. The menu and its widths are independent of the mesh and of the boundary assignments.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-smoothing",
          "exact_quote": "Given $b>0$, the window tolerance in Lemma <a href=\"#lem:transfer-window\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"lem:transfer-window\">17</a> can be chosen so that, for a sufficiently large fixed $M$ and then all sufficiently large $s$, $$\\sup_h\\|(I-T_M)P_Ch\\|_2<b.\n \\label{eq:transfer-smoothing}$$ All constants are uniform over the likelihood family and the finite menu of possible windows.",

    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-replacement",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-likelihood-smoothing",
        "step-pure-spin"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many pairs of positively separated supports, each a square or a square band, with buffered enlargements that remain disjoint. For every $t>0$, each lattice function $v$ of norm at most one on any one support can be replaced, for its full-plane correlations against all functions of norm at most one on the paired supports, with error at most $t$, by a function of finitely many smeared spin sums in its enlargement. The replacement has norm at most one. The number of sums, their shapes, and a modulus of continuity for the replacing functions belong to a fixed finite menu, independent of $v$ and of all sufficiently fine meshes. Along any mesh sequence, further subsequences of these replacements converge to bounded continuous tests of finitely many continuum smeared spins. Replacements on both sides of each gap can be made together, and preserve spin-flip and square symmetries by averaging.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-bounded-replacement",
      "inputs": [
        "Likelihood smoothing",
        "Separated buffered supports",

      "output": "Uniform bounded continuum proxies for separated correlations.",

    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-charges",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-transfer-charges",
          "exact_quote": "Let $z_1,\\ldots,z_{2m}$ be distinct complex points, with $m\\geq1$, and put $$W_z(u)=\\prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_i u_j/2},\\qquad\n A_q(z)=\\sum_{\\substack{u_i\\in\\{-1,1\\}\\\\\\sum_i u_i=q}}W_z(u),$$ where an empty charge class has sum zero. Define $$A_0^d(z)=\\sum_{\\sum_i u_i=0}W_z(u)\n                        \\left|\\sum_i u_i z_i\\right|^2.$$ Then $$A_0^dA_0=4A_2^2.\n \\label{eq:transfer-charge-identity}$$ If all points lie in the unit disk, then $A_2/A_0\\leq1$, and all ratios $A_q/A_0$ are bounded by one.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],

      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-transfer-charges"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-prop-radial-gap",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-pure-spin",
        "step-reflection-positive",
        "step-complex-charge-identity"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-radial-gap",
          "exact_quote": "On the subspace of $\\mathcal H$ generated by even, quarter-turn invariant spin tests, the spectrum of $\\mathcal D$ below $3$ consists of the constant state at $0$ and one nonzero one-dimensional state at $1$. There is a real linear functional $\\ell$ with $|\\ell(F)|\\leq\\|F\\|_2$ such that, for real even quarter-turn invariant tests $F,G$ of finite ordinary $L^2$ norm and $0<r\\leq1$, $$\\left|\\mathbb E[(\\Theta F)D_{-\\log r}G]\n       -\\mathbb EF\\mathbb EG-r\\ell(F)\\ell(G)\\right|\n \\leq r^3\\|F\\|_2\\|G\\|_2.\n \\label{eq:transfer-radial-gap}$$ The assertion extends from smeared polynomials to bounded field tests by ordinary $L^2$ approximation.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-radial-gap",
      "inputs": [
        "Smeared spin algebra",
        "Reflection positivity",
        "Complex charged-sum identity"
      ],
      "method": "Construct the reflection-positive dilation semigroup, expand charged correlations, cancel the scalar quadratic coefficient, and use positive spectral measures.",
      "output": "Constant and thermal spectral projections with a uniform higher-degree remainder.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-radial-gap"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-thm-transfer",
      "depends_on": [
        "step-bounded-replacement",
        "step-radial-gap",
        "step-pure-buffered"
      ],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-transfer",
          "exact_quote": "There is a constant $C<\\infty$ with the following property. For each $a>0$, all sufficiently large fixed integers $L$ admit an integer $s_0$, linear functionals $\\ell_s:\\mathcal X_s\\to\\mathbb R$, and vectors $e_s\\in\\mathcal X_s$, for every integer $s\\geq s_0$, such that $$\\begin{aligned}\n \\|\\ell_s\\|&\\leq C,& \\left\\|e_s\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q}&\\leq C,& \\ell_s(e_s)&=1,\n \\label{eq:transfer-uniform-norms}\\\\\n \\left\\|A_sf\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q}&\\leq CL|\\ell_s(f)|+a\\left\\|f\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q},\n \\label{eq:transfer-stable}\\\\\n |\\ell_{Ls}(A_sf)-L\\ell_s(f)|&\\leq a\\left\\|f\\right\\|_{\\mathrm q}.\n \\label{eq:transfer-compatible}\n\\end{aligned}$$ The constant $C$ is independent of $a,L,s$. The choices are made in the following order: first $L$, then a larger fixed measurement ratio $H$, then the approximation accuracies used below, and finally $s_0$. In particular, all estimates hold simultaneously for all $s\\geq s_0$ and all bounded functions in $\\mathcal X_s$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-uniform-transfer",
      "inputs": [
        "Bounded replacement across gaps",
        "Radial spectral gap",
        "Buffered boundary likelihoods"
      ],
      "method": "Measure a common scalar coefficient with a fixed distant field test, replace arbitrary inner and boundary functions, and use the exact Markov tower identity.",
      "output": "Uniform transfer norm and coefficient compatibility for every bounded local function.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-prop-radial-gap-2"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-reverse",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-reverse",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\mu_t$ be a differentiable family of strictly positive probability laws on a finite spin space, and let $I_x$ be specified update sets. Let $f_x$ be fixed bounded real functions, with $\\|f_x\\|_\\infty\\le a$, $a\\ge0$, and let $P_{t,x}$ denote conditional expectation under $\\mu_t$ given the complement of $I_x$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative}\n \\dot\\mu_t(\\sigma)=\\mu_t(\\sigma)c\n \\sum_x\\bigl(P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)-f_x(\\sigma)\\bigr),\n \\qquad c\\ge0.$$ If $a>0$, define the refresh law $$\\label{eq:flow-refresh-law}\n Q_{t,x}(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n =\\mu_t(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n \\left[1+\\frac{f_x(\\eta_{I_x}\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n                  -P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)}{2a}\\right].$$ It is a probability law. Updating set $I_x$ from this law at rate $2ac$, independently for each $x$, has generator $\\mathcal L_t$ with $\\mathcal L_t^*\\mu_t=-\\dot\\mu_t$. Thus using $\\mathcal L_{1-t}$ from time zero to one carries the law at time one exactly to the law at time zero. If $a=0$, the derivative in <a href=\"#eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\">[eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative]</a> vanishes and no refresh is needed.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-reverse-generator",
      "inputs": [
        "Finite strictly positive probability family",
        "Specified derivative identity",
        "Uniformly bounded local functions"
      ],
      "method": "Check normalization and nonnegativity of the tilted refresh table and compute its incoming-minus-outgoing mass.",
      "output": "An exact reverse refresh generator with spin-independent rates.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-flow-reverse"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-analytic",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-analytic",
          "exact_quote": "Fix $L\\ge9$ and $d>0$. There are $r>0$ and $C<\\infty$, independent of the integer $s$, such that for $\\|V\\|_{s,d}<r$, Equation <a href=\"#eq:flow-ode\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-ode\">[eq:flow-ode]</a> has a specified analytic solution taking values in $\\mathcal A_{s,d/2}$. Uniformly in $0\\le t\\le1$, $$\\label{eq:flow-intermediate}\n \\sum_\\ell |\\ell|e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\n \\|v_\\ell(t)\\|_\\infty\\le C\\|V\\|_{s,d}.$$ Its endpoint has the form $$\\label{eq:flow-before-compression}\n V(1)=(V-f)+P^0_{Ls}f+\\mathcal R_s(V),\\qquad\n \\|\\mathcal R_s(V)\\|_{s,d/2}\\le C\\|V\\|_{s,d}^2.$$ On a smaller ball the remainder is analytic and satisfies $\\|D\\mathcal R_s(V)\\|\\le C\\|V\\|_{s,d}$ and $\\|D^2\\mathcal R_s(V)\\|\\le C$, with these operator norms from $\\mathcal A_{s,d}$ to $\\mathcal A_{s,d/2}$. The construction preserves real coefficients and the stated symmetries.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],
      "id": "step-analytic-flow",
      "inputs": [
        "Small weighted interaction family",
        "Fixed single-square forcing",
        "Pure conditional expectation"
      ],
      "method": "Expand conditional numerator and reciprocal denominator in connected labeled lists; solve triangular truncations under a decreasing exponential-weight majorant.",
      "output": "Analytic one-step solution and quadratic endpoint remainder.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-flow-analytic"
    },
    {
      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-compression",
      "depends_on": [],
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-compression",
          "exact_quote": "For every integer $L\\ge9$, there is a linear relabeling map $C_s:\\mathcal A_{s,d/2}\\to\\mathcal A_{Ls,d}$ that leaves the local functions and their roots unchanged and satisfies $$\\|C_sW\\|_{Ls,d}\\le\\|W\\|_{s,d/2}.$$ If a declared union is contained in $B_{Ls}$, it is relabeled as a single square. The map commutes with the specified symmetries. The rewriting map $\\mathcal S_s=L^2C_s$ preserves the translation density: $$\\label{eq:flow-rewrite}\n \\mathcal H_{Ls}(\\mathcal S_sW)=\\mathcal H_s(W).$$ On input lists of length at least two it also satisfies $$\\label{eq:flow-carry}\n \\|\\mathcal S_sW\\|_{Ls,d}\n \\le L^2 e^{-d/2}\\|W\\|_{s,d}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }

      "inputs": [

      "output": "Weight-restoring relabeling that preserves translation density.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-flow-compression"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-stable-graph",
          "exact_quote": "With the choices just described, there are bounded single-square vectors $w_k\\in\\mathcal X_k$, $\\ell_kw_k=1$, and a real analytic function $h$ on a neighborhood of zero in $\\ker\\ell_0$, with $h(0)=0$, such that each sufficiently small vector $$\\label{eq:flow-graph}\n V_0=h(u_0)w_0+u_0,\\qquad u_0\\in\\ker\\ell_0,$$ has a trajectory $V_{k+1}=\\Phi_{s_k}(V_k)$ satisfying $$\\sup_{k\\ge0}2^k\\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}\\le C\\|u_0\\|_{s_0,d}.$$ In a sufficiently small ball these are exactly the initial conditions with a trajectory of finite sufficiently small norm $\\sup_k2^k\\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}$. The tangent space of the graph at zero is exactly the set of initial conditions whose linear trajectories $Z_{k+1}=T_{s_k}Z_k$ have finite norm $\\sup_k2^k\\|Z_k\\|_{s_k,d}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }

      "output": "Analytic graph of exactly the small decaying trajectories and its linear tangent characterization.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-lem-flow-stable-graph"
    },

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-critical-trajectory",
          "exact_quote": "For every $J>0$ and finite-range real potential $U$ satisfying the symmetries of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, the preceding scale parameters can be fixed independently of all domains and boundary data. The initial scale $s_0$ may be enlarged by any prescribed finite amount before the following construction. For each sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ there is $\\lambda_*>0$ and a real analytic function $\\beta_c:(-\\lambda_*,\\lambda_*)\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\beta_c(0)=\\beta_0$, for which the initial potential <a href=\"#eq:flow-physical-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-physical-input\">[eq:flow-physical-input]</a> has a trajectory $V_{k+1}=\\Phi_{s_k}(V_k)$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:flow-critical-decay}\n \\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}\\le\\epsilon2^{-k},\\qquad k\\ge0.$$ Its intermediate one-step trajectories obey $$\\label{eq:flow-critical-intermediate}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le1}\n \\sum_\\ell |\\ell|e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\n \\|v_{k,\\ell}(t)\\|_\\infty\\le C\\epsilon2^{-k}.$$ The same construction applies to both signs of $\\lambda$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"

        "Positive energy covariance"

      "method": "Test iterated temperature derivatives against distant bond energy, prove transversality to the decaying graph, and apply the analytic implicit-function theorem.",
      "output": "A real analytic domain-independent temperature branch with decaying interactions.",
      "proof_anchor": "proof-stmt-thm-critical-trajectory"
    },

      "claim_anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-periodization",
      "depends_on": [
