```json
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-flow-periodization",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose $Ls\\le N/10$, and suppose a real one-step trajectory obeys $$\\sup_{0\\le t\\le1}\\sum_\\ell |\\ell|\n e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\\|v_\\ell(t)\\|_\\infty\\le r_s,\n \\qquad \\|f\\|_\\infty\\le r_s,$$ with $r_s$ in the fixed small ball. Let $\\mu_t=\\mu_{s,N}^{V(t)}$, and let $P_{t,x}^{N}$ be its exact torus conditional expectation over $B_{Ls}^\\circ(x)$. There is a signed measure $e_t$, of total mass zero, such that $$\\label{eq:flow-finite-residual}\n \\dot\\mu_t=\\mu_t s^{-2}\\sum_{x\\in\\mathbb T_N}\n       (P_{t,x}^{N}f_x-f_x)+e_t,\n \\qquad\n \\|e_t\\|_{\\mathop{\\mathrm{TV}}}\\le C r_s(N/s)^2e^{-cN/s}.$$ The constants $C,c>0$ depend on the fixed flow parameters, but not on $s,N,t$.",
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"

      "evidence": [

          "anchor": "stmt-prop-edit-coupling",
          "exact_quote": "Let the physical torus law $\\mu_{\\lambda,N}$ be at the temperature of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:critical-trajectory\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:critical-trajectory\">26</a>, with trajectory bound <a href=\"#eq:flow-critical-decay\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-critical-decay\">[eq:flow-critical-decay]</a>. Let $$K_N=\\max\\{k\\ge0:Ls_k\\le N/10\\}$$ for all sufficiently large $N$. There is a coupling of $\\sigma^{(0)}\\sim\\mu_{0,N}$, $\\sigma^{(\\lambda)}\\sim\\mu_{\\lambda,N}$, and Bernoulli marks $M_{k,x}$, $0\\le k\\le K_N$, $x\\in\\mathbb T_N$, with the following properties. The marks are mutually independent and independent of $\\sigma^{(0)}$, and $$\\label{eq:flow-mark-probabilities}\n \\mathbb P(M_{k,x}=1)\\le C\\epsilon2^{-k}s_k^{-2}.$$ Except on an event of probability $o(1)$ as $N\\to\\infty$, $\\sigma^{(\\lambda)}$ can be obtained from $\\sigma^{(0)}$ by changing spins only inside the union of the marked squares $B_{Ls_k}(x)$. The values of the changed spins may depend on all previous refreshes. The constant and the exceptional probability are independent of test domains and exterior configurations. Enlarging these squares by any fixed number of lattice steps preserves the conclusion.",

        }

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability-pivotal",
          "exact_quote": "Let $T$ be a fixed polygonal crossing, circuit, or one of the three annular tests in <a href=\"#eq:stability-checks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stability-checks\">[eq:stability-checks]</a>. Suppose that two spin configurations agree off a square of radius $b$ but give different values to $T$. Enlarge this square by a fixed factor to contain all contour segments affected by the change. There is a positive geometry scale $a_T$ with the following properties. Set $$u=\\min\\{a_T,\\max\\{b,\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(z,\\mathcal E_T)\\}\\},\\qquad\n v=\\min\\{a_T,\\max\\{b,\\mathop{\\mathrm{dist}}(z,\\mathcal V_T)\\}\\},$$ where $\\mathcal E_T$ and $\\mathcal V_T$ are the finite collections of sides and vertices of the test polygons, and $z$ is the edit center. For $H_2$, these collections also include the confinement line segments and their intersections with the two square boundaries. For $b<a_T$, the common contours outside the enlarged edit square must supply the following checks, after fixed crops:*\n\n1.  *four passages on rings between scales $b$ and $u$;*\n\n2.  *two passages confined to an appropriate half-plane on rings between scales $u$ and $v$;*\n\n3.  *one passage on rings between scales $v$ and $a_T$.*\n\n*Rings within a fixed multiplicative distance of transition scales may be omitted. The half-plane checks may be centered on the nearby side and the last checks near the nearby vertex. For a fixed test only a bounded number of further rings need be omitted. The constants are uniform over translations, rotations, and dilations of each of the three fixed annular shapes.",

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-stability-products",
          "exact_quote": "There is a fixed constant $D$ with the following property. Take annular checks of the types <a href=\"#eq:stability-checks\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:stability-checks\">[eq:stability-checks]</a>, whose inner radii exceed $C b$, and whose enlarged neighborhoods and proportional inner and outer collars are disjoint. More precisely, after absorbing rotations and lattice margins, each check has a collar $W_j$ of the form $$S_{2R_j}(z_j,\\theta_j)\\setminus\n       S_{r_j/2}(z_j,\\theta_j),$$ the other check supports lie outside $W_j$, and the sets $\\mathcal J_j$ are disjoint. All collars lie in bulk torus charts. For arbitrary independent mark probabilities at or below this cutoff, $$\\mathbb P\\left(\\bigcap_{j=1}^m T_j^+\\right)\n       \\leq\\prod_{j=1}^m D\\mathbb P(T_j^+).\n \\label{eq:stability-product}$$ The constant $D$ depends on the fixed collar proportions and is independent of the annular aspect ratios and the mark probabilities.",

      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-prop-stability-bootstrap",
```

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-extreme-rectangle-control**: For a strictly positive MTP$_2$ mass on disjoint finite binary coordinate sets, the extreme corner ratio bounds every multiplicative rectangle ratio and its reciprocal. The finite implementation checks this algebraic conclusion with exact rational arithmetic.
- **inv-conditional-density-control**: The same extreme corner ratio bounds every pointwise ratio between conditional laws of the observed coordinates. Averaging these pointwise inequalities preserves them for arbitrary mixtures; the executable checks the conditional inequalities, while the mixture extension remains a mathematical consequence.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-assuming-mtp-from-positive-weights**: Apply the extreme corner bound to every positive binary probability table. -> Positivity alone does not imply MTP$_2$, so an interior rectangle can escape the extreme corner bound.
- **anti-unrestricted-pinned-attractiveness**: Treat all auxiliary-bit laws obtained by pinning physical spins as attractive and apply the finite rectangle result automatically. -> The paper establishes attractiveness for free vertex factors and explicitly withholds that assertion for pinned factors.
- **anti-finite-check-as-interface-certificate**: Interpret successful finite table tests as verification of the full perturbed-interface scaling limit. -> The finite algebraic lemma does not establish uniform transfer, the decaying critical trajectory, bulk stability, or oriented curve convergence.
- **anti-zero-mass-conditioning**: Allow zero weights and use the same division-based conditional comparison. -> A conditioning event or an extreme corner can have zero mass, leaving the displayed ratios undefined.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `algorithmic`. The executable isolates the finite algebraic result in Lemma 41, rather than the continuum universality theorem. Its source precondition is: Let $p(i,j)>0$ be an MTP$_2$ probability mass on two disjoint finite sets of binary coordinates $I,J$. Exact enumeration decides the rectangle and conditional-density conclusions for each supplied rational table; it does not certify all table sizes or the interface scaling limit.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON with n_i, n_j, and positive rational weights indexed by binary masks.
# PRECONDITIONS: Disjoint coordinate blocks; nonnegative integer dimensions with total at most 6; a complete strictly positive rational table.
# OUTPUTS: Applicability, exact extreme corner ratio, and exhaustive rectangle and conditional-density verdicts.
# TIME: Finite exhaustive enumeration of coordinate squares, rectangles, and conditional ratios.
# SPACE: The rational weight table and its conditional column totals.
# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python Fraction arithmetic; no logarithms or floating-point comparisons.
# FAILURE: Malformed inputs produce invalid_input; non-MTP2 tables produce an inapplicable result.
import json
import sys
from fractions import Fraction


def solve(data):
    ni, nj = data['n_i'], data['n_j']
    if type(ni) is not int or type(nj) is not int or ni < 0 or nj < 0 or ni + nj > 6:
        raise ValueError('dimensions')
    raw = data['weights']
    if not isinstance(raw, list) or len(raw) != (1 << (ni + nj)):
        raise ValueError('table')
    if any(type(v) not in (str, int) for v in raw):
        raise ValueError('rational encoding')
    w = [Fraction(v) for v in raw]
    if any(v <= 0 for v in w):
        raise ValueError('positivity')
    n = ni + nj
    applicable = True
    for a in range(n):
        for b in range(a + 1, n):
            for x in range(1 << n):
                if x & ((1 << a) | (1 << b)):
                    continue
                if w[x] * w[x | (1 << a) | (1 << b)] < w[x | (1 << a)] * w[x | (1 << b)]:
                    applicable = False
    if not applicable:
        return {'applicable': False, 'extreme_ratio': None, 'rectangles_valid': None, 'conditionals_valid': None}
    im = (1 << ni) - 1
    jm = ((1 << nj) - 1) << ni
    top = im | jm
    extreme = w[top] * w[0] / (w[im] * w[jm])
    lower = 1 / extreme
    rectangles_valid = True
    for i in range(1 << ni):
        for ip in range(1 << ni):
            for j in range(1 << nj):
                for jp in range(1 << nj):
                    ratio = w[i | (j << ni)] * w[ip | (jp << ni)] / (w[i | (jp << ni)] * w[ip | (j << ni)])
                    if not lower <= ratio <= extreme:
                        rectangles_valid = False
    totals = [sum((w[i | (j << ni)] for i in range(1 << ni)), Fraction(0)) for j in range(1 << nj)]
    conditionals_valid = True
    for i in range(1 << ni):
        for j in range(1 << nj):
            for jp in range(1 << nj):
                ratio = w[i | (j << ni)] * totals[jp] / (w[i | (jp << ni)] * totals[j])
                if not lower <= ratio <= extreme:
                    conditionals_valid = False
    return {'applicable': True, 'extreme_ratio': str(extreme), 'rectangles_valid': rectangles_valid, 'conditionals_valid': conditionals_valid}

try:
    result = solve(json.load(sys.stdin))
except (KeyError, TypeError, ValueError, ZeroDivisionError):
    result = {'error': 'invalid_input'}
print(json.dumps(result, sort_keys=True))
```

```json
    "reference_code": "# INPUTS: JSON with n_i, n_j, and positive rational weights indexed by binary masks.\n# PRECONDITIONS: Disjoint coordinate blocks; nonnegative integer dimensions with total at most 6; a complete strictly positive rational table.\n# OUTPUTS: Applicability, exact extreme corner ratio, and exhaustive rectangle and conditional-density verdicts.\n# TIME: Finite exhaustive enumeration of coordinate squares, rectangles, and conditional ratios.\n# SPACE: The rational weight table and its conditional column totals.\n# ARITHMETIC: Exact Python Fraction arithmetic; no logarithms or floating-point comparisons.\n# FAILURE: Malformed inputs produce invalid_input; non-MTP2 tables produce an inapplicable result.\nimport json\nimport sys\nfrom fractions import Fraction\n\n\ndef solve(data):\n    ni, nj = data['n_i'], data['n_j']\n    if type(ni) is not int or type(nj) is not int or ni < 0 or nj < 0 or ni + nj > 6:\n        raise ValueError('dimensions')\n    raw = data['weights']\n    if not isinstance(raw, list) or len(raw) != (1 << (ni + nj)):\n        raise ValueError('table')\n    if any(type(v) not in (str, int) for v in raw):\n        raise ValueError('rational encoding')\n    w = [Fraction(v) for v in raw]\n    if any(v <= 0 for v in w):\n        raise ValueError('positivity')\n    n = ni + nj\n    applicable = True\n    for a in range(n):\n        for b in range(a + 1, n):\n            for x in range(1 << n):\n                if x & ((1 << a) | (1 << b)):\n                    continue\n                if w[x] * w[x | (1 << a) | (1 << b)] < w[x | (1 << a)] * w[x | (1 << b)]:\n                    applicable = False\n    if not applicable:\n        return {'applicable': False, 'extreme_ratio': None, 'rectangles_valid': None, 'conditionals_valid': None}\n    im = (1 << ni) - | domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
```

| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The interface convergence theorem supplies an asymptotic mathematical specification, but no executable implementation or finite-mesh error certificate. Its existence statement does not provide a software procedure for computing the perturbed critical temperature. |
| games_simulation | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | The finite-state reverse refresh construction can be adapted to spin simulations when the probability path and its exact conditional tables are supplied. It constructs local stochastic updates with rates independent of the current spins; it does not by itself compute the infinite-scale critical trajectory. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The pointwise interior-density comparison could guide mathematical validation of spin experiments, but its buffer-dependent constant is established through probabilistic arguments rather than supplied as a computable tool contract. |
| algorithms | True | strong | implementable | inferred | The stopped-reveal proof gives a finite adaptive exploration algorithm on a triangulated disk or rectangle. With a protected inner disk, successful exploration leaves that disk unqueried and exposes only the requested color along the facing boundary. |
| data_structures | True | moderate | adaptable | inferred | Connected rooted covering lists provide a concrete representation of local interaction support. The compression construction replaces a fine-scale covering by a connected coarse-scale covering while preserving the root and local function, and controls the change in the weighted norm. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The uniform transfer estimates concern bounded spin functions and scale-dependent interaction spaces. They supply no accelerator layout, parallel execution construction, or hardware resource estimate. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The auxiliary bond bits represent physical spin weights through positive local tables. Their role is stochastic comparison, and the paper supplies no cryptographic construction or security property. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Gaussian observations approximate exterior likelihoods for separated Ising correlations. This supplies neither a learning procedure nor pointwise recovery of arbitrary microscopic spin functions. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The decaying sequence graph selects initial interactions through an analytic contraction argument. It does not specify a finite optimisation objective, operational constraints, or an algorithm for finding a globally optimal temperature. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Independent marked squares describe possible spatial edits in a coupling of spin laws. The marks are independent, but assigned spins may depend on preceding refreshes; this is not a distributed execution or consistency protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper directly contributes to mathematical physics and planar probability through full oriented interface universality. That continuum conclusion is not itself a finite constructive engineering contract, despite the finite auxiliary constructions used in its proof. |

Suppose there are boundary homeomorphisms $$\\phi_n:\\partial D\\longrightarrow\n       \\partial(\\delta_n D_{\\Omega_n})$$ such that $$\\sup_{z\\in\\partial D}\\left|\\phi_n(z)-z\\right|\\longrightarrow0,\\qquad\n \\phi_n(a)=\\delta_n f_{{\\rm in},n},\\qquad\n \\phi_n(b)=\\delta_n f_{{\\rm out},n}.$$ If $P_n$ has law $\\mu_{\\Omega_n,\\beta_c,\\lambda}^{\\xi_n,P_{o,n}}$, then $\\delta_n\\gamma(P_n)$ converges in distribution, in the metric <a href=\"#eq:curve-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:curve-metric\">[eq:curve-metric]</a>, to chordal $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ in $D$ from $a$ to $b$.",

```json
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-critical-trajectory",
          "exact_quote": "For every $J>0$ and finite-range real potential $U$ satisfying the symmetries of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:main\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:main\">1</a>, the preceding scale parameters can be fixed independently of all domains and boundary data. The initial scale $s_0$ may be enlarged by any prescribed finite amount before the following construction. For each sufficiently small $\\epsilon>0$ there is $\\lambda_*>0$ and a real analytic function $\\beta_c:(-\\lambda_*,\\lambda_*)\\to(0,\\infty)$, with $\\beta_c(0)=\\beta_0$, for which the initial potential <a href=\"#eq:flow-physical-input\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-physical-input\">[eq:flow-physical-input]</a> has a trajectory $V_{k+1}=\\Phi_{s_k}(V_k)$ satisfying $$\\label{eq:flow-critical-decay}\n \\|V_k\\|_{s_k,d}\\le\\epsilon2^{-k},\\qquad k\\ge0.$$ Its intermediate one-step trajectories obey $$\\label{eq:flow-critical-intermediate}\n \\sup_{0\\le t\\le1}\n \\sum_\\ell |\\ell|e^{(d/2)(|\\ell|-1)}\n \\|v_{k,\\ell}(t)\\|_\\infty\\le C\\epsilon2^{-k}.$$ The same construction applies to both signs of $\\lambda$.",

        }

          "exact_quote": "Suppose spins are queried one at a time, each next query and the stopping decision being determined by the preceding queries and their answers.           "anchor": "stmt-lem-bounded-replacement",
          "exact_quote": "Fix finitely many pairs of positively separated supports, each a square or a square band, with buffered enlargements that remain disjoint. The replacement has norm at most one. The number of sums, their shapes, and a modulus of continuity for the replacing functions belong to a fixed finite menu, independent of $v$ and of all sufficiently fine meshes. Along any mesh sequence, further subsequences of these replacements converge to bounded continuous tests of finitely many continuum smeared spins. Replacements on both sides of each gap can be made together, and preserve spin-flip and square symmetries by averaging.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-bounded-replacement"
        }

      "why": "Independent marked squares describe possible spatial edits in a coupling of spin laws. The marks are independent, but assigned spins may depend on preceding refreshes; this is not a distributed execution or consistency protocol."
    },
    {
      "anti_uses": [
        "Claiming a finite experiment certifies the full scaling-limit law.",
        "Extending the universality conclusion to asymmetric, infinite-range, or unrestricted-strength perturbations."
      ],
      "applies": false,
      "basis": "inferred",
      "computational_model": null,
      "data_structure": null,
      "domain": "other",
      "implementation_checks": [],
      "maturity": "not_applicable",
      "proposed_domain": null,
      "pseudocode": null,
      "source_evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-main",
          "exact_quote": "Let $J>0$, and let $U$ satisfy the finite-range and symmetry assumptions above. There is $\\lambda_0=\\lambda_0(J,U)>0$ such that for every $\\left|\\lambda\\right|<\\lambda_0$ there is a positive inverse temperature $\\beta_c=\\beta_c(J,U,\\lambda)$, independent of domains and boundary data, with $$\\beta_c(J,U,0)=\\beta_0:=\\frac{\\log(1+\\sqrt2)}{2J},$$ for which the following holds.*\n\n*Let $D$ be any bounded Jordan domain, let $a,b$ be distinct boundary points, and let $\\delta_n\\downarrow0$. For each $n$, choose a domain $\\Omega_n$, marks and stubs $\\xi_n$, and an admissible exterior contour $P_{o,n}$ as above. Suppose there are boundary homeomorphisms $$\\phi_n:\\partial D\\longrightarrow\n       \\partial(\\delta_n D_{\\Omega_n})$$ such that $$\\sup_{z\\in\\partial D}\\left|\\phi_n(z)-z\\right|\\longrightarrow0,\\qquad\n \\phi_n(a)=\\delta_n f_{{\\rm in},n},\\qquad\n \\phi_n(b)=\\delta_n f_{{\\rm out},n}.$$ If $P_n$ has law $\\mu_{\\Omega_n,\\beta_c,\\lambda}^{\\xi_n,P_{o,n}}$, then $\\delta_n\\gamma(P_n)$ converges in distribution, in the metric <a href=\"#eq:curve-metric\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:curve-metric\">[eq:curve-metric]</a>, to chordal $\\mathop{\\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ in $D$ from $a$ to $b$.",
          "source_object": "stmt-thm-main"
        },
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-curve-upgrade",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma:[0,1]\\to\\overline D$ be continuous and injective, with $\\Gamma(0)=a$, $\\Gamma(1)=b$, and $\\Gamma((0,1))\\subset D$. Let $g_n:[0,1]\\to\\mathbb C$ be continuous simple curves whose endpoints tend to $a,b$ and whose traces tend in Hausdorff distance to $\\Gamma([0,1])$. There are continuous functions $u_n:[0,1]\\to[0,1]$ with endpoint values tending to $0,1$ such that $$e_n:=\\sup_t|g_n(t)-\\Gamma(u_n(t))|\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:curves-coordinate-error}$$ Put $B_n=\\sup_{s<t}(u_n(s)-u_n(t))$. If $B_n\\to0$, then $d_{\\rm curv}(g_n,\\Gamma)\\to0$. Conversely, for every fixed $0<\\eta<1$, a backward drop $B_n\\geq\\eta$, with $n$ sufficiently large, forces six disjoint passages from radius $r$ to one fixed radius $R_\\eta>0$, for every $e_n<r<R_\\eta$, about a center on $\\Gamma([\\eta/2,1-\\eta/2])\\Subset D$.*\n\n*For random curves, suppose the trace and endpoint convergence hold in probability in a coupling with such a random $\\Gamma$, and suppose <a href=\"#eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck\">[eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck]</a> holds for every deterministic compact interior $F$ and sufficiently small fixed $R$. Then $d_{\\rm curv}(g_n,\\Gamma)\\to0$ in probability in that coupling.",
          "source_object": "stmt-lem-curve-upgrade"
        }
      ],
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-planar-square-symmetric-fixed-range-model** (hypothesis): The universality result concerns the specified planar square lattice with a fixed finite-range potential invariant under translations, rotations, and reflections. Blocked use: ['Applying the interface conclusion to nonplanar graphs, asymmetric interactions, or infinite-range potentials.', 'Changing the unit-lattice interaction as the mesh is refined.']
- **limit-small-coupling-at-constructed-temperature** (hypothesis): Interface convergence is asserted only within the sufficiently small coupling interval and at the constructed domain-independent inverse temperature. The result does not establish behavior at other temperatures or beyond that interval. Blocked use: ['Claiming universality for unrestricted perturbation strength.', 'Using an arbitrary inverse temperature in place of the constructed branch.', 'Inferring that an unsupported regime necessarily has a particular alternative scaling limit.']
- **limit-jordan-marked-boundary-approximation** (hypothesis): The domain and lattice approximations must satisfy the stated Jordan conditions, distinct marked endpoints, admissible stubs and exterior contours, and uniform boundary-homeomorphism convergence. Blocked use: ['Replacing the stipulated boundary convergence by kernel convergence alone.', 'Applying the theorem directly to domains with holes or non-Jordan boundaries.', 'Using exterior contour data that fail the parity conditions.']
- **limit-retain-actual-exterior-interactions** (formal_scope): The exact contour-to-spin correspondence retains actual exterior spins and every interaction meeting the interior. Adjacent arc signs do not authorize replacement of farther exterior data. Blocked use: ['Deleting interactions across a narrow fjord.', 'Replacing all exterior spins by their adjacent boundary-arc signs.', 'Treating virtual strip filling as a modification of the Gibbs law.']
- **limit-pure-comparison-and-transmission-buffers** (hypothesis): Pure likelihood comparisons and signed transmission have distinct pin and buffer hypotheses. Common pins may be arbitrary in the specified same-graph comparisons, while cross-domain collars and admissible transmission neighborhoods must remain free. Blocked use: ['Demanding a free neighborhood where the same-graph comparison explicitly permits common pins.', 'Transmitting a magnetic response through a cut with pinned required neighborhoods.', 'Changing target-side continuation while applying the ordered-source transmission inequality.']
- **limit-fixed-spatial-smearings-and-separated-replacement** (approximation): Spatial smearings are fixed before the mesh limit and belong to the stated bounded compactly supported Riemann-integrable class. Bounded replacement controls correlations across positive gaps, rather than pointwise reconstruction. Blocked use: ['Using arbitrary mesh-dependent spatial functions under the fixed-smearing convergence claim.', 'Reconstructing every microscopic spin observable from finitely many magnetization coordinates.', 'Removing the positive separation between paired supports.']
- **limit-transfer-symmetry-and-choice-order** (hypothesis): Uniform transfer applies to even square-invariant functions modulo constants, with scale ratios and approximation accuracies chosen before the lattice threshold. Fixed correlation convergence alone does not supply this uniform operator conclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying the scalar transfer bound to arbitrary nonsymmetric interaction functions.', 'Interchanging the prescribed parameter-selection order.', 'Replacing uniform bounded-function control by convergence of a fixed collection of observables.']
- **limit-radial-gap-sector-and-charge-distinctness** (formal_scope): The radial spectral classification is restricted to the even quarter-turn invariant sector. The finite charged-sum identity assumes distinct complex points; the charge-ratio bounds additionally require the unit-disk restriction. Blocked use: ['Extending the stated spectral gap to all spin-field sectors.', 'Evaluating charged weights at coincident insertion points.', 'Applying the unit-disk ratio bounds to unrestricted complex configurations.']
- **limit-reverse-refresh-finite-positive-path** (hypothesis): The reverse refresh construction requires a differentiable strictly positive finite probability family satisfying the specified derivative identity and a valid common function bound. Its vanishing-bound case requires no refresh. Blocked use: ['Using the refresh kernel for a probability path with an unrelated derivative.', 'Dividing by a vanishing function bound.', 'Substituting approximate conditional tables while claiming exact reversal.']
- **limit-analytic-flow-small-ball-and-fixed-forcing** (hypothesis): The analytic labeled flow is controlled on its common small input ball, with the initial single-square forcing held fixed through the step. Its weighted spaces retain covering-list information. Blocked use: ['Updating the forcing during the conditional differential equation.', 'Applying the analytic remainder estimate outside its small input ball.', 'Discarding covering lists and estimating generated interactions solely by support diameter.']
- **limit-compression-preserves-declared-function-and-root** (formal_scope): Compression relabels connected declared coverings while preserving their coefficient functions and roots. The stronger carry estimate is restricted to input lists of the stated minimum length. Blocked use: ['Treating compression as truncation or alteration of the coefficient function.', 'Removing the root convention without checking translation-density preservation.', 'Applying the multibox carry gain to a single-square input.']
- **limit-temperature-branch-local-construction** (nonconstructive): The temperature branch is selected through the analytic decaying-trajectory construction. Local uniqueness refers to that construction; an explicit finite numerical calibration procedure or identification with other scale maps is not supplied. Blocked use: ['Claiming global uniqueness of every possible critical-temperature definition.', 'Equating the branch with a temperature selected by an unrelated scale map.', 'Reporting a certified numerical perturbed critical temperature from the existence theorem alone.']
- **limit-periodic-flow-residual-and-edit-independence** (approximation): Periodic evaluation of the plane flow has a controlled residual rather than an exact torus probability identity. Edit marks are independent of the pure sample and of each other; refreshed values may depend on previous refreshes, and the coupling has a vanishing exceptional event. Blocked use: ['Dropping the periodization residual when asserting an exact finite-volume law.', 'Inferring independence of refreshed spin values from independence of edit locations.', 'Ignoring the exceptional event in a finite torus coupling.']
- **limit-fixed-test-families-and-mesh-first-passages** (hypothesis): Bulk stability transfers fixed finite families with fixed geometry. Passage bounds take the mesh limit before shrinking annulus ratios or enlarging grid families. Blocked use: ['Using an adaptively selected infinite family as a fixed bulk-test vector.', 'Interchanging the mesh and shrinking-radius limits.', 'Claiming a uniform finite-mesh convergence rate from the asymptotic bounds.']
- **limit-geometric-half-plane-and-passage-multiplicity** (formal_scope): The half-plane estimate concerns geometric confinement inside a bulk chart, rather than boundary conditions imposed on the line. Passage counts retain distinct parameter traversals and cannot be replaced by contour-component counts. Blocked use: ['Importing the result as an estimate for a different physical half-plane boundary law.', 'Counting repeated use of one traversal as separate passages.', 'Suppressing reverse excursions because they belong to the same contour component.']
- **limit-possibility-products-original-law-and-disjoint-marks** (hypothesis): The ring product bound uses original pure conditional laws, disjoint check collars, and disjoint independent local mark sets. The bootstrap monitors only checks whose scale exceeds the current edit cutoff as stipulated. Blocked use: ['Assuming a conditional mixing estimate for the edited spin law.', 'Applying the product bound when check neighborhoods share mark indices.', 'Using a ring below the required monitoring scale.']
- **limit-private-bits-small-positive-tables** (hypothesis): The auxiliary representation relies on finite disjoint private channels and local corrections sufficiently small to preserve positivity. Its continuity does not assert analyticity of the representation. Blocked use: ['Reusing private channels across factor patterns without preserving the marginal identity.', 'Extending strict finite-table margins to arbitrary perturbation strength.', 'Assuming the auxiliary tables are analytic merely because the temperature branch is analytic.']
- **limit-strict-incidences-not-unrestricted-spin-fkg** (formal_scope): Ordered coupling is justified only for the listed strict pin/free boundary changes on common free components. Equal-sign pinned incidences and unrestricted positive association of pinned perturbed physical spins are outside that conclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying unrestricted boundary monotonicity to the perturbed physical-spin law.', 'Treating pinned auxiliary factors as automatically attractive.', 'Skipping checks of facing nearest-neighbor incidences after a shield reveal.']
- **limit-stopped-transcripts-and-fixed-free-density-buffer** (hypothesis): Adaptive exploration must retain its complete transcript as conditioning data. Interior density comparison requires a fixed free buffer and retains every actual exterior pin. Blocked use: ['Replacing transcript conditioning by an unexplained spatial Markov assertion for a signed interaction.', 'Deleting revealed spins behind a shield from the conditional law.', 'Allowing the density-comparison buffer to shrink while retaining its fixed constant.']
- **limit-positive-mtp-mass-for-finite-rectangle-checks** (hypothesis): The finite rectangle comparison requires strict positivity and MTP$_2$ of the actual joint mass. Its finite rational instance checks do not establish the universal real-weight theorem or the continuum interface conclusion. Blocked use: ['Applying extreme-corner density bounds to an arbitrary positive binary table.', 'Assigning ratios to zero-mass conditioning events.', 'Treating finite rational table agreement as verification of the full scaling limit.']
- **limit-finite-strip-squeeze-and-drawing-only-filling** (formal_scope): Boundary squeeze concerns a predetermined finite crossing vector. Virtual filling and local redrawing serve geometric barrier evaluation and preserve the actual conditional law and traversal order. Blocked use: ['Claiming total variation agreement of the underlying pure and perturbed spin fields.', 'Selecting an unbounded adaptive strip family within a fixed-vector coupling.', 'Using virtual exterior colors to change interaction terms.']
- **limit-trace-identification-needs-oriented-upgrade** (formal_scope): Hausdorff trace identification alone does not determine traversal order. The full curve conclusion additionally requires convergent oriented endpoints, a simple limiting chord, and the interior bottleneck argument excluding persistent backward drops. Blocked use: ['Concluding oriented uniform curve convergence directly from Hausdorff trace convergence.', 'Applying the progress-coordinate upgrade without its simplicity and endpoint hypotheses.', 'Ignoring distinct annular passages created by reverse travel.']
- **limit-no-extra-fermionic-or-joint-limit-conclusion** (formal_scope): The interface universality theorem does not conclude the predecessor's specific fermionic asymptotics or a joint field-and-interface scaling limit. Blocked use: ['Citing interface convergence as proof of the specific fermionic conjecture.', 'Promoting separate field inputs and interface convergence to an unstated joint convergence theorem.']

Let $f_x$ be fixed bounded real functions, with $\\|f_x\\|_\\infty\\le a$, $a\\ge0$, and let $P_{t,x}$ denote conditional expectation under $\\mu_t$ given the complement of $I_x$. Suppose $$\\label{eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative}\n \\dot\\mu_t(\\sigma)=\\mu_t(\\sigma)c\n \\sum_x\\bigl(P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)-f_x(\\sigma)\\bigr),\n \\qquad c\\ge0.$$ If $a>0$, define the refresh law $$\\label{eq:flow-refresh-law}\n Q_{t,x}(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n =\\mu_t(d\\eta_{I_x}\\mid\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n \\left[1+\\frac{f_x(\\eta_{I_x}\\sigma_{I_x^c})\n                  -P_{t,x}f_x(\\sigma)}{2a}\\right].$$ It is a probability law. Updating set $I_x$ from this law at rate $2ac$, independently for each $x$, has generator $\\mathcal L_t$ with $\\mathcal L_t^*\\mu_t=-\\dot\\mu_t$. Thus using $\\mathcal L_{1-t}$ from time zero to one carries the law at time one exactly to the law at time zero. If $a=0$, the derivative in <a href=\"#eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative\">[eq:flow-exact-finite-derivative]</a> vanishes and no refresh is needed.",
```json
          "source_id": "PM-218.5"
        }
      ],

          "anchor": "stmt-lem-curve-upgrade",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\Gamma:[0,1]\\to\\overline D$ be continuous and injective, with $\\Gamma(0)=a$, $\\Gamma(1)=b$, and $\\Gamma((0,1))\\subset D$. Let $g_n:[0,1]\\to\\mathbb C$ be continuous simple curves whose endpoints tend to $a,b$ and whose traces tend in Hausdorff distance to $\\Gamma([0,1])$. There are continuous functions $u_n:[0,1]\\to[0,1]$ with endpoint values tending to $0,1$ such that $$e_n:=\\sup_t|g_n(t)-\\Gamma(u_n(t))|\\longrightarrow0.\n \\label{eq:curves-coordinate-error}$$ Put $B_n=\\sup_{s<t}(u_n(s)-u_n(t))$. If $B_n\\to0$, then $d_{\\rm curv}(g_n,\\Gamma)\\to0$. Conversely, for every fixed $0<\\eta<1$, a backward drop $B_n\\geq\\eta$, with $n$ sufficiently large, forces six disjoint passages from radius $r$ to one fixed radius $R_\\eta>0$, for every $e_n<r<R_\\eta$, about a center on $\\Gamma([\\eta/2,1-\\eta/2])\\Subset D$.*\n\n*For random curves, suppose the trace and endpoint convergence hold in probability in a coupling with such a random $\\Gamma$, and suppose <a href=\"#eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck\">[eq:curves-uniform-bottleneck]</a> holds for every deterministic compact interior $F$ and sufficiently small fixed $R$. Then $d_{\\rm curv}(g_n,\\Gamma)\\to0$ in probability in that coupling.",
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that every sufficiently small, square-symmetric finite-range perturbation of the planar Ising contour energy has a domain-independent inverse temperature at which its spin interface converges to chordal SLE<sub>3</sub>. The result allows interactions of either sign, arbitrary admissible exterior contours, and uniformly approximated Jordan domains. Convergence holds for the full oriented curve law in uniform distance modulo increasing reparametrization.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
#### Statements index

| anchor | kind | label | number | first sentence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| [stmt-thm-main](#stmt-thm-main) | theorem | `thm:main` | 1 | Let $J>0$, and let $U$ satisfy the finite-range and symmetry assumptions above. |

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Critical nearest-neighbor Ising spin interfaces have a conformally invariant scaling limit: with opposite boundary signs on two arcs, the limiting interface is chordal $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$. We prove that this conclusion persists under every sufficiently small finite-range perturbation of the contour energy that preserves the symmetries of the square lattice. The perturbation may contain arbitrary many-edge interactions and may have either sign. The boundary specification is part of the result: interactions across the boundary remain present, and the exterior may contain additional closed contours.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### The model and its boundary data

Fix $J>0$. Let $\mathcal E^\bullet$ be the nearest-neighbor edges of $\mathbb Z^2$, and let $\mathcal E$ be those of the dual lattice $\mathcal F=\mathbb Z^2+(1/2,1/2)$. Write $e^\bullet\leftrightarrow e$ when the primal and dual edges cross. A potential $U$ assigns a real number to each finite subset of $\mathcal E$, with $U(\varnothing)=0$. We assume that $U$ is invariant under lattice translations and the rotations and reflections of the square lattice. Its range is finite: there is $R<\infty$ such that $$U(S)=0
 \quad\text{if the Euclidean diameter of the union of the edges in \(S\)
 exceeds \(R\).}$$ All values of $U$ are fixed before the mesh is rescaled.

For a finite $\Omega\subset\mathbb Z^2$, put $$Q_\Omega=\bigcup_{v\in\Omega}\bigl(v+[-1/2,1/2]^2\bigr),
 \qquad D_\Omega=\operatorname{int}Q_\Omega.$$ We require $Q_\Omega$ to be the closure of a Jordan domain. Let $E_\Omega\subset\mathcal E$ consist of the edges crossing primal bonds with at least one endpoint in $\Omega$, and let $F_\Omega$ be their endpoints. Choose distinct $f_{\rm in},f_{\rm out}\in\partial D_\Omega\cap F_\Omega$. For each mark choose a dual edge outside $E_\Omega$ joining that mark to a vertex outside $Q_\Omega$; denote these edges by $e_{\rm in},e_{\rm out}$. They are the exterior stubs, and $\xi=((f_{\rm in},e_{\rm in}),(f_{\rm out},e_{\rm out}))$.

For a dual-edge set $A$, let $d_f(A)$ be its degree at $f$. The permitted interior contours are $$\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi
 =
 \left\{P\subset E_\Omega:
 d_f(P)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm in}\}}(f)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm out}\}}(f)
 \text{ is even for every }f\in\mathcal F\right\}.$$ We require this set to be nonempty. An admissible exterior contour is a finite set $P_o\subset\mathcal E\setminus E_\Omega$ containing the two stubs and satisfying $$d_f(P_o)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm in}\}}(f)+\mathbf 1_{\{f_{\rm out}\}}(f)
 \equiv0\pmod2
 \qquad(f\in\mathcal F).$$ Thus $P\cup P_o$ is even for every $P\in\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi$.

<a id="eq:contour-law"></a>
For inverse temperature $\beta>0$ and coupling $\lambda\in\mathbb R$, the law is $$\label{eq:contour-law}
 \mu_{\Omega,\beta,\lambda}^{\xi,P_o}(P)
 =
 \frac{1}{Z_{\Omega,\beta,\lambda}^{\xi,P_o}}
 \exp\!\left\{
 -2\beta J\left|P\right|
 -\beta\lambda
 \sum_{\varnothing\ne X\subseteq P}
 \sum_{Y\subseteq P_o} U(X\cup Y)
 \right\}.$$ The subset sums include equality, and the second includes the empty set. The normalizing constant is the sum over $\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi$. In particular, the boundary data retain every interaction joining an occupied interior edge to occupied exterior edges.

The curve $\gamma(P)$ uses $P$ and the half of each exterior stub between its mark and its midpoint. At degree two, join the two occupied edges. At degree four, join north to east and south to west, also at a mark if its half-stub raises the degree to four. Draw these connections disjointly in disks of radius less than $1/10$ around the dual vertices. There is a unique component between the two stub midpoints; orient it from the incoming midpoint to the outgoing midpoint. This is $\gamma(P)$. Its edges are each traversed at most once, and the resolved curve is simple. Any fixed local drawing with these connections is allowed.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### The main theorem

<a id="eq:curve-metric"></a>
An oriented curve is a continuous map from $[0,1]$ into the plane, considered modulo increasing reparametrization and zero distance for $$\label{eq:curve-metric}
 d_{\rm curv}(\gamma,\eta)
 =
 \inf_{\alpha,\rho}
 \sup_{t\in[0,1]}
 \left|\gamma(\alpha(t))-\eta(\rho(t))\right|,$$ where $\alpha,\rho$ range over increasing homeomorphisms of $[0,1]$. The orientation distinguishes the two endpoints.

For a bounded Jordan domain $D$ and distinct $a,b\in\partial D$, chordal $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ from $a$ to $b$ is the conformal image of the upper-half-plane Loewner trace with $$\partial_t g_t(z)=\frac{2}{g_t(z)-\sqrt3 B_t},
 \qquad g_0(z)=z,$$ where $B$ is standard real Brownian motion; the conformal map sends $0$ to $a$ and $\infty$ to $b$. This law is independent of the choice of such map. The trace is simple and meets the Jordan boundary only at its endpoints; see Schramm (2000; Rohde and Schramm 2005) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Schramm2000](#ref-Schramm2000), [RS2005](#ref-RS2005)].

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $J>0$, and let $U$ satisfy the finite-range and symmetry assumptions above. There is $\lambda_0=\lambda_0(J,U)>0$ such that for every $\left|\lambda\right|<\lambda_0$ there is a positive inverse temperature $\beta_c=\beta_c(J,U,\lambda)$, independent of domains and boundary data, with $$\beta_c(J,U,0)=\beta_0:=\frac{\log(1+\sqrt2)}{2J},$$ for which the following holds.*

*Let $D$ be any bounded Jordan domain, let $a,b$ be distinct boundary points, and let $\delta_n\downarrow0$. For each $n$, choose a domain $\Omega_n$, marks and stubs $\xi_n$, and an admissible exterior contour $P_{o,n}$ as above. Suppose there are boundary homeomorphisms $$\phi_n:\partial D\longrightarrow
       \partial(\delta_n D_{\Omega_n})$$ such that $$\sup_{z\in\partial D}\left|\phi_n(z)-z\right|\longrightarrow0,\qquad
 \phi_n(a)=\delta_n f_{{\rm in},n},\qquad
 \phi_n(b)=\delta_n f_{{\rm out},n}.$$ If $P_n$ has law $\mu_{\Omega_n,\beta_c,\lambda}^{\xi_n,P_{o,n}}$, then $\delta_n\gamma(P_n)$ converges in distribution, in the metric <a href="#eq:curve-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curve-metric">[eq:curve-metric]</a>, to chordal $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ in $D$ from $a$ to $b$.*

</div>

No smoothness of $\partial D$ is assumed. The same unit-lattice potential $U$ is used for every $n$, and the exterior contours may vary arbitrarily with $n$. The theorem concerns the full curve law; it does not assume convergence of an observable or a tightness conjecture for the perturbed model.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Historical context

Schramm’s Loewner description identifies conformally invariant random interfaces through a one-dimensional driving process (Schramm 2000) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[Schramm2000](#ref-Schramm2000)]. Smirnov proved conformal covariance of the critical FK-Ising fermionic observable on the square lattice (Smirnov 2010, Theorem 2.2) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Smirnov2010](#ref-Smirnov2010)]; Chelkak and Smirnov developed the spin and FK observables on isoradial graphs (Chelkak and Smirnov 2012, Theorems A–B) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[ChelkakSmirnov2012](#ref-ChelkakSmirnov2012)]. Together with geometric crossing control, these observables lead to convergence of the critical nearest-neighbor spin interface to $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ (Chelkak et al. 2014, Theorem 1) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[CDHKS2014](#ref-CDHKS2014)]. Conformal covariance of spin correlations and convergence of nested loop collections to $\mathop{\mathrm{CLE}}_3$ are established in Chelkak, Hongler, and Izyurov (2015; Benoist and Hongler 2019) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[CHI2015](#ref-CHI2015), [BH2019](#ref-BH2019)]. The spin alternating-arm exponents, including the four-arm value $21/8$, are given by Wu (2018) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[Wu2018](#ref-Wu2018)].

For non-integrable perturbations, constructive renormalization has established universality for several correlation observables. Giuliani, Greenblatt, and Mastropietro prove full-plane energy-correlation scaling limits for weak, rotation-invariant finite-range pair perturbations (Giuliani, Greenblatt, and Mastropietro 2012) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[GGM2012](#ref-GGM2012)]. Antinucci, Giuliani, and Greenblatt extend energy universality to finite cylinders with periodic boundary conditions in one direction and free boundary conditions in the other, allowing translation-invariant finite-range even multispin interactions (Antinucci, Giuliani, and Greenblatt 2023, Theorem 1.1 and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[AGG2023Cylinder](#ref-AGG2023Cylinder)]. Cava, Giuliani, and Greenblatt prove boundary-spin scaling limits in the half-plane with free boundary conditions, including the renormalized Pfaffian limit under weak translation-invariant finite-range even perturbations (Cava, Giuliani, and Greenblatt 2025, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[CGG2025Boundary](#ref-CGG2025Boundary)]. These results treat either sign of a sufficiently small perturbation and give precise information in the stated plane, cylinder, and half-plane geometries.

A different approach uses random currents. For ferromagnetic, translation-invariant, square-symmetric finite-range pair interactions whose interaction graph is connected, Aizenman et al. (2019, Theorem 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[AizenmanDuminilCopinTassionWarzel2019](#ref-AizenmanDuminilCopinTassionWarzel2019)] prove an asymptotic Pfaffian structure of critical boundary-spin correlations in the half-plane, without a small-perturbation assumption. Its relative Pfaffian relation and the preceding correlation scaling limits concern observables and hypotheses different from the full signed contour-potential interface law considered here.

A close predecessor for the interface problem is the work of Greenblatt and Peltola (2024) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[GreenblattPeltola2024](#ref-GreenblattPeltola2024)] on non-integrable finite-range interactions. Their Theorem 4.4 expresses an exploration local martingale as a ratio of Grassmann correlations, and their Conjecture 4.5 formulates fermionic asymptotics, local renormalization, and boundary stability that would yield an $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ limit. Their Section 5 explains why estimates in fixed geometries must be extended to the irregular boundaries created by exploration. We establish the interface limit for the symmetric class in Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> through bounded spin observables, sparse changes, and restricted boundary comparisons. The specific fermionic asymptotics in their conjecture are not a conclusion of this argument.

There is a complementary geometric lineage. Multi-crossing estimates control random-curve regularity in Aizenman and Burchard (1999, Hypothesis H1 and Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[AizenmanBurchard1999](#ref-AizenmanBurchard1999)], and conditional bounds on unforced crossings yield tightness and Loewner limits in Kemppainen and Smirnov (2017, Condition G2 and Theorem 1.5) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[KemppainenSmirnov2017](#ref-KemppainenSmirnov2017)]. The distinction between driving-function convergence and strong curve convergence is developed in Sheffield and Sun (2012, Theorem 1.2 and Example 2.1) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[SheffieldSun2012](#ref-SheffieldSun2012)]. Our geometric input is a mesh-first interior four-passage estimate. We prove the trace-to-curve implication needed here directly by excluding collapsing reverse traversals.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Proof strategy

We work with spins by assigning plus at infinity and changing sign each time a primal path crosses an edge of the even contour set $P\cup P_o$. This assigns a spin $\sigma_v\in\{-1,+1\}$ to each lattice vertex; the spins outside $\Omega$ are fixed by $P_o$. A prescribed spin is called a *pin*. Expanding each contour interaction in these spins gives a finite-range even spin interaction. Section <a href="#subsec:spin-formulation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="subsec:spin-formulation">1.5</a> proves the exact identity of the two conditional laws, including all boundary interactions. Our first comparison is between the periodic version of this bulk interaction and the critical nearest-neighbor law on a large periodic square.

Section <a href="#sec:pure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:pure">2</a> specifies the pure-model estimates used in that comparison. Besides the public results above, we use the buffered likelihood comparisons, ordered-source transmission, nearby-line prediction, and complete-resolution results of the companion article *Buffered comparison and stopping-band resolution in critical Ising* (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[OpenAIStatic2026](#ref-OpenAIStatic2026)]. These are equilibrium statements about the ordinary critical model. Their hypotheses distinguish arbitrary common pins in likelihood comparisons from the free neighborhoods needed to transmit a magnetic response.

The linear transfer takes a bounded spin function supported in a square, conditions it under the pure law on the boundary spins of a concentric square larger by a fixed ratio $L$, and multiplies by $L^2$ to normalize an interaction density. The first obstacle is that repeated transfer creates functions of more and more microscopic spins. Formulas for a fixed number of field insertions do not control all such functions. Camia, Garban, and Newman construct smeared magnetization-field limits from spin correlations (Camia, Garban, and Newman 2015, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[CGN2015](#ref-CGN2015)]; Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">3</a> proves the further uniform approximation needed here. We observe each spin through a weak Gaussian signal. Conditional on these observations the pure law remains ferromagnetic. Ordered-source transmission and prediction from nearby spin sums show that the remaining uncertainty has little effect across a positive gap. Only finitely many spatial averages of the observation are then needed. This reduces the transfer of arbitrary bounded spin functions to the explicit continuum spin formulas. Reflection positivity and a finite determinant identity show that, among even square-invariant interaction densities, exactly one scalar direction can grow under this transfer.

Section <a href="#sec:flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:flow">4</a> turns the linear transfer into an exact flow of local interactions. A finite-state identity explains the construction: moving a potential toward its conditional expectation can be reversed by random local refreshes at rates independent of the spins. We control the interactions produced at successive scales. Positive long-distance energy covariance shows that changing temperature moves transversely to the set of initial interactions whose trajectories decay. We can therefore choose the inverse temperature to obtain such a trajectory. This choice uses only bulk data and is therefore independent of the domain. Reversing the flow couples the resulting torus spin law to the pure law by changes inside independently marked squares. The marking probabilities decay with scale; the spins assigned inside marked squares remain unrestricted.

Section <a href="#sec:stability" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:stability">5</a> proves that these possible changes preserve every fixed finite collection of polygonal crossing, circuit, and four-passage tests with probability tending to one. The geometric reason has three forms. An edit in the interior of a test can change its answer only if four long contour portions reach the edit. Near a straight test edge two portions must remain on one side, and near a corner one portion must extend to a fixed distance. The corresponding probabilities decay faster than the numbers of possible locations grow. The strict bulk threshold comes from the spin four-arm exponent $21/8>2$; the half-plane estimate is derived here. A bootstrap over the edit scales makes the conclusion valid for every assignment in all marked squares, including assignments chosen after examining the pure configuration.

To pass from periodic squares to the prescribed domain, we need comparison under boundary conditions. Signed perturbations need not preserve ferromagnetic comparison for the physical spins. Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">6</a> represents each small interaction by additional binary variables on nearest-neighbor bonds. Their local conditional probabilities have strict margins under specified changes from minus pins to free spins or from free spins to plus pins. These margins provide the boundary comparisons used here. Combined with the bulk crossing tests, they also bound the pointwise density ratio between a target-domain spin marginal on a square and the perturbed torus marginal on that square, when a fixed free buffer separates the square from all pins.

Finally, Section <a href="#sec:curves" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:curves">7</a> constructs two displaced pure comparison domains, favoring the opposite colors. Finite collections of colored strip crossings transfer barriers near their interfaces to the target. In any subsequential limit, the barriers and connectedness of the target trace order the two limiting simple chords. Both chords have the same $\mathop{\mathrm{SLE}}_3$ law, so they coincide and identify the trace. A persistent backward traversal near a simple chord would create six passages through a small interior annulus. The four-passage estimate excludes this event and yields convergence in the full oriented curve metric <a href="#eq:curve-metric" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:curve-metric">[eq:curve-metric]</a>.

The inverse temperature is selected by the decaying trajectories of the conditional flow in Section <a href="#sec:flow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:flow">4</a>. Its local uniqueness refers to that construction. No identification with a temperature branch selected by a different scale map, or joint field-and-interface scaling limit, is required for Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="subsec:spin-formulation"></a>
#### Exact spin formulation

<a id="eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian"></a>
The proof uses spin conditional expectations in the bulk. We record the exact passage from <a href="#eq:contour-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:contour-law">[eq:contour-law]</a> to spins, including the exterior data. The contour-potential formulation with exterior interactions is also used by Greenblatt and Peltola (2024, sec. 4) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[GreenblattPeltola2024](#ref-GreenblattPeltola2024)]. For a primal bond crossing $e$, write $$d_e(\sigma)=\frac{1-\sigma_v\sigma_w}{2},
 \qquad e^\bullet=\{v,w\},\quad \sigma\in\{-1,1\}^{\mathbb Z^2}.$$ The formal spin Hamiltonian is $$\label{eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian}
 \mathcal H_{\beta,\lambda}(\sigma)
 =
 -\beta J\sum_{\{v,w\}\in\mathcal E^\bullet}\sigma_v\sigma_w
 +\beta\lambda
  \sum_{\varnothing\ne S\subset\mathcal E}
        U(S)\prod_{e\in S}d_e(\sigma).$$ Only terms depending on the spins in the chosen finite domain enter its conditional law. All resulting sums are finite.

<a id="stmt-lem-contour-spin"></a>
<div id="lem:contour-spin" class="lemma">

**Lemma 2**. *For the boundary data above, every even completion $P\cup P_o$ determines a unique spin configuration that is plus sufficiently far away and has disagreement set $P\cup P_o$. Its restriction $\tau$ to $\mathbb Z^2\setminus\Omega$ is independent of $P$. The map $P\mapsto\sigma|_\Omega$ is a bijection between $\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi$ and all spin assignments in $\Omega$. Under this bijection, <a href="#eq:contour-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:contour-law">[eq:contour-law]</a> is precisely the Gibbs conditional law for <a href="#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> with exterior spins $\tau$. Denote this law by $\nu_{\Omega,\beta,\lambda}^{\tau}$.*

*The exterior nearest neighbors along each of the two boundary arcs between the marks have a constant sign, and the signs of the two arcs are opposite. The interaction in <a href="#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> is finite-range, invariant under global spin flip and the square symmetries, and has finitely many local interaction types up to translation.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-contour-spin"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-contour-spin</summary>

*Proof.* A finite even dual subgraph has even intersection parity with every closed primal walk. One way to see this is to reduce a closed walk modulo two to elementary primal faces; the intersection parity around such a face is the degree of the dual vertex it encloses. Starting with a plus spin at infinity, flip signs on crossing an occupied dual edge. Path independence follows from this parity statement. Finiteness fixes the plus sign in the unbounded region and gives uniqueness.

The exterior cell centers $\mathbb Z^2\setminus\Omega$ form a connected nearest-neighbor graph. Indeed the complement of a Jordan polyomino has no bounded component, and a path in its exterior can be perturbed to cross cell sides away from vertices, giving a path of exterior cells. The Jordan condition excludes a diagonal pinch. A primal path using only exterior vertices crosses only dual edges outside $E_\Omega$. Their occupied set is $P_o$, so all exterior signs are fixed independently of $P$.

Conversely, extend any assignment in $\Omega$ by these fixed exterior spins. Its disagreement set outside $E_\Omega$ is $P_o$, and its complete dual disagreement graph is even. The interior part therefore belongs to $\mathcal P_\Omega^\xi$. These two constructions are inverse.

For the signed-arc assertion, traverse the polygonal boundary. At a flat boundary vertex, the two successive exterior neighboring cells are separated by one exterior primal bond. At an unmarked vertex the crossing exterior dual edge is absent by the parity condition, so the two exterior signs agree. At a convex corner, the corresponding exterior path has two bonds; its crossing parity is again even at an unmarked vertex. At a reflex corner, the two boundary edges have the same adjacent exterior cell. At either mark the exterior parity is odd, and the sign changes. Reflex corners cannot support an exterior stub of the required kind. Thus there are exactly the two asserted constant, opposite arc signs.

Now fix an interior spin configuration and its contour $P$. A nonconstant interaction term in <a href="#eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian">[eq:intro-spin-hamiltonian]</a> has a support $S$ meeting $E_\Omega$. Write $$X=S\cap E_\Omega,\qquad Y=S\setminus E_\Omega.$$ The product of disagreement indicators is one exactly when $X\subseteq P$ and $Y\subseteq P_o$. Summing over all such supports gives precisely the double sum in <a href="#eq:contour-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:contour-law">[eq:contour-law]</a>. Terms supported entirely outside $E_\Omega$ are constant in the interior spins. The nearest-neighbor energy is $2\beta J\left|P\right|$ up to an exterior-dependent constant. This proves equality of the conditional laws.

Finally, choose an edge in a nonempty support of $U$. Finite range leaves only finitely many possible other edges relative to it, hence only finitely many support types up to translation. Expanding the products of $d_e$ gives finite local spin polynomials, invariant under spin flip and under the prescribed square symmetries. ◻

</details>

In particular, exterior contours do not give an additional bulk parameter. Their only effect is through the actual exterior spins in the conditional law. This observation does not permit deleting interactions across a narrow fjord or replacing distant exterior spins by their adjacent arc signs.

<a id="sec:pure"></a>
### Pure critical Ising inputs and contour conventions

The comparison and regularity estimates in this section concern only the nearest-neighbor Ising model at coupling $K_0=\beta_0J=\frac12\log(1+\sqrt2)$. Their role is to supply uniform control of bounded spin observables across a free region, and geometric estimates for the contours of the pure model. Section <a href="#sec:transfer" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:transfer">3</a> will derive the uniform transfer theorem from these inputs.

We write $\mu_0$ for the critical full-plane law and $\mathbb E_0$ for its expectation. A *pin* is a prescribed spin. Common pins have the same locations and values in the laws being compared; they may have either sign. A free lattice chart is a region isometric to part of the square lattice in which no spins have been pinned. A collar always includes the edges of that chart, as well as its vertices. Write $B_s(x)=x+[-s,s]^2\cap\mathbb Z^2$, and $B_s=B_s(0)$. For integer $s$, the outer layer is $\partial B_s=B_s\setminus B_{s-1}$; integrating the open square means integrating $B_{s-1}$ while keeping this layer fixed. Integer rounding and a bounded displacement of these layers can be absorbed by a fixed fraction of any stated positive collar once the lattice scale tends to infinity.

We cite three equilibrium estimates and one complete-resolution result from the companion article *Buffered comparison and stopping-band resolution in critical Ising* (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[OpenAIStatic2026](#ref-OpenAIStatic2026)]. The citations below specify its named results and section headings. We use these results as pure-model inputs; the transfer, scale flow, and perturbed-interface arguments are proved in this article.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Pointwise comparisons through collars

Pointwise comparison is stronger than comparison of a fixed collection of observables. It will permit arbitrary boundary assignments to enter the transfer argument through uniformly bounded likelihoods.

<a id="stmt-prop-pure-buffered"></a>
<div id="prop:pure-buffered" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Buffered and small-patch comparisons). *Consider a finite critical nearest-neighbor ferromagnetic Ising graph, disjoint vertex sets $I,J,F$, and common pins on $F$. Let $\mu_b$ be the marginal law on $I$ obtained by prescribing an arbitrary configuration $b$ on $J$. Whenever the following sets lie in the indicated square-lattice charts, the bounds hold for all configurations $a$ on $I$ and all $b,b'$.*

1.  *For every $c>0$ there is $K_c<\infty$ such that $I\subset B_R(z)$, $J\subset B_{(1+c)R}(z)^c$ imply $$K_c^{-1}\le \frac{\mu_b(a)}{\mu_{b'}(a)}\le K_c.$$*

2.  *There are absolute $K<\infty$, $\zeta>0$ such that $I\subset B_w(z)$, $J\subset B_R(z)^c$, and $R\ge8w\ge8$ imply $$\left|\log\frac{\mu_b(a)}{\mu_{b'}(a)}\right|
     \le K(w/R)^\zeta.$$ No free neighborhood of $I$ is required in these two assertions: additional common pins may lie anywhere outside $I\cup J$.*

3.  *Either law can be replaced by any mixture of the indicated conditional laws. The fixed-ratio comparison also holds for laws from different domains, including the plane and a torus, if there is a common isometric lattice collar separating the observed spins from every difference in the data. In this cross-domain assertion the collar is unpinned in both laws. Applying a common randomization kernel to the observed spins preserves each density bound.*

*All constants are independent of the number, positions, and values of the common pins and of the mesh. In the first two assertions the same ratio bounds hold when the roles of the two terminal sets are reversed.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-pure-buffered"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-pure-buffered</summary>

*Source and the terminal symmetry.* The first three assertions are Corollary 2.5, “Buffered and small-patch comparisons”, in Section 2, “Pointwise comparison through buffers”, of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[OpenAIStatic2026](#ref-OpenAIStatic2026)]. We record the symmetry needed below. For the positive joint marginal $p(a,b)$, put $f=\log p$. The source’s Lemmas 2.1 and 2.2, “Extreme rectangles in a ferromagnetic marginal” and “Pointwise ratios with arbitrary common pins”, bound $$\left|f(a,b)+f(a',b')-f(a,b')-f(a',b)\right|
 \le 4\operatorname{arctanh}q_0,$$ where $q_0$ is the zero-field FK connection probability after deleting the common pinned vertices and separately contracting the two terminal sets. Deletion retains the fields induced by the pinned spins in the original law; all these fields are then set to zero in the comparator. The right side is symmetric in the terminals. A dual circuit in the separating collar bounds $q_0$ away from one; several separated dual circuits give its power decay in the small-patch case. Normalizing either family of conditional laws turns the oscillation bound into the displayed pointwise ratio bounds. Thus reversing which terminal is observed requires no new geometric estimate; this is also Remark 2.3 of the companion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-cor-pure-annular"></a>
<div id="cor:pure-annular" class="corollary">

**Corollary 4** (Two collars around an annulus). *Let $2\le r<R/4$, $A=B_R\setminus B_r$, and $W=B_{2R}\setminus B_{r/2}$, with integer rounding of the boundaries. Suppose $W$ is an unpinned square-lattice chart. The law on $A$, conditional on arbitrary spins outside $W$, has pointwise density relative to $\mu_0|_A$ between $K^{-1}$ and $K$, for an absolute $K$ independent of $R/r$. The assertion applies in a torus chart and to mixtures of exterior conditions. Fixed changes of the two collar ratios only change $K$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-pure-annular"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-pure-annular</summary>

*Proof.* First change the spins outside $B_{2R}$, keeping all inner pins common. Proposition <a href="#prop:pure-buffered" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:pure-buffered">3</a> across the outer collar costs a fixed factor. Next change the spins in $B_{r/2}$, keeping the outer data common. Use the terminal-symmetric form of the same proposition across the collar from $r/2$ to $r$: the small terminal is now the source and $A$ lies beyond its fixed-ratio collar. This costs another fixed factor. The product is independent of the distance between the collars. Finally mix the exterior data according to the full-plane law. Cross-domain comparison is legitimate because both collars are common lattice charts. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### Signed sources and line magnetizations

The transfer proof will produce a signed density that has a positivity property under increasing tests, but is not necessarily increasing. The following definition states precisely the property required of it.

<a id="stmt-def-pure-order-positive"></a>
<div id="def:pure-order-positive" class="definition">

**Definition 5** (Order-positive source). For a probability measure $\mu_S$ on spins in a finite set $S$, a real function $q$ is an order-positive centered density if $$\mathbb E_{\mu_S}q=0,\qquad \mathbb E_{\mu_S}[qg]\ge0
 \quad\hbox{for every increasing }g.$$ Here density means the density of a signed measure.

</div>

We must also specify the small free neighborhoods used by the transmission estimate. Let $S,T$ be disjoint source and target sets. A cut $J$, disjoint from $S,T$ and from all common pins, is *separating* if every free-graph path from $S$ to $T$ meets $J$. Its target-side Gibbs continuation is then independent of the source after the cut spins have been prescribed.

Fix $A,K\ge1$ and $a\in(0,1/(16A))$. The cut is *admissible at scales $w,s$*, where $0<w\le s$, if it has a partition into patches $b_i$, straight counting segments $L_i\subset J$, centers $z_i$, and scales $\ell_i\in[aw,Aw]$ such that

1.  $b_i\cup L_i\subset B_{\ell_i}(z_i)$ and $a\ell_i\le |L_i|\le2\ell_i$;

2.  $B_{16\ell_i}(z_i)$ is a pin-free planar lattice chart, including freedom from source pins;

3.  each vertex belongs to at most $K$ counting segments;

4.  $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(T,z_i)\ge s/A$, $16\ell_i\le s/A$, and the small-patch comparison applies from $b_i$ toward $T$ in a chart extending to a fixed positive multiple of $s$.

The constants $a,A,K$ and the fixed chart geometry are part of this definition; the number of patches is not. A square contour in a free collar of width comparable to $w$, at distance comparable to $s$ from the target, is admissible. Partition its sides into intervals of length a sufficiently small fixed multiple of $w$. At corners use counting intervals on an adjacent side. Their overlap remains bounded. The same construction works for a fixed finite union of square contours, including the two boundaries of a square band.

<a id="stmt-prop-signed-transmission"></a>
<div id="prop:signed-transmission" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:pure-signed"></a>
**Proposition 6** (Transmission of a signed source). *There is a fixed exponent $\eta\in(0,1/8)$ with the following property. Let $\mu$ be a pure critical Gibbs law with common pins, let $q=q(\sigma_S)$ be order-positive and centered for its source marginal, and let $J$ be an admissible cut at scales $w,s$. For every bounded target observable $h=h(\sigma_T)$, when $w$ is sufficiently large, $$\left|\mathbb E_\mu[qh]\right|
 \le C\left\|h\right\|_\infty (w/s)^{-7/8+\eta}
               \mathbb E_\mu[qM_J],
 \qquad M_J=s^{-7/8}\sum_{x\in J}\sigma_x.
 \label{eq:pure-signed}$$ The constant depends only on the admissibility constants and the fixed chart geometry. It is independent of the mass of $q_+$, the mesh, the number and values of source pins, and the ratio $w/s$. The same inequality holds for expectation differences of two ordered source configurations, and after averaging an ordered coupling of random source configurations, with the common Gibbs continuation. All changed pins and fields lie on the source side of the cut; the target-side continuation is identical in the compared laws. One may decrease $\eta$, keeping it positive.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-signed-transmission"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-signed-transmission</summary>

*Source and signed-measure interpretation.* These are Proposition 4.2, “Transmission”, and Corollary 4.11, “Random and signed sources”, in Section 4, “Ordered transmission across a cut”, of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[OpenAIStatic2026](#ref-OpenAIStatic2026)]. To clarify the signed assertion, put $m=\mathbb Eq_+=\mathbb Eq_-$. If $m=0$, both sides vanish. Otherwise the source probability measures $m^{-1}q_+\mu_S$ and $m^{-1}q_-\mu_S$ are stochastically ordered by Definition <a href="#def:pure-order-positive" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="def:pure-order-positive">5</a>. Couple them in that order, apply the deterministic-source transmission inequality, and multiply by $m$. Its nonnegative magnetic difference permits averaging and gives <a href="#eq:pure-signed" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pure-signed">[eq:pure-signed]</a>. No division by a mesh-dependent lower bound on $m$ occurs. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-prop-line-continuity"></a>
<div id="prop:line-continuity" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:pure-lines"></a>
**Proposition 7** (Nearby line sums). *Let $J_s,J'_s$ be horizontal or vertical lattice segments of length $O(s)$, with corresponding portions displaced by at most $Cw$ and unmatched end portions of total length $O(w)$. The assertion also applies to concentric square contours of radii comparable to $s$, differing in radius by at most $Cw$, and to a fixed finite union of these pairs. Assume their union has a free neighborhood of width $cs$, covered by a fixed number of buffered lattice charts. For each fixed $h=w/s\in(0,h_0]$, $$\limsup_{s\to\infty}
 \left\|s^{-7/8}\sum_{J_s}\sigma_x
       -s^{-7/8}\sum_{J'_s}\sigma_x\right\|_{L^2(\mu)}
 \le C_0h^{3/8}.
 \label{eq:pure-lines}$$ The bound is uniform over arbitrary pins beyond the stated free neighborhood and mixtures of their laws. The constant depends only on the fixed geometry and buffer.*

</div>

This is Proposition 5.1, “Differences of nearby line sums”, in Section 5, “Nearby contours and prediction”, of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[OpenAIStatic2026](#ref-OpenAIStatic2026)]. Its exponent follows from integrating the full-plane covariance kernel $c|x-y|^{-1/4}$ along segments: separations below $h$ contribute $O(h^{3/4})$ to the squared norm, while above $h$ the first-derivative bound gives $Ch\int_h^1t^{-5/4}\,dt=O(h^{3/4})$. Short unmatched end pieces contribute $O(h^{7/4})$ in squared norm. Buffered pointwise comparison transfers the nonnegative squared error to the allowed laws. This explanation uses the continuum kernel after taking the mesh limit and asserts no uniform discrete derivative bound.

<a id="stmt-cor-line-prediction"></a>
<div id="cor:line-prediction" class="corollary">

**Corollary 8** (Prediction from a nearby contour). *In Proposition <a href="#prop:line-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:line-continuity">7</a>, let $\mathcal F$ be any spin information containing $\sigma_{J'_s}$, and let $P_{\mathcal F}$ denote conditional expectation. Then, for each fixed $w/s$, $$\limsup_{s\to\infty}
 \left\|M_{J_s}-P_{\mathcal F}M_{J_s}\right\|_2
 \le C_0(w/s)^{3/8}.$$ Both line sums use the same normalization $s^{-7/8}$.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-line-prediction"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-line-prediction</summary>

*Proof.* The second line sum is an $\mathcal F$-measurable competitor for the orthogonal projection. Apply <a href="#eq:pure-lines" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pure-lines">[eq:pure-lines]</a>. This is also the Corollary 5.2, “Prediction from a nearby contour”, in Section 5 of (OpenAI 2026) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[OpenAIStatic2026](#ref-OpenAIStatic2026)]. ◻

</details>

In particular, when $w\asymp s/n$ for $n$ concentric cuts, Proposition <a href="#prop:signed-transmission" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:signed-transmission">6</a> has a constant independent of $n$. In Proposition <a href="#prop:line-continuity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:line-continuity">7</a> one first fixes $n$ and then takes $s$ large; the threshold may depend on $n$. The fixed exponent $\eta>0$, geometric constants, and normalization are chosen before either operation.

<a id="pm-h-11"></a>
#### Spin correlations and the thermal direction

<a id="stmt-prop-pure-spin"></a>
<div id="prop:pure-spin" class="proposition">

<a id="eq:pure-spin-formula"></a>
<a id="eq:pure-two-point"></a>
<a id="eq:pure-plus-one-point"></a>
<a id="eq:pure-multipoint-bound"></a>
**Proposition 9** (Spin normalization and moment bounds). *There is $a_\sigma>0$, determined by the square-lattice convention, such that, for distinct physical points $z_1,\ldots,z_n$, $$a_\sigma^{-n}\delta^{-n/8}
 \mathbb E_0\prod_{i=1}^n\sigma_{x_i^\delta}
 \longrightarrow S(z_1,\ldots,z_n),
 \qquad \delta x_i^\delta\longrightarrow z_i.$$ For odd $n$, $S=0$; for even $n$, $S$ is the nonnegative square root of $$S(z_1,\ldots,z_n)^2
 =2^{-n/2}\!\sum_{\substack{u_i\in\{-1,1\}\\\sum_i u_i=0}}
       \prod_{i<j}|z_i-z_j|^{u_iu_j/2}.
 \label{eq:pure-spin-formula}$$ Thus $S(z,w)=|z-w|^{-1/4}$. The convergence is uniform when the points lie in compact sets with fixed mutual separation. Also $$\begin{aligned}
 0\le\mathbb E_0[\sigma_x\sigma_y]&\le C(1+|x-y|)^{-1/4},
 \label{eq:pure-two-point}\\
 0\le\mathbb E^+_{B_r}[\sigma_0]&\le C(1+r)^{-1/8}.
 \label{eq:pure-plus-one-point}
\end{aligned}$$ For $n\ge2$, repeated sites allowed, $$\left|\mathbb E_0\prod_{i=1}^n\sigma_{x_i}\right|
 \le C^n\prod_{i=1}^n
 \left(1+\min_{j\ne i}|x_i-x_j|\right)^{-1/8}.
 \label{eq:pure-multipoint-bound}$$ For every fixed finite collection of bounded compactly supported Riemann-integrable spatial test functions $\varphi$, the smeared variables $$\Phi_\delta(\varphi)
 =a_\sigma^{-1}\delta^{15/8}
                  \sum_{x\in\mathbb Z^2}\varphi(\delta x)\sigma_x$$ have joint moment limits obtained by integrating $S$ against the test functions. Here Riemann integrability is equivalent to having a set of discontinuities of two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero. Spatial smearings are chosen in this class and fixed before taking the mesh limit; polygonal cell indicators are included. The limiting correlation functions are covariant under Möbius transformations, including inversion, with weight $1/8$ at each spin insertion.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-pure-spin"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-pure-spin</summary>

*Sources and the domination needed for smearing.* The construction of smeared magnetization fields from spin correlations is developed in (Camia, Garban, and Newman 2015, sec. 3) <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[CGN2015](#ref-CGN2015)]. We give the domination argument for the spatial test functions used here. The spin limit and its full-plane normalization follow from (Chelkak, Hongler, and Izyurov 2015, Theorems 1.1–1.2, Remark 1.4, and Equation (1.6)) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[CHI2015](#ref-CHI2015)]. That paper uses a rotated lattice with nearest-neighbor spacing $\sqrt2$ times its mesh parameter; its positive normalization constant is absorbed into $a_\sigma$. The plus-boundary one-point convergence in a fixed square and the full-plane two-point asymptotic give <a href="#eq:pure-plus-one-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pure-plus-one-point">[eq:pure-plus-one-point]</a> and <a href="#eq:pure-two-point" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pure-two-point">[eq:pure-two-point]</a> for large distances. Enlarging $C$ covers the finitely many smaller distances.

For <a href="#eq:pure-multipoint-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:pure-multipoint-bound">[eq:pure-multipoint-bound]</a>, put disjoint squares of radius a small fixed fraction of the nearest other insertion distance around the distinct insertion sites.
